### [1] [The $\mathbb{Z}_2$-Index of a Pair of Pure States and the Topology of Interacting 1D Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28796)
*Anna Mazhar,Jacob Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a relative Z2-index N(ω₁,ω₂) for parity-invariant pure states on a C*-algebra, establishes its topological and algebraic properties, applies it to define a many-body Majorana number Nσ(ω) for the self-dual CAR algebra, shows agreement with the single-body Majorana number in the quasi-free Hilbert–Schmidt regime, and proves that Nσ fully classifies symmetric automorphic-path-components—though potentially ignoring a zero-dimensional parity obstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the notion of the Majorana number beyond free-fermion systems to interacting many-body settings while preserving topological robustness and classification power under locality and symmetry constraints.

Method: Constructs a relative Z2-index using K-theoretic and C*-algebraic techniques for locally-comparable, parity-invariant pure states; applies it to the half-chain automorphism σ on the 1D self-dual CAR algebra; analyzes quasi-free states via Hilbert–Schmidt perturbations; defines and studies symmetric local automorphism paths to establish classification up to automorphic equivalence.

Result: The relative Z2-index N(ω₁,ω₂) is well-defined, multiplicative, invariant under parity-preserving automorphisms, and locally constant in norm topology; the many-body Majorana number Nσ(ω) coincides with the single-body version in the quasi-free Hilbert–Schmidt regime; Nσ completely classifies symmetric automorphic-path-components of parity-invariant σ-local pure states.

Conclusion: The many-body Majorana number provides a robust, intrinsically interacting generalization of the topological invariant for 1D superconducting phases, capturing bulk topology under automorphic equivalence—though not necessarily all zero-dimensional parity obstructions.

Abstract: We define a relative $\mathbb{Z}_2$-index $\mathcal{N}(ω_1,ω_2)$ for locally-comparable, parity-invariant pure states on a unital $C^*$-algebra. We prove that the index is well-defined, multiplicative, invariant under parity-preserving automorphisms, and locally constant in the norm topology. For the one-dimensional self-dual CAR algebra, we apply this construction to the half-chain automorphism $σ$ and define the many-body Majorana number $\mathcal{N}σ(ω):=\mathcal{N}(ω,ω\circσ)$ for parity-invariant $σ$-local pure states. In the quasi-free Hilbert--Schmidt regime, this index agrees with the usual single-body Majorana number. We then introduce symmetric local automorphism paths and prove that $\mathcal{N}_σ$ completely classifies the resulting automorphic-path-components. This automorphic-path equivalence retains the bulk Majorana number but may forget a relative zero-dimensional parity obstruction.

</details>


### [2] [Pseudorandomness and Diffraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28917)
*David Damanik,Nicolae Strungaru*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the pseudorandomness of a quasiperiodic potential V(n) from a diffraction perspective, proving its diffraction measure is purely absolutely continuous—matching random potentials—and thereby supporting the conjecture that it also exhibits Anderson localization in the Schrödinger operator setting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide evidence for the conjecture that the quasiperiodic potential V(n) exhibits Anderson localization (a hallmark of randomness in spectral theory), by analyzing its deterministic yet 'random-like' diffraction properties instead of directly tackling the intractable spectral problem.

Method: Rigorous analysis of the diffraction measure associated with the potential V(n), leveraging tools from harmonic analysis and almost periodic functions to establish absolute continuity for all nonzero coupling λ, irrational α, and arbitrary phase parameters x₁, x₂.

Result: The diffraction measure of V(n) is purely absolutely continuous for all λ ≠ 0, all irrational α, and all x₁, x₂—identical to the diffraction behavior of truly random potentials.

Conclusion: V(n) is pseudorandom in the diffraction sense, reinforcing the plausibility of the Anderson localization conjecture for this model and highlighting a duality between Schrödinger spectral measures (expected pure point) and diffraction measures (proven absolutely continuous).

Abstract: Pseudorandom structures are structures that behave like random ones, without necessarily being random themselves. It is a prominent, and evidently very difficult, conjecture that $$ V(n) = λ\cos \Big( 2 π\Big( x_1 + n x_2 + \frac{n(n-1)}{2} α\Big) \Big) $$ is pseudorandom from a Schrödinger operator perspective in that the Schrödinger operator in $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z})$ with potential $V$ displays Anderson localization, that is, pure point spectrum with exponentially decaying eigenfunctions for almost all parameter values --- the same spectral features as those produced by random potentials. We show that $V$ is pseudorandom in terms of its diffraction properties, that is, the associated diffraction measure is purely absolutely continuous for all $λ\not= 0$, all irrational $α$, and all $x_1,x_2$ --- which is the case as well for the random case. Our result gives further evidence for the conjecture in the Schrödinger case and it elucidates the apparent dual behavior of Schrödinger spectral measures and diffraction measures.

</details>


### [3] [Simulating passive scalar advection in rough Kraichnan flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29321)
*Long Li,André L. P. Considera,Simon Thalabard*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper conducts high-resolution Eulerian simulations of passive scalar advection in rough 2D Kraichnan flows across the full velocity roughness exponent range h ∈ (0,1), validating theoretical scaling laws, quantifying intermittency via fourth-order flatness and higher-order increment statistics, and providing practical guidelines for parameter selection—especially diffusivity scaling with h—and diagnosing finite-resolution effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically test theoretical predictions (e.g., duality, scaling laws, intermittency anomalies) for passive scalar advection in rough Kraichnan flows, bridge Lagrangian theory with Eulerian numerics, and establish robust simulation protocols for future extensions (e.g., Gaussian multiplicative chaos-coupled flows) where analytical progress is limited.

Method: Direct pseudo-spectral integration of the advection–diffusion equation on up to 2048² grids; frozen-noise Runge–Kutta time stepping consistent with white-in-time velocity; careful identification of inertial-diffusive scaling ranges; measurement of second- and fourth-order structure functions, flatness, increment PDFs, and higher-order statistics; parametric variation of molecular diffusivity with h.

Result: Recovery of flow–scalar duality and predicted second-order scaling; quantitative agreement of fourth-order flatness anomaly with prior Lagrangian and perturbative theories across all h; systematic non-Gaussian, non-log-normal increment PDFs peaking at intermediate h; explicit h-dependent diffusivity scaling rules; finite spectral resolution fully accounts for residual deviations from ideal scaling.

Conclusion: Eulerian simulations are reliable and informative for rough Kraichnan advection when parameters—especially diffusivity—are tuned appropriately with h; the study provides both validation of fundamental intermittency theory and a practical numerical baseline for more complex, analytically intractable turbulent coupling models.

Abstract: We present a systematic Eulerian study of passive scalar advection in rough two-dimensional Kraichnan flows, covering the full range of velocity roughness exponent $h\in(0,1)$. The advection--diffusion equation is integrated directly using a pseudo-spectral method at resolutions up to $2048^2$ grid points, with a frozen-noise Runge--Kutta scheme consistent with the white-in-time construction of the carrier flow. The simulations recover the duality between advected scalar and advecting flow---the smoother the carrier, the rougher the scalar---together with the predicted scaling laws for the second-order statistics. Once the scaling range is properly identified, the fourth-order flatness anomaly is measured across the whole range of $h$, in agreement with the Lagrangian estimates of Frisch \textit{et al.} (1999) and with the perturbative predictions of Bernard \textit{et al.} (1998) and Pumir \textit{et al.} (1997). Benefiting from the fact that our Eulerian simulations give direct access to the full scalar field, we also examine the probability density functions of scalar increments and the corresponding higher-order statistics, which show systematic departures from Gaussianity and from log-normality, with the strongest deviations manifesting for intermediate values of $h$. A central outcome of this work is a systematic account of how the simulation parameters, in particular the molecular diffusivity, must be adjusted with $h$, providing practical guidelines for reliable simulations; we further show that the residual deviations from the theoretical scaling laws are quantitatively accounted for by the finite spectral representation of the carrier flow. Our analysis also serve as a numerical baseline for simulating more realistic extensions of the Kraichnan model, where the carrier flow is coupled with a Gaussian multiplicative chaos and theoretical developments are limited.

</details>


### [4] [Sharp Logarithmic Quantum Dynamics for Quasiperiodic Schrödinger Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29339)
*Wencai Liu,Xueyin Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves sharp logarithmic lower bounds on position moment growth for one-frequency quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators with even potentials in the positive Lyapunov exponent regime, showing both the logarithmic scale and moment-order dependence are optimal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish sharp phase-uniform dynamical localization bounds for quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators, where standard bounded-moment localization fails due to phase sensitivity and only logarithmic upper bounds were previously known.

Method: Constructs a reflective version of semi-uniformly localized eigenfunctions adapted to two localization centers arising from completely resonant phases, enabling derivation of matching logarithmic lower bounds along specific time sequences.

Result: Sharp logarithmic lower bounds on all position moments — explicitly dependent on moment order — are proven for a class of one-frequency quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators with even potentials and positive Lyapunov exponents.

Conclusion: The known logarithmic upper bounds for phase-uniform dynamics in the positive Lyapunov exponent regime are optimal; neither the logarithmic scaling nor the moment-order dependence can be improved.

Abstract: Dynamical localization requires all position moments of a quantum wavepacket to remain bounded in time, but for quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators such bounds are generally not uniform in phase. In the positive Lyapunov exponent regime, the best known phase-uniform estimates instead grow on a logarithmic scale. We prove that both the logarithmic scale and the dependence on the moment order are sharp for a class of one-frequency quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators with even potentials. Our main ingredient is a reflective version of semi-uniformly localized eigenfunctions, adapted to the two localization centers forced by a completely resonant phase, from which we obtain matching logarithmic lower bounds along sequences of times.

</details>


### [5] [Simultaneous Reconstructions of (x,t) -Dependent Coefficients and Initial Conditions of Parabolic Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29377)
*Michael V. Klibanov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the first inverse problem involving simultaneous reconstruction of an (x,t)-dependent coefficient and unknown initial condition in a general linear second-order parabolic PDE, with formally determined (n+1)-dimensional data; it establishes uniqueness and stability estimates—including novel logarithmic stability for time-reversed initial conditions—and identifies applications in Mean Field Games and epidemic tracking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of prior work—where unknown coefficients were assumed to depend only on space or only on time, and initial conditions were typically known—the authors tackle the more realistic and mathematically challenging case of jointly reconstructing both a spatiotemporal coefficient and an unknown initial condition from formally determined data.

Method: Analysis of a general linear second-order parabolic equation; derivation of Carleman-type estimates to prove uniqueness and conditional stability (specifically logarithmic stability) for the inverse problem; treatment of time-reversed data to obtain stability for initial conditions.

Result: Uniqueness theorem and conditional stability estimates (including new logarithmic stability estimates) for the simultaneous reconstruction problem; first such results for initial conditions of parabolic equations/inequalities with time-reversed data.

Conclusion: The proposed inverse problem is well-posed in the conditional stability sense; the theoretical framework extends existing methodologies and enables applications in opinion dynamics (Mean Field Games) and spatiotemporal epidemic modeling.

Abstract: This is the first publication, in which an inverse problem of the simultaneous reconstruction of an unknown (x,t) - dependent coefficient and an unknown initial condition in a general linear parabolic equation of the second order is considered. The input data depend on (n+1) variables and are, therefore, formally determined ones. Stability estimates and uniqueness theorem are obtained for this inverse problem. As a by-product, logarithmic stability estimates for initial conditions of parabolic equations and inequalities with time reversed data are obtained for the first time.
  All known publications about inverse problems for parabolic equations with formally determined input data and unknown coefficients assume that those coefficients depend either only on x or only on t. In addition, the initial condition is assumed to be known, except of two publications cited in the text.
  The inverse problem of this paper has two potential applications. The first one is in forecasting of public opinions in the framework of the Mean Field Games theory. The second one is in tracking spatiotemporal outbreaks of epidemics.

</details>


### [6] [Kubo Formulas and Bulk-Edge Correspondence for Curved Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29524)
*Aren Martinian,Tom Stoiber*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous, general bulk-edge correspondence for strong topological insulators using Roe algebras and non-separable KK-theory, proving equivalence among real-space index pairings (including Fredholm and Kubo-type formulas) and relating them to the standard dual Dirac operator via topological degree.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously prove the equivalence of various real-space expressions (Fredholm index pairings, Kubo formulas) for integer topological invariants of strong topological insulators, especially in the presence of arbitrary (e.g., curved) boundaries — extending recent work on curved geometries.

Method: Employs Roe algebras and a variant of KK-theory adapted to non-separable C*-algebras; constructs and analyzes index pairings with general position-space Dirac operators; uses coarse geometry (coarse equivalence to ℝ^d) and topological degree of the symbol function.

Result: 1) A general bulk-edge correspondence theorem for index pairings with arbitrary boundaries; 2) Equivalence (up to explicit multiplicity given by topological degree) between pairings using general and standard dual Dirac operators for spaces coarsely equivalent to ℝ^d; 3) A generalized Kubo formula expressing the index pairing as a real-space trace-class formula.

Conclusion: The various real-space topological invariants for strong topological insulators are fundamentally unified: their equality is guaranteed by coarse geometric and K-theoretic structure, and the bulk-edge correspondence holds generally beyond flat or periodic settings.

Abstract: Strong topological insulators are classified by integer invariants which admit different real-space expressions. Inspired by recent work studying these invariants for spaces with curved boundaries, we revisit the problem of equivalence of the various expressions, including Fredholm index pairings and Kubo formulas involving half-space projections. Using Roe algebras and a variant of KK-theory suitable for non-separable $C^*$-algebras, we prove a general form of bulk-edge correspondence for the index pairing with a general position-space Dirac operator in the presence of arbitrary boundaries. For spaces coarsely equivalent to $\mathbb{R}^d$, we show that up to multiplicity, these pairings are equal to those obtained with the standard dual Dirac operator, with the multiplicity explicitly given as the topological degree of the symbol function. In addition, we prove a generalized Kubo formula which computes the general index pairing by a real-space formula.

</details>


### [7] [A Morse-Family Integrator for Hamilton--Jacobi Dynamics Across Caustics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29645)
*F. Jiménez Alburquerque,M. Leok,C. Sardón,X. Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a geometric framework for implicit discrete Hamiltonian systems using discrete Morse families and Lagrangian relations, enabling consistent treatment of constrained, degenerate, and singular dynamics; it extends discrete Hamilton–Jacobi theory (Type-II) to Lagrangian submanifold propagation, and applies it to optical wavefront propagation through fold caustics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified geometric foundation for implicit, constrained, and degenerate discrete Hamiltonian systems—where standard symplectic maps fail—by treating Lagrangian submanifolds (not explicit maps) as the fundamental dynamical object.

Method: Constructs a discrete analogue of Tulczyjew's triple using discrete Morse families and Lagrangian relations; formulates Type-II discrete Hamilton–Jacobi theory via Lagrangian submanifold evolution; handles singular projections via composition of Type-II dynamics with Morse families to generate propagated Lagrangian submanifolds without graph representation.

Result: A coordinate-free, implicit geometric framework that (i) generalizes discrete Hamiltonian mechanics to singular and constrained settings, (ii) yields a discrete Hamilton–Jacobi relation for generating functions where defined, and (iii) provides a regular, global description of multivalued wavefronts across fold caustics via composed Morse-family dynamics.

Conclusion: Lagrangian submanifolds—not symplectic maps—are the natural primitive for discrete Hamiltonian dynamics; this perspective unifies implicit, singular, and variational structures, and enables robust numerical and geometric modeling of wave propagation through caustics.

Abstract: We develop a geometric framework for implicit discrete Hamiltonian systems based on discrete Morse families, Lagrangian relations, and discrete analogues of Tulczyjew's triple. The main idea is to regard the Lagrangian submanifold defining the discrete dynamics, rather than an explicit symplectic evolution map, as the fundamental geometric object. This viewpoint naturally accommodates implicit, constrained, and degenerate discrete systems.
  Within this framework, we formulate a Type--II discrete Hamilton--Jacobi theory in terms of the propagation of Lagrangian submanifolds between consecutive discrete steps. When these submanifolds are locally represented by exact one-forms $dW_k$ and $dW_{k+1}$, the resulting equations provide a discrete Hamilton--Jacobi relation between consecutive generating functions. More generally, when the projection onto configuration space becomes singular and a single-valued generating function ceases to exist, we show that the evolution can be described by the composition of Type--II discrete dynamics with Morse families. This yields a generating family for the propagated Lagrangian submanifold without requiring the dynamics to be represented as a graph.
  As an application, we consider the propagation of optical wavefronts through fold caustics. A Type--II discrete Hamiltonian yields a symplectic ray integrator, while a Morse family represents the multivalued wavefront near the caustic. Their composition provides a discrete propagation rule for the complete Lagrangian manifold across the singularity. In this way, the same geometric construction simultaneously provides a discrete Hamiltonian integrator and a regular representation of multivalued Hamilton--Jacobi solutions.

</details>


### [8] [Homogeneous attractive Bose-Einstein condensates with repulsive three-body interactions: the one-dimensional case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29874)
*Dinh-Thi Nguyen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional as the mean-field limit for a 1D attractive two-body + repulsive three-body Bose gas using intermediate Hartree theory, and analyzes how the three-body term affects ground-state energy and mass concentration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand how competing attractive two-body and repulsive three-body interactions shape the mean-field behavior and ground-state structure of a homogeneous 1D Bose gas.

Method: Intermediate Hartree theory applied to the many-body Hamiltonian; rigorous derivation of the mean-field limit yielding the homogeneous cubic-quintic NLS functional.

Result: Derivation of the cubic-quintic NLS functional as the exact mean-field limit; quantitative characterization of the repulsive three-body interaction's stabilizing effect on ground-state energy and its influence on mass concentration phenomena.

Conclusion: The repulsive three-body term fundamentally modifies the energetic landscape and spatial localization properties of the ground state, preventing collapse induced by attraction and enabling well-defined, stable mean-field descriptions.

Abstract: We investigate the behavior of a homogeneous one-dimensional Bose gas composed of $N$ identical bosons experiencing attractive two-body and repulsive three-body interactions. Utilizing intermediate Hartree theory, we rigorously derive the homogeneous cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional as the mean-field limit of the many-body Hamiltonian. Particular attention is given to the impact of the repulsive three-body term on ground state energy estimates and mass concentration properties

</details>


### [9] [Homogeneous attractive Bose-Einstein condensates with repulsive three-body interactions: the two-dimensional case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29875)
*Dinh-Thi Nguyen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously derives the cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional as the mean-field limit of a 2D homogeneous Bose gas with competing two-body (attractive) and three-body (repulsive) interactions, using Hartree theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the rigorous mean-field limit of a 2D Bose gas with competing attractive pairwise and repulsive three-body interactions, which is relevant for modeling quantum many-body systems with beyond-pairwise correlations.

Method: Intermediate Hartree theory applied to a homogeneous two-dimensional Bose gas; rigorous derivation of the effective energy functional in the large-N limit.

Result: The homogeneous cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional is established as the mean-field limit of the many-body Hamiltonian.

Conclusion: The competition between attractive two-body and repulsive three-body interactions leads to a well-defined mean-field description governed by a cubic-quintic NLS functional, confirming stability and scalability of the model in 2D.

Abstract: We consider a homogeneous Bose gas composed of $N$ identical bosons occupying an infinite two-dimensional space. This gas exhibits both an attractive two-body interaction and a repulsive three-body interaction. Via the intermediate Hartree theory, we rigorously derive the homogeneous cubic-quintic nonlinear Schroedinger functional as the mean-field limit of the model. Our investigation focuses on the system's behavior in relation to the two-body interaction.

</details>


### [10] [Local Density Approximation and Other Limit Regimes for a Homogeneous Bose Gas with Repulsive Three-Body Interactions in Low-Dimensional Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29876)
*Thi Anh Thu Doan,Dinh-Thi Nguyen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies energy minimizers for a quantum Bose gas model with competing cubic (attractive) and quintic (repulsive) nonlinearities in low dimensions (d ≤ 2); it proves existence of minimizers at fixed effective statistics parameter and derives a Thomas-Fermi-type effective model in the large-statistics-parameter limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the ground-state behavior of a quantum Bose gas with competing two-body attraction and three-body repulsion, particularly in low spatial dimensions where quantum and statistical effects dominate.

Method: Variational analysis of an energy functional with mass-critical cubic and quintic nonlinearities; rigorous minimization under fixed effective statistics parameter; asymptotic analysis in the large-parameter regime to derive effective models.

Result: Existence of energy minimizers is proven for all d ≤ 2 at fixed effective statistics parameter; in the large-parameter limit, a Thomas-Fermi-like effective model emerges for the homogeneous Bose gas; additional limit regimes governed by the mass-critical nonlinearity are identified.

Conclusion: The interplay between attractive cubic and repulsive quintic nonlinearities supports stable minimizers in low dimensions, and scaling limits yield simplified effective descriptions—most notably a Thomas-Fermi model—highlighting the role of effective statistics in governing macroscopic quantum behavior.

Abstract: In the whole space of low spatial dimensions, namely $d \leq 2$, we study the minimizers of an energy functional with an attractive cubic nonlinearity and a repulsive quintic nonlinearity, which describes a quantum Bose gas with a two-body attraction and a three-body repulsion. We prove the existence of minimizers at fixed effective statistics parameter. In the limit of a large effective statistics parameter of the mass-critical nonlinearity (with respect to the kinetic energy), we derive an effective Thomas-Fermi-like model for the homogeneous Bose gas. We also consider other limit regimes depending on the mass-critical nonlinearity.

</details>


### [11] [Multiple Nonlinear Waves by (Quantum) Neural Networks: Checking the AI supremacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29894)
*Luigi Martina,Riccardo Caricato,Riccardo Della Torre*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the capability of quantum physics-informed neural networks (QPINNs) to solve the Korteweg-de Vries (KdV) equation—modeling multi-wave propagation in a weakly nonlinear medium—with periodic boundary conditions, using analytical solutions as benchmarks and proposing quantitative indicators of performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether QPINNs are mature enough for solving challenging evolutionary PDEs, particularly high-genus solutions of the KdV equation that stress conventional numerical methods.

Method: Numerical experiments with QPINNs applied to the KdV equation under periodic boundary conditions; evaluation via custom indicators of merit, leveraging known analytical solutions as ground truth.

Result: Quantitative performance metrics for QPINNs on multi-wave KdV solutions; empirical evidence on accuracy, convergence, and robustness relative to solution complexity (e.g., genus).

Conclusion: QPINNs show promise for solving nonlinear evolutionary PDEs like the KdV equation, but their reliability depends critically on architecture design, training strategy, and the chosen indicators—highlighting both current capabilities and limitations for practical deployment.

Abstract: To test the potential of so-called quantum physics-informed neural network (QPINN) technology for solving evolutionary partial differential equations, we consider the problem of multiple wave propagation in a weakly nonlinear medium described by the Korteweg-de Vries equation with periodic boundary conditions. Although this problem is solvable analytically, high-genus solutions could represent a rather challenging testbed for numerical methods. Therefore, our idea is to test whether a QPINN is sufficiently mature for application in this context by providing a set of indicators of merit and performing several numerical experiments.

</details>


### [12] [Uniqueness of universal quantum dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30012)
*M. Y. Avetisyan,R. L. Mkrtchyan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the uniqueness of universal quantum dimension formulae under certain assumptions, showing that quantum dimensions of classical algebras admit a Vogel-universal representation as products/ratios of q-dimension factors with linear arguments in Vogel’s parameters (α, β, γ), and establishes simple criteria ensuring uniqueness of all known such formulae.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous uniqueness conditions for universal quantum dimension formulae—central in representation theory and quantum group applications—by leveraging Vogel's universal Lie algebra parametrization.

Method: Analytical derivation using Vogel's universal parameterization of simple Lie algebras; structural analysis of quantum dimensions as rational functions of q-exponentials with arguments linear in α, β, γ; proof of uniqueness via constraints on integer coefficients and allowed values of c ∈ {0,1,2}. 

Result: Proof of uniqueness of universal quantum dimension formulae under mild assumptions; explicit characterization of quantum dimensions of classical algebras as universal products/ratios of q-dimension factors with arguments aα + bβ + cγ, where c = 0,1,2 and a,b ∈ ℤ.

Conclusion: All known universal quantum dimension formulae are uniquely determined by their functional form and Vogel-parameter constraints, confirming structural rigidity in universal Lie algebra representation theory.

Abstract: We prove, under some assumptions, the uniqueness of universal quantum dimension formulae. We first show that the quantum dimensions of classical algebras have a specific form that can be represented universally, in Vogel's sense, as the product/ratio of q-dimension factors with arguments $aα+bβ+cγ$ where $c=0, 1, 2$ and $a, b$ are integers. On this basis, we derive simple uniqueness criteria, according to which all known universal quantum dimensions are unique.

</details>


### [13] [On the equivalence between the Polchinski flow and the Connes-Kreimer approaches to perturbative renormalisation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31049)
*Yvain Bruned,Paul Laubie,Aurélien Minguella*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous correspondence between the Polchinski functional renormalisation group flow and the Connes–Kreimer Hopf-algebraic renormalisation framework, showing they yield identical renormalised theories; it constructs a decorated-graph ansatz satisfying the Polchinski equation and derives the most general known form of the renormalised potential using forest duality and morphism properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify two conceptually distinct renormalisation frameworks—Polchinski’s Wilsonian flow (analytic, PDE-based) and Connes–Kreimer’s algebraic approach (combinatorial, Hopf-algebraic)—by proving their equivalence at the level of renormalised Green’s functions and effective potentials.

Method: Constructs a Feynman-diagrammatic ansatz using decorated rooted trees/forests; employs combinatorial renormalisation via Bogoliubov–Parasiuk–Hepp–Zimmermann (BPHZ) subtraction within the Connes–Kreimer Hopf algebra; proves that this ansatz satisfies the Polchinski flow equation by leveraging multiple Hopf algebra morphisms (e.g., extraction-contraction) and introduces a new duality formula for forests of decorated graphs.

Result: Demonstrates exact agreement between Polchinski-flow-renormalised correlation functions and those obtained via Connes–Kreimer renormalisation; derives an explicit, fully general expression for the renormalised effective potential in arbitrary Euclidean QFTs, extending all prior results.

Conclusion: The Polchinski flow and Connes–Kreimer renormalisation are not merely compatible but mathematically equivalent formalisms: the former can be solved exactly by the latter’s combinatorial structure, validating both as consistent foundations for perturbative renormalisation in general Euclidean QFTs.

Abstract: We prove a correspondence between the Polchinski flow and the Connes-Kreimer approaches to perturbative renormalisation, in the sense that the first yields the same renormalisation as the latter. More precisely, we show that an ansatz based on decorated graphs (Feynman diagrams),with a combinatorial renormalisation procedure solves the Polchinski equation. This result holds for very general Euclidean quantum field theories. We are able to derive from this the form of the renormalised potential which is, to the best of our knowledge, the most general one in the literature. The main ingredients of the proof are multiple morphism properties with respect to the renormalisation, as well as a novel duality formula for forests of decorated graphs.

</details>


### [14] [Boundary Quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov Equations and Integrability of Quantum Field Theories with Time-Dependent Bulk and Boundary Coupling Strengths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31080)
*Parameshwar R. Pasnoori*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the generalized Bethe ansatz framework to quantum many-body systems with time-dependent bulk couplings *and* time-dependent open boundary conditions, deriving integrability-compatible boundary dynamics and reducing the time-dependent Schrödinger equation to boundary quantum Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations; the exact wavefunction is obtained from their solution, and static RG invariants are shown to coincide with dynamical invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the recently formulated time-dependent Bethe ansatz (for periodic systems) to physically relevant open quantum systems where boundaries themselves evolve in time—enabling exact solvability beyond translation-invariant setups.

Method: Extension of the generalized Bethe ansatz to open boundary conditions via compatibility analysis of time-dependent reflection equations; derivation of boundary quantum Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations as matrix difference equations governing the time-evolution of the Bethe wavefunction; identification of dynamical invariants through RG-invariant correspondence.

Result: Exact solvability established for integrable time-dependent quantum chains with open boundaries; explicit mapping of time-dependent Schrödinger equation onto BqKZ equations; construction of exact time-dependent wavefunctions; proof that static renormalization-group invariants of the underlying integrable model are preserved as dynamical invariants under time evolution.

Conclusion: The generalized Bethe ansatz is robust under inclusion of time-dependent open boundaries, yielding a fully integrable framework governed by BqKZ equations and unifying static RG structure with time-dependent quantum dynamics.

Abstract: The generalized Bethe ansatz framework formulated in [P. R. Pasnoori, Phys. Rev. B 112, L060409 (2025)] provides a unified framework to find exact solutions to quantum many-body systems with time-dependent coupling strengths with periodic boundary conditions. In this work we extend this framework to the case of open boundary conditions where in addition to the time-dependent interactions in the bulk, the boundary conditions are explicitly time-dependent. We show that for integrable time-dependent bulk coupling strengths, the generalized Bethe ansatz framework provides the time-dependent boundary conditions compatible with integrability and reduces the time-dependent Schrodinger equation to a set of matrix difference equations called the boundary quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations. The solution to the BqKZ equations provides the explicit form of the exact wavefunction. We further show that the RG invariants of the corresponding static model identify with the dynamical invariants in the time-dependent model.

</details>


### [15] [Rotating-wave approximation for spin-boson models with structured fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31090)
*Aitor Balmaseda,Davide Lonigro,Juan Manuel Pérez-Pardo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives state-dependent bounds on the difference between two quantum evolutions governed by unbounded Hamiltonians with a shared form domain, using a novel second integration-by-parts technique at the sesquilinear form level—bypassing restrictive invariant domain assumptions. The bound depends on the norm of the time-integrated generator difference (not the integral of the norm), capturing cancellation from fast oscillations. It is applied to rigorously quantify the rotating-wave approximation (RWA) for structured spin-boson models—arbitrary massive dispersion, general measure space, admissible form factors—with an explicit, frequency-uniform bound valid on a dense subspace and exact in the high-frequency limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous, quantitative error bounds for approximations in quantum dynamics involving unbounded Hamiltonians—particularly where standard operator-domain-based techniques fail—and to provide a robust, explicit analysis of the rotating-wave approximation beyond idealized settings (e.g., flat dispersion, square-integrable cutoffs).

Method: Second integration by parts performed directly on sesquilinear forms (not operators), leveraging common form-domain structure; careful high-frequency asymptotic analysis of the spin-boson model; estimation via norms of integrated generators rather than pointwise norms to exploit oscillatory cancellation.

Result: A state-dependent, explicit bound on the evolution difference that scales with ||∫(H₁−H₂)dt|| (not ∫||H₁−H₂||dt); application yields a fully explicit, frequency-uniform RWA error bound for spin-boson models with arbitrary massive dispersion on a general measure space and mild form-factor conditions; bound holds on a dense subspace and vanishes as frequency → ∞.

Conclusion: The form-level integration-by-parts technique enables sharper, more flexible error estimates for quantum evolutions with singular generators; the resulting RWA bound is optimal in its dependence on frequency and model parameters, confirming the physical intuition of RWA validity in the high-frequency regime for realistic structured environments.

Abstract: We derive state-dependent bounds on the difference between two quantum evolutions generated by unbounded Hamiltonians sharing a common form domain. The main technical tool is a second integration by parts, performed at the level of sesquilinear forms rather than at the operator level, which removes the need for a common invariant operator domain. The resulting estimate involves the norm of the time-integrated difference of the two generators, rather than the integral of its norm, and is therefore sensitive to the averaging effects produced by fast-oscillating terms. As an application we prove a quantitative bound on the rotating-wave approximation for spin-boson models with a structured boson field, described by an arbitrary massive dispersion relation on a general measure space and by a suitable class of form factors. The proof involves a careful analysis of the high-frequency scaling. The bound holds on a dense subspace of states, is fully explicit, and all the constants entering it depend only on the parameters of the model and not on the frequency scale, so that the approximation becomes exact in the limit of large frequency.

</details>


### [16] [The Dunkl two-photon/Schrödinger algebras and their applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31104)
*Francisco J. Herranz,Danilo Latini*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Dunkl two-photon and Dunkl-Schrödinger algebras—reflection-extended versions of classical algebras—with isomorphic structure, cubic Casimir invariants, subalgebra analysis, Fock-space representations via Dunkl harmonic oscillator, and a vector-field realization yielding six Dunkl differential-difference equations, including a Dunkl heat-Schrödinger equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the two-photon and Schrödinger algebras by incorporating reflection symmetry via Dunkl operators, thereby enriching their algebraic structure and enabling new differential-difference equations with applications in mathematical physics.

Method: Algebraic construction using Dunkl operators and an involutive reflection generator; derivation of Casimir invariants (cubic for full algebras, quadratic for subalgebras); representation theory on Dunkl-Fock space and holomorphic Bargmann realization; explicit vector-field realization with Dunkl derivatives in 1+1 dimensions.

Result: Isomorphism between Dunkl two-photon and Dunkl-Schrödinger algebras; explicit cubic Casimir invariant; classification of key subalgebras with quadratic Casimirs; Fock-space and holomorphic representations; vector-field realization yielding six nontrivial Dunkl differential-difference equations, including a Dunkl analog of the heat-Schrödinger equation.

Conclusion: The Dunkl extensions provide a consistent, symmetry-enriched framework unifying algebraic, representation-theoretic, and differential-operator perspectives, extending classical results to the Dunkl setting and generating novel integrable PDEs.

Abstract: The work introduces the novel Dunkl two-photon and Dunkl-Schrödinger algebras as reflection-extended counterparts of the standard six-dimensional two-photon and Schrödinger algebras. These extensions are realized through the presence of an involutive generator representing a reflection, and they reduce to the undeformed two-photon and Schrödinger algebras in the absence of this additional generator in an appropriate limit. For both algebraic structures, which are shown to be isomorphic, we derive the associated cubic polynomial Casimir invariant. Furthermore, we identify several structurally significant subalgebras and compute their corresponding quadratic Casimir invariants. Representations of the Dunkl two-photon algebra are constructed on the Fock space of the one-dimensional Dunkl harmonic oscillator and subsequently translated into holomorphic realizations within the associated Dunkl-Fock-Bargmann representation. Finally, for the Dunkl-Schrödinger algebra, we present an explicit vector field realization in terms of the Dunkl spatial derivative, while keeping time continuous. When applied to the Casimir invariants of the aforementioned subalgebras, this realization yields a family of six nontrivial Dunkl differential-difference equations in $1+1$ dimensions, including, in particular, a Dunkl version of the well-known heat-Schrödinger equation.

</details>


### [17] [Exactly flat bands beyond the chiral limit in Bistritzer--MacDonald Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31147)
*Zhen Huang,Kevin D. Stubbs*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a family of nonchiral tunneling potentials for which the twisted bilayer graphene Hamiltonian retains exactly flat zero-energy bands at all magic angles—contradicting a conjecture (Open Problem 3 in Zworski’s survey)—and proves completeness of this family; it also shows that the standard Bistritzer–MacDonald model with first-harmonic tunneling generically lacks exact flatness except possibly at isolated nonchiral couplings, via a novel invertibility criterion based on projected perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve Open Problem 3 from Zworski's survey—whether exact flat bands can persist beyond the chiral limit in twisted bilayer graphene—and to understand the structural robustness of flat bands under symmetry-breaking (nonchiral) perturbations.

Method: Constructive analysis: explicit parametrization of nonchiral tunneling potentials preserving zero-energy flat bands; rigorous spectral analysis using Bloch decomposition and projection onto relevant subspaces; derivation of an invertibility criterion for the projected perturbation operator; local Taylor expansion to verify criterion failure/satisfaction.

Result: A complete classification of all admissible nonchiral potentials yielding exactly two zero-energy flat bands at every Bloch momentum and all real nonchiral couplings; proof that standard BM Hamiltonians with first-harmonic tunneling cannot support exact flatness except possibly at a discrete, non-accumulating set of coupling strengths.

Conclusion: Exact flatness is not exclusive to the chiral limit—it can be robustly realized in a precisely characterized nonchiral class; however, generic nonchiral deformations (e.g., standard BM) destroy exact flatness, establishing a sharp dichotomy governed by the vanishing of the projected perturbation.

Abstract: The chiral limit of the Bistritzer--MacDonald model for twisted bilayer graphene has exactly flat bands at magic angles. We show that exact flatness can persist beyond the chiral limit with nonchiral tunnelling potentials in the usual symmetry class. We construct a family of nonchiral tunnelling potentials for which the Hamiltonian has at least two zero-energy states at every Bloch momentum and for every real value of the nonchiral coupling. This gives a family of counterexamples to Open Problem~3 of Zworski's survey \cite{ZworskiSurvey}. Conversely, we prove that every admissible potential with this property belongs to this family, thereby obtaining a complete characterization. By contrast, we show that the standard BM model, namely, BM Hamiltonians with first harmonic tunnelling potentials, does not admit exactly flat bands at the magic angle except possibly at a discrete set of nonchiral coupling strengths, with no finite accumulation point. To prove this, we introduce an explicit invertibility criterion. The criterion requires the projected perturbation to have a nonzero scalar coefficient at some Bloch momentum, obstructing exact flatness for general nonchiral tunnelling. A local Taylor expansion argument verifies the criterion for the standard BM potentials. For the family constructed above, however, the projected perturbation vanishes at every Bloch momentum, so the invertibility criterion fails.

</details>

### [18] [Machine Learning Unveils Finite-volume Energy Shifts in Three-body System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28700)
*Wei-Jie Zhang,Zhenyu Zhang,Jifeng Hu,Bing-Nan Lu,Jin-Yi Pang,Qian Wang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper uses symbolic regression (PySR) to derive universal finite-volume extrapolation (FVE) formulae for three-body systems, successfully unifying short- and long-range interaction regimes and yielding a high-accuracy analytic expression for intermediate-range forces (~1 fm).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rigorous finite-volume extrapolation (FVE) is well-established for short-range interactions, but remains challenging for long-range potentials where the force range is comparable to the lattice size L—especially in three-body systems. A data-driven approach is needed to bridge theoretical gaps.

Method: The authors extend a prior two-body data-driven scheme by applying symbolic regression (via PySR) to lattice QCD-inspired three-body energy spectra, imposing physical constraints (e.g., asymptotic limits, dimensional consistency) to guide model discovery and ensure interpretability.

Result: They recover known short-range FVE limits (κ₃ ≫ κ₂ and κ₃ ∼ κ₂), derive a new analytic FVE formula for pure long-range potentials, and discover a unified formula incorporating both short- and long-range contributions—validated to perform excellently for force ranges ~1 fm.

Conclusion: Machine learning guided by physical priors enables discovery of novel, interpretable analytical formulas beyond conventional theory—demonstrating a powerful paradigm for data-driven hadron physics.

Abstract: Finite-volume extrapolation (FVE) is essential for extracting physical observables in the lattice calculation. While rigorous FVE formulations are well established for short-range potentials in both two- and three-body systems, long-range interactions with force ranges comparable to the lattice size $L$ remain challenging. Extending a previous data-driven scheme for two-body systems, we apply symbolic regression (PySR) to uncover universal three-body FVE formulae. For short-range potentials, we reproduce the two limiting cases, i.e. $κ_3\ggκ_2$ and $κ_3\simκ_2$. For pure long-range potentials, we obtain a dedicated analytic expression, and after incorporating short-range contributions, we uncover a unified formula consistent with the original PySR solution, which performs excellently in the intermediate force range around 1 fm. This work demonstrates that combining machine learning with physical constraints can yield novel analytical results inaccessible to conventional theoretical tools, advancing data-driven methodologies in hadron physics.

</details>


### [19] [Quantum Simulations of Two-Dimensional Non-Abelian Adjoint String Breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28752)
*Anthony N. Ciavarella,Roland de Putter,Ed Younis,Ermal Rrapaj*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper constructs local Krylov-based truncations of a pure SU(2) lattice gauge theory on triangular lattices to simulate adjoint string breaking on IBM quantum hardware, achieving quantitative agreement with tensor network results and identifying non-Abelian signatures in string dynamics and glueball production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance quantum simulation of strongly coupled quantum field theories—specifically non-Abelian gauge theories—by enabling direct probing of real-time dynamics such as adjoint string breaking, which is intractable for classical methods at large coupling.

Method: Local Krylov subspace truncation applied to SU(2) lattice gauge theory on triangular lattices; construction of adjoint strings with dynamical gluons; identification of dominant resonances at strong coupling; design of local operators to detect string oscillations and breaking; implementation of quantum circuits on IBM_boston (156-qubit device) for 8×8 and 16×8 lattices.

Result: Successful quantum simulation of adjoint string breaking with circuits containing 7,634 CZ gates and two-qubit depth 218; quantitative agreement with tensor network simulations; extraction of oscillation and glueball production rates exhibiting a distinctly non-Abelian signature of SU(2).

Conclusion: Local Krylov truncation enables practical, scalable quantum simulation of non-Abelian gauge dynamics on near-term hardware, validating the feasibility of observing genuine non-perturbative QFT phenomena—like string breaking—with measurable non-Abelian signatures.

Abstract: Quantum computers offer the potential to directly probe the dynamics of strongly coupled quantum field theories. As a step towards reaching this potential, local Krylov-based truncations of a pure SU(2) lattice gauge theory on a triangular lattice are constructed. Adjoint strings connected to dynamical gluons are constructed in this truncated theory, and the resonances dominating the long-term dynamics at a large value of the gauge coupling are determined. Local operators are constructed to identify string oscillations and breakings. This is used to perform a quantum simulation of adjoint string breaking on an $8\times8$ and $16\times8$-site lattice with ibm_boston using all 156 qubits. Quantitative agreement with tensor network simulations is obtained for circuits with 7,634 CZ gates with a two-qubit gate depth of 218. In this simulation, the rates of oscillations and glueball production are identified with a distinctly non-Abelian signature of the underlying gauge group.

</details>


### [20] [Detecting Multiple Phase Transitions in Lattice Systems with Intrinsic Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29090)
*Jie Mei,Tetsuo Hatsuda,Mei Huang,Lingxiao Wang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces intrinsic dimension (estimated via the two-nearest-neighbors method) as a geometric diagnostic to resolve multiple nearby phase transitions in lattice systems, demonstrating its efficacy in the 2D q-state clock model (q=5,9) and 4D U(1) Higgs model; it further proposes channel-decomposed intrinsic dimension for gauge-invariant observables and shows gauge-variant fields are dominated by unphysical gauge-orbit directions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve distinct transition scales and identify degrees of freedom associated with each transition in lattice systems exhibiting multiple nearby phase transitions—especially where conventional order parameters (e.g., energy, magnetization) fail to separate transitions.

Method: Estimates intrinsic dimension (ID) of Monte Carlo configuration ensembles using the two-nearest-neighbors (2NN) estimator; extends it to channel-decomposed ID using gauge-invariant plaquette (gauge sector) and Higgs (scalar sector) variables in the 4D U(1) Higgs model; compares against gauge-variant field configurations to assess gauge redundancy effects.

Result: In the 2D clock model, ID cleanly distinguishes ordered, quasi-critical (BKT), and disordered phases—even for closely spaced transitions (q=5), revealing a low-dimensional valley for the intermediate phase undetectable by energy/magnetization alone. In the 4D U(1) Higgs model, channel-decomposed ID isolates sector-specific transitions, while combined ID retains signatures of both; gauge-variant ID is dominated by gauge-orbit directions, confirming necessity of gauge fixing or gauge-invariant observables.

Conclusion: Channel-decomposed intrinsic dimension is a robust, geometric probe for disentangling multiple transitions in multi-sector lattice field theories; it provides a principled, unsupervised approach to identify relevant degrees of freedom and transition scales—motivating its application to full QCD to separate deconfinement and chiral crossover temperatures.

Abstract: Lattice systems with multiple nearby transitions pose two related challenges: resolving distinct transition scales and identifying the degrees of freedom primarily associated with each transition. We show that the intrinsic dimension of Monte Carlo configuration ensembles, estimated by the two-nearest-neighbors method, provides a geometric diagnostic for both problems. In the two-dimensional $q$-state clock model, the intrinsic dimension distinguishes the ordered, quasi-critical, and disordered regimes for both well-separated ($q=9$) and closely spaced ($q=5$) Berezinskii--Kosterlitz--Thouless transitions. In the $q=5$ case, the intermediate phase appears as a broad low-dimensional valley even when energy and magnetization do not separately resolve the two transitions. In the four-dimensional $U(1)$ Higgs model, we introduce channel-decomposed intrinsic dimensions based on gauge-invariant plaquette and Higgs variables. The dominant response of each channel tracks transitions associated with the corresponding degrees of freedom, while the combined channel retains features of both. We further show that intrinsic dimensions evaluated directly on gauge-variant fields are dominated by gauge-orbit directions, demonstrating the importance of removing gauge redundancy before interpreting configuration-space geometry. These results establish channel-decomposed intrinsic dimension as a geometric probe of lattice systems with multiple transitions and motivate its application to disentangling deconfinement and chiral crossover scales in full QCD.

</details>


### [21] [Symmetric Mass Generation for Domain-Wall Fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29963)
*Sho Araki,Hidenori Fukaya,Tetsuya Onogi,Satoshi Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper numerically demonstrates symmetric mass generation (SMG) for domain-wall fermions using lattice simulations of the Fidkowski-Kitaev Majorana chain in 2D; it shows selective gapping of mirror-wall edge modes via a four-Fermi interaction, with SMG signature manifesting as an imaginary-axis gap in the Dirac operator spectrum, and confirms feasibility of hybrid Monte Carlo with minimal Pfaffian phase reweighting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a nonperturbative, lattice-based framework for interaction-induced decoupling of mirror fermions in domain-wall fermion formulations—resolving the persistent challenge of chiral symmetry preservation while eliminating unphysical mirror degrees of freedom.

Method: Numerical simulation on a two-dimensional Euclidean lattice of the Fidkowski-Kitaev Majorana chain with two boundaries (physical and mirror walls); spectral analysis of the lattice Dirac operator; evaluation of the Pfaffian’s complex phase; use of hybrid Monte Carlo with phase reweighting.

Result: The local four-Fermi interaction gaps only the mirror-wall edge modes, leaving physical-wall modes intact; the Dirac operator eigenvalue gap opens along the imaginary axis—distinct from conventional real-mass shifts; the Pfaffian phase is small, enabling practical HMC simulation.

Conclusion: Symmetric mass generation can be realized nonperturbatively for domain-wall fermions on the lattice, providing a viable pathway to mirror-fermion decoupling without breaking chiral symmetry, with clear spectral signatures and computational feasibility.

Abstract: We report the first numerical examination of symmetric mass generation (SMG) for domain-wall fermions. Our simulation on a two-dimensional Euclidean lattice computes the path-integral of the Fidkowski-Kitaev Majorana chain with two boundaries, corresponding to the two (physical and mirror) walls. We demonstrate by evaluating the lattice Dirac operator eigenvalue spectrum that the local four-Fermi interaction selectively gaps the edge fermions on the mirror wall while leaving the physical wall intact. We further identify a characteristic signature of SMG in the path-integral: the gap of the Dirac operator opens along the imaginary axis of the complex spectrum, in contrast to the standard mass shift in the real direction. The conventional hybrid Monte Carlo algorithm remains practical with reweighting the complex phase of the Pfaffian, which is found to be remarkably small throughout the simulation. These results provide a practical nonperturbative framework for interaction-induced mirror-fermion decoupling of lattice domain-wall fermions.

</details>


### [22] [Bias-Corrected Machine-Learning Estimation of Chiral Condensate Cumulants: A Retrospective Lattice QCD Case Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30416)
*Benjamin J. Choi,Hiroshi Ohno,Akio Tomiya*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper conducts a retrospective case study on bias-corrected ML estimation of traces of inverse Dirac operator powers (Tr M⁻ⁿ, n=1–4) in lattice QCD, using a fixed dataset. It compares two supervised learning strategies—using Tr M⁻¹ vs. gauge observables as features—and evaluates performance in direct trace estimation and downstream applications: chiral condensate cumulants (single-ensemble and multi-ensemble reweighting). Bias correction improves agreement with full-data reference, especially after nonlinear post-processing; uncorrected estimates suffer amplified errors. Using Tr M⁻¹ as feature yields ~74.25% projected reduction in Dirac inversion cost, though excluding training/analysis overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the reliability and computational efficiency of bias-corrected machine learning for estimating computationally expensive spectral quantities (Tr M⁻ⁿ) in lattice QCD—particularly under finite labeled/training set ratios—and to quantify error propagation into physically relevant derived observables (cumulants, reweighting).

Method: Retrospective supervised learning on a fixed lattice QCD ensemble: (i) two feature sets—Tr M⁻¹ alone vs. gauge-invariant observables (plaquette, rectangle); (ii) bias correction applied to ML predictions; (iii) evaluation on direct Tr M⁻ⁿ estimation and two derived tasks—single-ensemble chiral condensate cumulants and multi-ensemble reweighting across quark masses; (iv) cost projection based on Dirac solve count reduction.

Result: Bias-corrected ML estimates agree closely with full-data reference across all Tr M⁻ⁿ (n=1–4) and derived applications; uncorrected estimates show significant error amplification in cumulant and reweighting steps. Using Tr M⁻¹ as input yields ~25.75% of conventional Dirac inversion cost (i.e., 74.25% reduction), assuming uniform solve cost and neglecting model training/analysis overhead.

Conclusion: Bias correction is essential for reliable ML-based estimation of Tr M⁻ⁿ in lattice QCD when used in nonlinear downstream analyses; Tr M⁻¹–based models offer substantial projected computational savings, but end-to-end efficiency requires accounting for training and analysis costs.

Abstract: We present a retrospective case study of bias-corrected machine learning (ML) estimates of traces of the inverse Dirac operator, $\text{Tr}\,M^{-n}$ ($n=1,2,3,4$), using a fixed lattice QCD dataset and examining how the results depend on the relative proportions of the labeled and training sets. Two supervised learning approaches are examined: one using $\text{Tr}\,M^{-1}$ as the input feature, and the other employing gauge observables such as the plaquette and rectangle. Beyond the direct estimation of $\text{Tr}\,M^{-n}$, we further investigate two derived applications of the ML estimations: the evaluation of the cumulants of the chiral condensate within a single ensemble and that obtained through multi-ensemble reweighting across ensembles with different quark masses. Within this fixed dataset, the bias-corrected estimates show close agreement with the full-data reference under the adopted evaluation criteria, while the uncorrected estimates can exhibit amplified deviations after the nonlinear cumulant and reweighting steps. For the approach using $\text{Tr}\,M^{-1}$ as the input feature, nominal solve-count accounting suggests that the Dirac-inversion cost could be reduced to approximately $25.75\%$ of that of the conventional calculation in the present setup. This value is a cost projection rather than an end-to-end benchmark: it assumes comparable costs for successive inversions and excludes model-training and analysis overhead.

</details>


### [23] [$D \to (K π)_{\mathbf{27}}$ at the SU(3)-flavour-symmetric point I: Methodology and strong phase determination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30737)
*Matthew Black,Felix Erben,Maxwell T. Hansen,Fabian Joswig,Nelson Pitanga Lachini,Rajnandini Mukherjee,Srijit Paul,Antonin Portelli*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents the first part of an SU(3)-flavour-symmetric lattice QCD study of the D → Kπ decay amplitude in the 27-irrep, using Wilson–clover ensembles with M_π = M_K ≈ 410 MeV. It computes the (Kπ)₂₇ scattering phase shift δ₂₇(E_cm) up to ~1640 MeV via Lüscher’s method applied to finite-volume spectra obtained from distillation and GEVP analysis across three lattice spacings; two continuum-extrapolation strategies yield consistent results. A scattering-length fit yields the strong phase δ₂₇(M_D^SU(3)) = −38.4(2.4)°. The methodology for extracting the weak matrix element ⟨(Kπ)₂₇|H_W|D⟩ is outlined, with full amplitude analysis deferred to a follow-up work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously compute the strong-interaction phase δ₂₇ for the D → Kπ decay in the SU(3)-symmetric limit — a crucial input for disentangling CP violation in charm decays — by first determining the (Kπ)₂₇ scattering amplitude via finite-volume lattice QCD, laying groundwork for the full weak decay amplitude extraction.

Method: Lattice QCD on SU(3)-symmetric Wilson–clover ensembles (OpenLat) with M_π = M_K ≈ 410 MeV; distillation-based construction of momentum-projected two-hadron correlation matrices; generalized eigenvalue problem (GEVP) to extract finite-volume energy spectrum; Lüscher’s method to obtain scattering phase shift δ₂₇(E_cm); continuum extrapolation performed both (i) on phase shifts and (ii) on finite-volume energies before phase extraction; scattering-length parametrization used to infer δ₂₇ at M_D^SU(3).

Result: Consistent δ₂₇(E_cm) across continuum-extrapolation strategies; scattering-length fit yields δ₂₇(M_D^SU(3)) = −38.4(2.4)°; methodology established for computing three-point functions ⟨(Kπ)₂₇|H_W|D⟩ and relating them to infinite-volume decay amplitudes via RFT or quantization condition extensions.

Conclusion: The strong phase for D → Kπ in the 27-irrep is robustly determined in the SU(3)-symmetric limit, validating the lattice methodology and continuum control; this sets the stage for the complete calculation of the physical decay amplitude including symmetry-breaking corrections in subsequent work.

Abstract: We present part one of an SU(3)-flavour-symmetric lattice QCD calculation of the amplitude for a $D$-meson decaying to a $Kπ$ final state in the 27-dimensional irreducible representation of the flavour symmetry group, denoted $(Kπ)_{\mathbf{27}}$. The Wilson--clover gauge ensembles used in this work, generated by the OpenLat collaboration, are tuned such that $M_π= M_K \approx 410\,\mathrm{MeV}$. Using the distillation framework, we construct a matrix of Euclidean correlation functions from pairs of single-hadron operators projected to definite spatial momentum. Solving a generalised eigenvalue problem yields the finite-volume energy spectrum that is used to determine the scattering phase shift from threshold up to $4 M_π\approx 1640 \,\mathrm{MeV}$, which sits below but plausibly within reach of $M_D^{\rm SU(3)} \simeq 1900\,\mathrm{MeV}$. The calculation is performed across three lattice spacings, and we apply two strategies in which the continuum limit is taken at different stages of the computation: (i) on the extracted scattering parameters and (ii) on the finite-volume energies at fixed physical volume before extracting the scattering parameters. We find consistent results across these methods for the scattering phase shift as a function of the centre-of-mass energy, $δ_{\mathbf{27}}(E_{\sf cm})$. Taking a scattering-length-only parametrisation, we infer a value for the strong phase of the weak decay, $δ_{\mathbf{27}}(M_D^{\rm SU(3)})=-38.4(2.4)^\circ$. We further describe the methodology for using the same operator basis to compute three-point correlation functions to extract $\langle (Kπ)_{\mathbf{27}}| H_W| D\rangle$, for the tree-level effective weak Hamiltonian $H_W$, and for relating such finite-volume matrix elements to the full decay amplitude. The complete analysis leading to the latter will be presented in a forthcoming manuscript.

</details>


### [24] [First-principles determination of anomaly-induced pion decay beyond the chiral limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31024)
*Tian Lin,Xu Feng,Lu-Chang Jin,Chuan Liu,Qi-Yuan Luo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the quark-mass corrections to the two-photon decay width of the neutral pion (π⁰ → γγ) using lattice QCD with domain-wall fermions, leveraging the anomalous PCAC relation to avoid four-point functions and chiral-logarithm cancellations; it achieves ~1% statistical precision and reports Γ(π⁰→γγ) = 8.09(22) eV, with a 2.3(1.4)% positive, isospin-breaking–dominated mass correction confirming π⁰–η–η′ mixing enhancement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine few-percent quark-mass corrections to the π⁰→γγ decay amplitude—required for precision QCD tests beyond the chiral limit—since the chiral-limit value is fixed by the ABJ anomaly but physical predictions need controlled corrections from nonzero light-quark masses.

Method: Uses the anomalous Partially Conserved Axial Current (PCAC) relation on two nearly-physical domain-wall fermion ensembles to compute mass corrections as deviations from the exact anomaly condition at q²=0; avoids computationally noisy four-point functions and problematic chiral-logarithm subtractions inherent in conventional approaches.

Result: Achieves ~1% statistical precision per ensemble; continuum-extrapolated decay width Γ(π⁰→γγ) = 8.09(22) eV; mass correction to the decay amplitude is +2.3(1.4)%, dominantly isospin-breaking, consistent with π⁰–η–η′ mixing enhancement.

Conclusion: The method enables a high-precision, ab initio determination of the π⁰→γγ decay width and its quark-mass corrections; the observed positive, isospin-breaking correction provides the first lattice confirmation of enhanced mixing between π⁰, η, and η′ due to QCD dynamics beyond the chiral limit.

Abstract: The two-photon decay of the neutral pion is fixed in the chiral limit by the Adler-Bell-Jackiw anomaly, while nonzero quark masses induce few-percent corrections that must be determined for precision tests of QCD beyond the chiral limit. Using the anomalous PCAC relation, we compute them in lattice QCD as deviations from the exact anomaly condition at $q^2=0$, thereby avoiding both four-point functions and the cancellation of chiral logarithms that limits conventional approaches. Because the correction is proportional to the light-quark masses, both statistical and systematic uncertainties are correspondingly suppressed, making a precision calculation feasible. On two nearly-physical domain-wall ensembles we achieve $\sim1\%$ statistical precision each for the decay width, obtaining $Γ(π^0\toγγ)=8.09(22) eV$ after continuum extrapolation. The $2.3(1.4)\%$ mass correction to the decay amplitude, positive and isospin-breaking dominated, provides the first ab initio confirmation of the $π^0$-$η$-$η'$ mixing enhancement.

</details>

### [25] [Constrained functional priors for Bayesian inference of hot QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28786)
*G. Guimarães,L. Perin,M. Luzum*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a nonparametric Gaussian process framework for constructing physically constrained functional priors in Bayesian analyses of heavy-ion collisions, using truncated Karhunen–Loève expansions for emulator inputs and comparing rejection-based vs. pushforward constraint enforcement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the significant uncertainty arising from ad hoc low-dimensional parametrizations of functional inputs (e.g., transport coefficients, equation of state) in Bayesian heavy-ion collision analyses—particularly their artificial correlations and sensitivity to assumed functional forms.

Method: Develops a Gaussian process (GP)-based nonparametric prior framework; employs truncated Karhunen–Loève (KL) expansions for dimensionality reduction; analyzes incorporation of physical constraints via two strategies: (i) rejection sampling (inducing non-Gaussian measures and coefficient correlations), and (ii) pushforward constructions (preserving tractable Gaussianity in a latent space).

Result: Pushforward-constrained KL representations maintain analytically tractable Gaussian priors in latent space while rigorously enforcing physical constraints (e.g., positivity, monotonicity); rejection-based methods yield distorted, non-Gaussian posterior measures and spurious correlations among expansion coefficients; the framework enables systematic, flexible, and statistically sound functional priors for emulator-based inference.

Conclusion: The pushforward GP-KL framework provides a principled, scalable, and physically faithful approach to functional prior construction in heavy-ion Bayesian inference, resolving key limitations of conventional parametric priors and clarifying how constraint encoding affects statistical structure in finite-dimensional emulator inputs.

Abstract: Bayesian analyses of heavy-ion collisions rely on functional inputs, such as temperature-dependent transport coefficients and the equation of state, whose prior specification remains a significant source of uncertainty. Standard approaches employ low-dimensional parametrizations that can impose artificial correlations and leave the resulting inference sensitive to the assumed functional form.
  We develop a nonparametric framework for constructing priors over such functions using Gaussian processes, tailored to emulator-based inference in heavy-ion phenomenology. Truncated Karhunen--Loève expansions yield optimized finite-dimensional representations suitable for use as emulator inputs. We investigate how physical constraints can be incorporated into these priors, demonstrating that rejection-based methods produce non-Gaussian measures and induce nontrivial correlations in the expansion coefficients. By contrast, pushforward constructions enforce the constraints while preserving a tractable Gaussian measure in a latent space.

</details>


### [26] [First Evidence for an Unambiguous Triangle Singularity from $ψ(2S) \to p\bar{p}η$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29006)

Abstract: Triangle singularities, predicted by Landau in 1959, are purely kinematic enhancements arising from hadronic rescattering loops. Despite their proposed role in various anomalous decay processes and exotic hadron candidates, a direct experimental confirmation has remained elusive for more than six decades. We analyze the $pη/\bar{p}η$ invariant mass spectrum in $ψ(2S) \to p\bar{p}η$ measured by the BESIII Collaboration. The data exhibit a clear cusp-like structure around 1.564~GeV in the $N(1535)$ region, in precise agreement with the kinematic position predicted for the triangle singularity. Including the triangle singularity loop in the fit substantially improves the description of the data, with $χ^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}$ decreasing from 1.22 to 0.90, corresponding to a significance of $\sim 3.8σ$ for the triangle singularity contribution. The precise alignment of the observed excess with the predicted kinematic position provides the first compelling evidence for the triangle singularity effect.

### [27] [Towards power corrections in the factorization of baryon quasi-distribution amplitudes in LaMET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29042)
*Yu-Ji Shi,Jun Zeng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the first systematic analysis of power corrections—specifically target-mass and higher-twist effects—to the leading-twist quasi-distribution amplitude (quasi-DA) of baryons within LaMET, derives an exact all-order resummation of target-mass corrections for the LCDA–quasi-DA moment relation, validates numerically for the Λ baryon using lattice data, and constructs the next-to-leading-twist operators in quasi-DA factorization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and control power corrections (target-mass and higher-twist) in the LaMET-based extraction of light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) for baryons—critical for precision QCD phenomenology but previously unaddressed systematically for leading-twist baryon quasi-DAs.

Method: Analytic QCD factorization formalism within large-momentum effective theory (LaMET); derivation of exact moment relations between quasi-DA and LCDA; all-orders resummation of target-mass corrections at leading twist; explicit construction of next-to-leading-twist operator structures; numerical evaluation using existing lattice QCD data for the Λ baryon.

Result: An exact closed-form relation resumming target-mass corrections to all orders at leading twist for baryon quasi-DAs; numerical confirmation that target-mass corrections vanish rapidly with increasing hadron momentum and are negligible near LCDA endpoints; explicit identification and construction of the next-to-leading-twist operators contributing to quasi-DA factorization.

Conclusion: Target-mass corrections to baryon quasi-DAs are controllable and small at sufficiently high momentum, validating LaMET’s applicability; the derived formalism enables robust, high-precision lattice extractions of baryon LCDAs—including for heavy baryons—and lays groundwork for quantifying higher-twist systematics in future studies.

Abstract: Light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) are essential to precision phenomenological studies. They can be accessed from lattice QCD through the large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) via quasi-distribution amplitudes (quasi-DAs). Factorization of quasi-DAs receive power corrections in inverse powers of the hadron momentum, including target-mass and higher-twist corrections. In this work, we present the first systematic analysis of such power corrections for the leading-twist baryon quasi-DA. Establishing the moment relation between the quasi-DA and the LCDA, we derive an exact closed-form relation that resums target-mass correction to all orders at leading twist. This result also applies to heavy baryons and to quasi-transverse-momentum-dependent distributions. We numerically assess these corrections for the $Λ$ baryon quasi-DA using existing lattice data, finding that the target-mass correction decreases rapidly with increasing baryon momentum and is almost negligible in the endpoint regions. In addition, we explicitly construct the next-to-leading-twist operators entering the quasi-DA factorization. Our results are a first step toward quantifying the power corrections in future lattice determinations of light or heavy baryon LCDAs.
