Conclusion: Non-invertible symmetries provide a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable mechanism to control flavor structure in lepton-portal dark matter, offering distinctive, testable predictions beyond standard invertible symmetry approaches.

Abstract: We point out that a non-invertible selection rule can control the flavor violation inherent in lepton portal dark matter, a weakly interacting massive particle candidate that can evade severe constraints from direct detection experiments. We show that ordinary invertible symmetries cannot suppress flavor-violating portal couplings while accommodating the large mixing angles in the PMNS matrix. We then present a simple construction based on the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauging of a $\mathbb{Z}_5$ symmetry, which realizes one-flavor dominance of the portal couplings consistently with the PMNS mixing matrix. We also discuss possible flavor leakage in a type-I seesaw completion and study experimental probes of the model through lepton observables, especially lepton flavor non-universality in $Z$ boson decays.

</details>


### [29] [Higgs boson pair production in SMEFT: shape and truncation studies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29132)

TL;DR: This paper compares how Wilson coefficients affect Higgs boson pair production in SMEFT (up to dim-6, including loop-suppressed operators), incorporating NLO QCD corrections and polynomial parametrizations; it identifies benchmark scenarios with significant shape distortions in $m_{hh}$ and $p_{T,h}$ distributions, revealing limits of the linear SMEFT truncation.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the reliability and validity of the linear SMEFT expansion in describing Higgs pair production observables—particularly where higher-order (quadratic) Wilson coefficient contributions become essential for physical consistency.

Method: Comparative analysis of SMEFT dimension-6 Wilson coefficient effects on hh production cross sections using updated polynomial parametrizations that include NLO QCD corrections and loop-suppressed operators; differential distributions ($m_{hh}$, $p_{T,h}$) are studied to identify shape-sensitive benchmark scenarios.

Conclusion: The linear SMEFT truncation is insufficient for interpreting certain Higgs pair production observables under large anomalous couplings; inclusion of quadratic Wilson coefficient contributions is necessary to maintain physical consistency in differential predictions.

Abstract: We present a comparative study of the influence of Wilson coefficients on Higgs boson pair production cross sections in SMEFT up to dimension-6, also including loop-suppressed operators. Our study accounts for NLO QCD corrections, and is based on updated parametrizations of the cross section as a polynomial in the Wilson coefficients. We focus on differential distributions for the Higgs boson pair invariant mass, as well as the transverse momenta of the leading Higgs bosons, identifying benchmark scenarios exhibiting pronounced shape distortions in the $m_{hh}$ and $p_{T,h}$ distributions. While some of these scenarios remain physically meaningful under a purely linear truncation of the SMEFT expansion, others yield unphysical differential cross sections when quadratic dimension-6 contributions are omitted, exposing the breakdown of the linear approximation for large anomalous couplings.

</details>


### [30] [Probing strange-sector flavor-changing neutral currents at DUNE-like facilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29178)
*Xin-Shuai Yan,Miao Liu,Qin Chang,Ya-Dong Yang*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model via flavor-changing neutral-current (FCNC) interactions involving down–strange quarks in neutrino scattering — a process highly suppressed in the SM — using next-generation long-baseline experiments like DUNE.

Method: Analyzes inclusive deep-inelastic neutrino scattering at DUNE-like facilities within a model-independent, low-energy effective field theory framework accommodating both Dirac and Majorana neutrinos; computes projected sensitivities to dimensionless Wilson coefficients of FCNC operators for CP- and τ-optimized beam configurations; incorporates statistical and systematic uncertainties (notably background-rate for near detector, signal-rate for far detector); evaluates dependence on neutrino mass ordering and δ_CP.

Result: Near-detector bounds on dimensionless Wilson coefficients reach O(10⁻³–10⁻²) relative to G_F; far-detector bounds are ~10× weaker; systematic uncertainties dominate sensitivity: background-rate uncertainty limits near-detector performance, while signal-rate uncertainty limits far-detector reach; only electron-flavor FCNC coefficients exhibit significant dependence on neutrino mass ordering and δ_CP.

Conclusion: DUNE-like facilities offer competitive sensitivity to ds-quark FCNC neutrino interactions, especially at the near detector; optimal exploitation requires control of background-rate systematics near and signal-rate systematics far; results highlight complementarity between near/far detectors and underscore the importance of beam optimization and flavor-specific analysis for probing BSM physics.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of DUNE-like facilities to flavor-changing
  neutral-current interactions between down and strange quarks through inclusive
  deep-inelastic neutrino scattering. In the framework of a general low-energy
  effective Lagrangian for Dirac and Majorana neutrinos, we evaluate projected
  sensitivities for CP- and $τ$-optimized beam configurations. The strongest
  bounds on the dimensionless Wilson coefficients, defined relative to the Fermi
  constant $G_F$, reach $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})\text{--}\mathcal{O}(10^{-2})$ at the
  near detector, whereas far-detector limits are roughly one order of magnitude
  weaker. When systematic uncertainties are included, the near-detector
  sensitivity is dominated by the background-rate uncertainty, while the
  far-detector sensitivity is limited primarily by the signal-rate uncertainty.
  At the far detector,
  the dependence of the projected bounds on the neutrino mass ordering and the Dirac
  CP-violating phase $δ_{\mathrm{CP}}$ is largely confined to coefficients
  involving the electron flavor.

</details>


### [31] [Embedding dependence of fermion mass hierarchies in $S_3$-symmetric Yukawa sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29259)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how embedding choices of irreducible representations in family-symmetric Yukawa tensors affect mass spectra, focusing on S₃ symmetry with three generations. It identifies continuous families of invariant Yukawa subspaces, derives spectral constraints (e.g., singular-value bounds), and shows that fixed embeddings impose hierarchy limits—while varying embeddings can realize any positive mass spectrum. It applies these results to a Clifford-algebraic model, revealing an obstruction to reproducing the observed charged-lepton mass hierarchy due to its rigid embedding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the freedom in embedding degenerate irreducible representations—beyond representation content alone—affects physical observables like fermion mass spectra in family-symmetric models.

Method: Group-theoretic analysis of S₃ representation embeddings in Sym²V; classification of invariant Yukawa subspaces as a CP¹×CP¹ family 𝒧_{t,κ}; solution of the inverse singular-value problem for κ=0; derivation of rank-one matrix conditions and singular-value ratios; application to a specific Clifford-algebraic Yukawa construction.

Result: For S₃ with V = 1⊕2, invariant complex-symmetric Yukawa tensors form a CP¹×CP¹ family; the κ=0 branch allows arbitrary positive mass spectra iff embedding is varied; rank-one matrices exist in 𝒧_{t,κ} iff 2tκ²=1, implying unbounded σ₃/σ₂; in a Clifford-based model with embedding 𝒧_{2,0}, singular values obey m₁+m₂ ≤ m₃ ≤ m₁+3m₂, excluding the observed charged-lepton hierarchy under arbitrary vacuum alignment.

Conclusion: Mass spectrum viability in family-symmetric models depends critically on the choice of multiplicity embedding—not just the group representation assignment—and rigid embeddings can introduce unavoidable phenomenological obstructions.

Abstract: When irreducible representations occur with multiplicity, a family symmetry fixes the representation content of a Yukawa sector but need not fix its embedding in the space of generation tensors. We study the spectral consequences of this additional embedding data for complex-symmetric three-family tensors with the $S_3$ assignment $V=\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{2}$. Since $\operatorname{Sym}^2V=2\,\mathbf{1}\oplus2\,\mathbf{2}$, three-dimensional invariant Yukawa subspaces of type $\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{2}$ form a continuous $\mathbb{CP}^1\times\mathbb{CP}^1$ family, denoted $\mathcal L_{t,κ}$ on a finite affine chart. On the $κ=0$ branch, we solve the inverse singular-value problem and obtain necessary and sufficient conditions for any prescribed ordered positive mass spectrum. A fixed singlet embedding imposes a finite hierarchy bound, whereas varying the embedding accommodates every positive three-family spectrum. For general embeddings, $\mathcal L_{t,κ}$ contains a nonzero rank-one matrix if and only if $2tκ^2=1$; equivalently, these are precisely the finite-chart embeddings for which $σ_3/σ_2$ is unbounded. In a conventional complex-symmetric $S_3$ three-Higgs sector, the same condition becomes $y_2y_5^2=\sqrt{2}\,y_1y_3^2$, so order-one Yukawa coupling ratios can support arbitrarily large mass hierarchies. We then apply the result to a recent Clifford-algebraic construction and show that its family-resolved Yukawa tensors span $\mathcal L_{2,0}$, whose singular values obey $m_1+m_2\leq m_3\leq m_1+3m_2$. This excludes the observed charged-lepton hierarchy for arbitrary complex vacuum alignment within the existing Higgs directions. The resulting obstruction is therefore tied to the fixed multiplicity embedding rather than to the $S_3$ representation content alone.

</details>


### [32] [Probing Ultra-Compressed Scotogenic Dark Matter at the HL-LHC via 4D Spacetime Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29275)

Main category: hep-ph

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing LHC searches (displaced-lepton, disappearing-track) have poor, unoptimized sensitivity to the ultra-compressed scotogenic regime where co-annihilation yields correct DM relic density; the small mass splitting (~2 GeV) and macroscopic decay length (~100–1000 mm) motivate using precision timing as a discriminative fourth dimension.

Method: Exploits HL-LHC’s 30 ps timing resolution to detect arrival-time delays (70–700 ps) of leptons from charged inert scalar decays; employs low-pT disappearing-track stubs matched to delayed timing hits—bypassing full lepton reconstruction; validated on 4.20×10⁶ simulated prompt SM events.

Result: Signal efficiency 12–36% (14% at 200 GeV benchmark); zero background observed; projects 95% C.L. exclusion up to scalar masses ≈670 GeV with 3000 fb⁻¹; timing window is compatible with, but not uniquely fixed by, radiative neutrino mass generation (constrained by y²λ₅).

Conclusion: The co-annihilation corridor—traditionally hardest to probe—is made accessible via 4D spacetime tracking; precision timing transforms otherwise challenging ultra-compressed scenarios into viable discovery targets at HL-LHC.

Abstract: The ultra-compressed scotogenic regime, in which a 2\,GeV mass splitting between the charged inert scalar and the dark-matter fermion is motivated by and compatible with co-annihilation to the observed relic density for suitable choices of the remaining model parameters, produces decay leptons for which existing LHC displaced-lepton and disappearing-track searches retain only partial, unoptimised acceptance, leaving room for a dedicated timing-assisted strategy. We propose exploiting the 30\,ps timing resolution of the HL-LHC precision timing detectors as a fourth observable, converting the macroscopic scalar decay length of 100--1000\,mm into an arrival-time delay of 70--700\,ps---more than three orders of magnitude above the sub-picosecond delays of all prompt Standard Model backgrounds. Operating at the level of a low-$p_T$ disappearing-track stub matched to a delayed timing hit, without requiring full lepton reconstruction, our 4D timing strategy achieves signal efficiencies of 12--36\% across the probed mass range (14\% at the 200\,GeV benchmark) and zero background across $4.20\times10^6$ simulated prompt SM events, projecting 95\%\,C.L.\ exclusion up to scalar masses of $\simeq\!670$\,GeV at 3000\,fb$^{-1}$ under conservative background assumptions. The scalar lifetime is set by the Yukawa coupling $y$ through $cτ\propto y^{-2}$, while sub-eV neutrino masses constrain only the product $y^2λ_5$; the small couplings that place the charged scalar in the optimal timing window are therefore compatible with, though not uniquely fixed by, the radiative neutrino-mass mechanism, making the co-annihilation corridor---conventionally the most elusive regime---an accessible target for 4D spacetime tracking at the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [33] [Finite-Width Dissolution of Radial Spectroscopy in Single-Top Mesonic Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29476)
*Bing-Dong Wan,Shuo Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how the width of a heavy unstable particle (like the top quark) propagates to bound-state poles in a relativistic Bethe–Salpeter framework, confirming width inheritance at leading order and showing radial excitations dissolve as width increases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the finite width of a heavy unstable constituent (e.g., top quark) affects the spectrum and pole structure of its bound states—especially given the extreme hierarchy between its width and mass—and to assess observability of radial excitations in realistic, width-dependent settings.

Method: Employs an instantaneous Bethe–Salpeter formalism with complex (non-Hermitian) top quark pole mass; uses biorthogonal Hellmann–Feynman theorem to demonstrate width inheritance at operator level; performs artificial heavy-mass scans to verify $m_Q^{-2}$ scaling; computes low-pole source-projected response and full resolvent via non-Hermitian re-diagonalization.

Result: Confirms width inheritance up to $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm kin}^2/m_Q^2)$; radial splittings remain $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm rad})$; increasing width dissolves narrow radial peaks into a single broad maximum near physical top width in $t\bar b$, $t\bar c$, $t\bar u$ channels; stable-top eigenvalues persist as reference poles but not as resolvable multi-peak spectrum.

Conclusion: Radial excitations of top-quark-containing systems are not experimentally resolvable as distinct peaks due to top-width-induced spectral smearing; instead, they may manifest indirectly via process-dependent signatures such as broad threshold enhancements or altered color flow in $Wb\bar q$ final states.

Abstract: Within the heavy-mass expansion, the pole width of a system containing one unstable heavy constituent inherits the constituent width up to $\mathcal O(Λ_{\rm kin}^2/m_Q^2)$ corrections, while radial splittings remain $\mathcal O(Λ_{\rm rad})$. The top quark is an extreme realization of this hierarchy. We implement the complex top pole mass in an instantaneous Bethe--Salpeter framework, where a biorthogonal Hellmann--Feynman relation realizes width inheritance at the operator level and an artificial heavy-mass scan confirms the predicted $m_Q^{-2}$ suppression. The low-pole source-projected response has a single broad maximum at the physical top width in the $t\bar b$, $t\bar c$, and $t\bar u$ channels. Full width-dependent non-Hermitian re-diagonalization and a direct full-matrix resolvent evaluation confirm the progressive dissolution of the small-width radial maxima. </details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We discuss several issues regarding the modern generalization of Weinberg's compositeness criterion. In particular, we demonstrate that the on-shell renormalization in NREFT reproduces Weinberg's result and provides a simple description of resonances, bound states, and coupled channel effects. Additionally, we present the complete propagator, including the charged channel for the near-threshold p-wave states.

Through recursive application of the renormalization group equation, non-conformal terms are absorbed into the strong running coupling, yielding a scheme-independent, scale-invariant perturbative series with improved convergence. Based on the improved and more precise series, the $95.5\%$ BA credible interval yields an uncertainty of $Δδ_\mathrm{QCD}|_{\mathrm{PMC, BA}}^{\mathrm{N^5LO}} = \pm 1.0\times 10^{-5}$. The resulting hadronic branching ratio is $\mathcal{B}(W\to \text{hadrons})|_\mathrm{PMC} = (65.84\pm 1.54)\%$, which is consistent with experimental data within reasonable errors.

*Mamiya Kawaguchi,Kazuhiro Tanaka,Mitsuru Tanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes meson gravitational D-form factors in the three-flavor linear sigma model, linking them to chiral and scale symmetry breaking; it derives analytical expressions in symmetric/chiral limits, identifies anomaly-driven corrections for singlets, quantifies flavor-breaking effects on scalar-meson D-form factors, and contrasts predictions with dilaton-augmented chiral perturbation theory—highlighting D-form factors as sensitive probes of symmetry-breaking mechanisms in low-energy QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how meson gravitational form factors—specifically D-form factors—encode information about chiral symmetry breaking, scale symmetry breaking, and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly in low-energy QCD, using a phenomenologically grounded effective model.

Method: Analytical computation of meson D-form factors within the three-flavor linear sigma model; treatment of scalar meson dominance; derivation in symmetric and chiral limits; inclusion of explicit flavor symmetry breaking via scalar-meson mass-splitting interactions; comparison with dilaton-augmented chiral perturbation theory based on canonical mass dimension analysis of interaction terms.

Result: In the chiral limit, octet pseudoscalar D-form factor forward value is fixed at −1; $U(1)_A$ anomaly induces nonzero contributions to singlet pseudoscalar and scalar-meson D-form factors; realistic flavor breaking strongly modifies scalar-meson D-form factors; differences between linear sigma and dilaton-chPT results are determined by mass dimensions of symmetry-breaking interaction terms.

Conclusion: Meson D-form factors are highly sensitive discriminators of distinct symmetry-breaking patterns (chiral vs. scale, anomalous vs. non-anomalous, symmetric vs. broken flavor), making them valuable observables for probing the structure of low-energy QCD dynamics.

Abstract: We investigate meson gravitational D-form factors in the three-flavor linear sigma model and their connection to symmetry breaking in low-energy QCD. Within the scalar meson dominance picture, we examine the scalar meson exchange contributions and their relation to meson masses through scalar-meson couplings. In the three-flavor symmetric limit, we first derive analytical expressions for the D-form factors of pseudoscalar and scalar mesons. In the chiral limit, the forward-limit value of the octet pseudoscalar D-form factor is fixed to $-1$, whereas the $U(1)_A$ anomaly generates additional contributions to the singlet pseudoscalar and scalar-meson D-form factors. For realistic flavor breaking, the interaction introduced to reproduce the scalar-meson mass hierarchy below $1$ GeV significantly affects the scalar-meson D-form factors. We further compare the linear sigma model results with those obtained from a chiral perturbation theory Lagrangian including a dilatonic scalar field and show that their differences are governed by the canonical mass dimensions of the interaction terms contributing to meson mass generation. These results indicate that meson D-form factors provide sensitive probes of symmetry-breaking structures in low-energy hadron physics.

</details>

*Zhong-Bo Kang,Hongxi Xing,Fanyi Zhao,Yiyu Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reveals a jet-induced parity selection rule in transversely polarized semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS) with jet detection, using TMD QCD factorization: azimuthal harmonics cos(nφ_qJ) of spin-dependent structure functions obey strict parity-based symmetry constraints—e.g., Sivers asymmetry flips between left-right asymmetry (even n) and symmetry (odd n), while worm-gear retains up-down asymmetry but switches left-right behavior with n parity—enabling new EIC observables for soft-gluon dynamics and precision nucleon spin structure extraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Jets at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) are key probes of the nucleon's 3D spin structure, but their presence breaks azimuthal isotropy of soft-gluon radiation, distorting the very spin asymmetries used for extraction; this necessitates a rigorous theoretical treatment of jet-induced anisotropy within TMD factorization.

Method: Applies transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) QCD factorization to semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering with identified jets; expands transversely polarized structure functions in Fourier harmonics cos(nφ_qJ), where φ_qJ is the angle between the jet axis and the lepton-jet momentum imbalance vector; derives symmetry properties under parity transformations to establish selection rules for each harmonic.

Result: A parity selection rule emerges: for each harmonic cos(nφ_qJ), the azimuthal symmetry (left-right or up-down) of spin-dependent asymmetries is determined solely by the parity of n — e.g., Sivers function exhibits left-right asymmetry for even n and left-right symmetry for odd n; worm-gear retains up-down asymmetry for all n but switches left-right symmetry/antisymmetry with n parity; higher harmonics are predicted to be individually measurable at EIC kinematics.

Conclusion: Jet-induced anisotropy fundamentally reshapes transverse-spin observables via a rigorous parity-based harmonic selection rule; this provides novel, experimentally accessible azimuthal observables that must be incorporated into precision extractions of TMDs like the Sivers and worm-gear functions at the EIC.

Abstract: Jets provide a powerful probe of the three-dimensional spin structure of the nucleon, a central goal of the Electron-Ion Collider. Yet the observed jet defines an axis that breaks the azimuthal isotropy of soft-gluon radiation, thereby reshaping the very asymmetries used to extract that structure. Using transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) QCD factorization, we show for the first time that this jet-induced anisotropy imposes a parity selection rule on polarized asymmetries. Expanding the transversely polarized structure functions in harmonics $\cos(nφ_{qJ})$, where $φ_{qJ}$ is the angle between the jet and the lepton-jet momentum imbalance, makes this rule explicit: the symmetry of each harmonic is fixed by the parity of $n$, independently of the magnitudes of the harmonic coefficients. For the Sivers function, the canonical left-right asymmetry about the proton spin survives for even $n$ but is replaced by a counterintuitive left-right symmetry for odd $n$. The worm-gear function retains its up-down asymmetry at every harmonic while being left-right symmetric for even $n$ and asymmetric for odd $n$. At EIC kinematics, the higher harmonics studied here are predicted to be individually measurable, providing new observables for the azimuthal dynamics of soft radiation and an essential ingredient in precision extractions of nucleon structure.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the experimental anomalies in the anomalous magnetic moments of the muon and electron, (g−2)_μ and (g−2)_e, within a theoretically consistent extension of the Standard Model that also suppresses lepton-flavor violating (LFV) processes.

Result: The model accommodates the observed (g−2)_μ discrepancy (~10⁻⁹) and (g−2)_e discrepancy (~O(10⁻¹³)); all considered LFV decays are highly suppressed due to the U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} gauge symmetry.

Conclusion: The U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} left-right model with inverse seesaw provides a viable explanation for both (g−2) anomalies without violating stringent LFV bounds, highlighting the predictive power of gauged flavor-dependent symmetries.

Abstract: In the framework of a $U(1)_{L_μ -L_τ}$ left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrinos recently proposed, we show that one-loop contributions from heavy singly charged Higgs bosons to the anomalous magnetic moments of charged leptons $(g-2)_{e,μ}$ can accommodate the observed discrepancies, of $10^{-9}$ and $\mathcal{O}(10^{-13})$ for the muon and electron, respectively. Meanwhile, all lepton-flavor violating decay rates for $h\to e_b^\pm e_a^\mp $, $Z\to e_b^\pm e_a^\mp$, and $e_b\to e_a γ$ are found to be highly suppressed as a consequence of the gauged $U(1)_{L_μ-L_τ}$ symmetry.

</details>


### [40] [From Threshold Crossing to Wave-function Renormalization: Defining the Pion Mott Temperature in a Magnetic Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31125)
*Sidney S. Avancini,Maximo Coppola,Dyana C. Duarte,Ricardo L. S. Farias,Norberto N. Scoccola,William R. Tavares*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies neutral pion dissociation in hot, magnetized quark matter using the two-flavor NJL model, proposing an inflection-point-based definition of the magnetic Mott temperature via the pion wave-function renormalization factor Z_π⁰(T,eB), revealing pion dissociation as a spectral crossover—not a sharp threshold crossing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in defining the Mott temperature for neutral pions under strong magnetic fields, where Landau quantization generates multiple quark–antiquark continua and invalidates the conventional threshold-crossing criterion (m_π⁰ = 2M).

Method: Uses the two-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model to compute the neutral pion propagator and pole structure in hot, magnetized quark matter; defines the magnetic Mott temperature via the inflection point of the pion wave-function renormalization factor Z_π⁰(T,eB) associated with the lowest real pole.

Result: The proposed inflection-point criterion reproduces the conventional Mott temperature at eB → 0 and tracks the chiral pseudocritical temperature for both constant and magnetic-field-dependent couplings; shows that pion dissociation evolves smoothly as a spectral crossover under increasing magnetic field.

Conclusion: Pion dissociation in strong magnetic fields is not governed by a sharp threshold but manifests as a continuous spectral crossover; the inflection point of Z_π⁰(T,eB) provides a robust, physically motivated definition of the magnetic Mott temperature.

Abstract: We investigate the dissociation of the neutral pion in hot magnetized quark matter within the two-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model. At zero magnetic field, the Mott temperature is conventionally determined by $m_{π^0}(T_{\rm Mott})=2M(T_{\rm Mott})$, above which a real pole ceases to exist. At finite magnetic field, Landau quantization replaces this single threshold by a hierarchy of quark--antiquark continua and generates multiple solutions of the pion pole equation, rendering a direct threshold-crossing criterion ambiguous since a real pion solution below the lowest nominal threshold always exists. We therefore propose to define the magnetic Mott temperature through the inflection point of the pion wave-function renormalization factor $Z_{π^0}(T,eB)$ of that lowest pole, corresponding to the fastest loss of its spectral function strength. The prescription reproduces the conventional Mott temperature as $eB\to 0$ and tracks the chiral pseudocritical temperature for both constant and magnetic-field-dependent couplings. Our results characterize pion dissociation in a magnetic field as a spectral crossover rather than a simple threshold crossing.

</details>

Main category: hep-ph

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a phenomenological framework that explains the observed mass and decay width of the X(2370) — the clearest experimental glueball candidate — and assess other glueball candidates consistently within a constituent gluon picture grounded in lattice QCD.

Method: Assumes total decay width ∝ energy stored in the adjoint flux tube; constructs a linear mass–width relation; fits it first on light unflavoured mesons, then extrapolates to glueballs using constituent two-gluon bound-state masses from quenched lattice QCD.

Result: X(2370)'s mass (2370 MeV) and decay width are consistent with the model; f₂(2150) and f₂(2300) are identified as promising tensor glueball candidates; scalar channel candidates are reviewed but not highlighted as strongly supported.

Conclusion: The flux-tube–based linear mass–width model provides a coherent, quantitatively viable phenomenology for glueball identification across pseudoscalar and tensor channels, reinforcing X(2370) as the strongest glueball candidate and suggesting f₂(2150)/f₂(2300) as viable tensor counterparts.

Abstract: The pseudoscalar state X(2370) is the clearest glueball candidate experimentally identified so far. From a phenomenological point of view, constituent approaches, in which the lightest glueballs are modelled as bound states of two transverse constituent gluons linked by an adjoint flux tube, reach a good agreement with quenched lattice QCD pure gauge spectrum. Based on the assumption that the total decay width of glueballs or light unflavoured mesons is proportional to the energy stored in the flux tube, a model is developed that linearly correlates the hadron decay width to its mass. The model is first fitted on light unflavoured mesons and then extended to glueballs. Both the mass and decay width of the X(2370) are compatible with the results of the constituent approach. Other experimental glueball candidates in the scalar and tensor channels are also reviewed to show the ability of the developed approach to identify glueballs: The f$_2$(2150) and f$_2$(2300) states appear as promising tensor glueball candidates.

</details>


### [42] [Seesaw and Axion in No-Scale Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29801)
*Anamaria Hell,Lincoln da S. Pereira,Tsutomu T. Yanagida*

TL;DR: This paper argues that excluding the singular point (e.g., φ = 0) in the frame transformation restores classical equivalence between Jordan and Einstein frames in No-Scale gravity; the Jordan frame better reveals model symmetries, facilitating particle-physics constructions such as discrete symmetries for a high-quality axion and natural seesaw mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing debate on frame equivalence in No-Scale gravity and scalar-tensor theories, particularly addressing the role of singularities (e.g., φ = 0) in breaking classical equivalence between Jordan and Einstein frames.

Method: Analytical study of frame transformations in No-Scale gravity; identification and exclusion of the singular point φ = 0 to restore classical frame equivalence; symmetry analysis in the Jordan frame to derive discrete symmetries, axion quality, and seesaw mechanism realization.

Result: Classical equivalence between Jordan and Einstein frames is restored upon excising the singular point φ = 0; the Jordan frame exposes hidden global and discrete symmetries, enabling robust axion model building with high quality and natural incorporation of the type-I seesaw mechanism.

Conclusion: The Jordan frame—when properly defined by excluding the singular field configuration—is not only classically equivalent to the Einstein frame but also superior for manifesting symmetries essential to particle physics model building in No-Scale gravity.

Abstract: The No-Scale gravity is a compelling framework to describe particle physics, gravity and cosmology, in which all mass scales are an illusion given by the value of a scalar field $φ$. This theory, however, falls under the umbrella of more general scalar-tensor theories, which propagate different modes depending on the scalar field, and faces the extensive debate regarding the equivalence between its Jordan and Einstein frame descriptions. In this work we advocate that by excluding the singular point in the transformation between the two frames (e.g., $φ=0$) we can have the classical equivalence between them. To show the merit of removing this singular point, we argue the Jordan frame makes the symmetries of the model manifest, which creates a suitable playground for the particle-physics model building which we illustrate by showing the emergence of discrete symmetries which leads to a high-quality axion and also naturally incorporate the seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [43] [Debye screening mass in hot QCD at three loops: Canonical form of the integrand](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29844)
*York Schröder,Miguel Váez*

TL;DR: This paper revisits the calculation of the Debye screening mass in hot QCD at three-loop order, employing canonical forms of thermal integrands and integration variable shifts to streamline the reduction of complex contributions.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the systematic organization and simplification of multi-loop thermal integrals contributing to the Debye screening mass in hot QCD, especially given its central role in screening, transport, heavy-particle dynamics, and dimensional reduction.

Method: Application of canonical forms for thermal (sum-)integrands and systematic integration variable shifts to reduce and organize three-loop contributions to the Debye screening mass.

Result: A refined reduction strategy for the three-loop Debye mass is presented, demonstrating how canonical representations simplify term management and lay groundwork for higher-order calculations.

Conclusion: Canonical forms and variable shifts significantly enhance the tractability of high-order thermal QCD computations, particularly for the Debye screening mass, and provide a robust framework for future multi-loop studies.

Abstract: The Debye screening mass is a fundamental parameter characterizing the screening of chromo-electric fields in a hot quark-gluon plasma. In this contribution, we revisit the reduction strategy that serves to organize the large number of terms that contribute to the screening mass at the three-loop level, applying recent advances based on canonical forms of thermal (sum-) integrands. We emphasize the role of integration variable shifts, and discuss how canonical representations of thermal integrands may simplify future high-order calculations in hot QCD.

</details>


### [44] [Kaon gravitational form factors and mechanical structure in a three-flavor NJL model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29848)
*Zhibo Liu,Hiroaki Abuki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes kaon gravitational form factors (GFFs) in a three-flavor NJL model with proper-time regularization, preserving the gravitational Ward–Takahashi identity via dressed quark–graviton vertices and contact terms; it decomposes light/strange quark contributions, compares with pion GFFs, extracts mechanical properties (pressure, shear, mass radii), and highlights flavor-symmetry breaking effects—e.g., larger strange-quark momentum share, less negative D-term, more compact and centrally enhanced mechanical structure—and stresses the necessity of WT-identity-preserving C-term reduction for consistency with the pion low-energy theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute and interpret the gravitational form factors of the kaon—a light–strange pseudoscalar meson—in a chiral effective model that respects QCD constraints (especially energy–momentum tensor conservation via the gravitational Ward–Takahashi identity), while disentangling flavor-specific mechanical contributions and benchmarking against the pion to probe SU(3) flavor symmetry breaking.

Method: Uses a proper-time-regularized three-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model; treats the kaon as a bound state of light (u/d) and strange quarks; evaluates its energy–momentum tensor matrix element using a dressed quark–graviton vertex; includes both quark triangle diagrams and interaction-induced contact contributions to preserve the gravitational Ward–Takahashi identity; performs flavor-decomposed analysis of GFFs (A, B, C, D-terms); derives pressure and shear-force distributions via Fourier–Bessel transforms; computes Breit-frame and transverse light-front mass radii.

Result: Strange-quark contributions dominate kaon momentum and mechanical response due to explicit flavor-symmetry breaking; kaon has a less negative D-term and more compact mechanical distributions than the pion; central pressure and shear forces are enhanced; Breit-frame and transverse light-front mass radii are extracted; a WT-identity-preserving reduction scheme for the C-term is shown necessary to recover the pion low-energy theorem in this regularization.

Conclusion: The kaon’s mechanical structure is qualitatively distinct from the pion’s due to significant strange-quark involvement and SU(3) breaking; consistent treatment of the energy–momentum tensor—including contact terms and proper regularization—is essential for physical GFF predictions and low-energy theorem compliance; this framework provides a reliable, chirally symmetric basis for hadronic gravity observables beyond two-flavor systems.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the kaon in a
  proper-time-regularized three-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model. We treat the kaon as a light--strange pseudoscalar bound state and evaluate
  its energy--momentum-tensor matrix element using a dressed quark--graviton vertex. By retaining the interaction-induced contact contribution alongside the
  quark triangle diagrams, we preserve the gravitational Ward--Takahashi
  identity and ensure the conservation of the total energy--momentum tensor. We determine the light- and strange-quark contributions to the kaon GFFs,
  contrast them with the corresponding pion
  results, and extract the associated pressure and shear-force distributions,
  together with the Breit-frame and transverse light-front mass radii. Explicit flavor-symmetry breaking dictates that the strange sector
  carries a larger share of the kaon momentum and mechanical response. Consequently, compared to the pion, the kaon exhibits a less negative
  $D$-term and more compact mechanical distributions, featuring enhanced
  central pressure and shear. We further show that a Ward--Takahashi-identity-preserving reduction
  of the $C$-term projection is essential for maintaining the pion
  low-energy theorem in proper-time regularization.

</details>


### [45] [In-Medium Modification of $φ$ Meson Mass over Temperature and Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29849)
*Hidefumi Matsuda,Philipp Gubler,Koichi Hattori*

Main category: hep-ph

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the φ meson's properties—particularly its mass—are modified in a hot hadronic medium near the chiral crossover, which serves as a probe of QCD vacuum restoration and medium effects.

Method: QCD sum rules applied to the vector current correlator, decomposed into transverse and longitudinal components relative to spatial momentum in the medium rest frame; analysis performed at vanishing baryon chemical potential and below T_c.

Result: A clear transverse–longitudinal mass splitting emerges, increasing monotonically with both temperature and spatial momentum of the φ meson.

Conclusion: The polarization-dependent mass shift signals significant anisotropic medium effects on vector mesons, implying that conventional isotropic mass-shift paradigms are insufficient; such splitting may be observable in heavy-ion collisions via dilepton spectra.

Abstract: We analyze the in-medium modification of the $φ$ meson mass below the pseudo-critical temperature $T_\text{c}$ at vanishing baryon chemical potential using QCD sum rules. The sum rules are applied separately to the transverse and longitudinal polarization modes of the $φ$ meson defined relative to the spatial momentum in the medium rest frame. We map out the temperature and momentum dependence of the mass in each mode and quantify the resulting transverse--longitudinal mass splitting. The splitting develops with increasing temperature and momentum.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision of the inclusive weak radiative B → X_s γ decay, a key probe for physics beyond the Standard Model, by eliminating unphysical interpolation in charm quark mass used in prior NNLO calculations.

Method: Four-loop QCD calculation of Wilson coefficients and matrix elements with physical m_c and m_b, employing unitarity-cut techniques and multi-scale integration-by-parts reduction for hundreds of propagator-type diagrams.

Result: CP- and isospin-averaged SM prediction: B(B → X_s γ) = (3.54 ± 0.14)×10⁻⁴ for E_γ > 1.6 GeV (B rest frame), reducing theory uncertainty compared to interpolation-based NNLO estimates.

Conclusion: The first complete physical-mass NNLO QCD calculation confirms SM consistency with current experimental data, tightening constraints on new physics contributions to b → sγ transitions.

Abstract: The inclusive weak radiative $B$-meson decay provides important constraints on many popular extensions of the Standard Model. Some of the numerically relevant Next-to-Next-to-Leading-Order QCD corrections to its branching ratio have so far been evaluated using an interpolation in the charm quark mass between the $m_c=0$ and $m_c \gg m_b$ limits. In the current paper, we present their determination for the physical value of $m_c$, which required calculating hundreds of four-loop propagator diagrams with unitarity cuts and two mass scales. Our final result for the CP- and isospin-averaged branching ratio reads ${\mathcal B}_{s γ}^{\rm SM} = (3.54 \pm 0.14)\times 10^{-4}$ for $E_γ> 1.6\,{\rm GeV}$ in the decaying meson rest frame. It is in good agreement with the current Particle Data Group average ${\mathcal B}_{s γ}^{\rm exp} = (3.49 \pm 0.19)\times 10^{-4}$.

</details>


### [47] [Quantum-statistical effects of bosonic warm dark matter in microscopic interacting dark sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29947)
*Zhijian Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how quantum statistical properties of bosonic warm dark matter (BWDM), particularly its Bose–Einstein condensate (BEC) component, affect annihilation rates in a Yukawa-coupled dark-sector model; it identifies a critical condensate fraction r_c that separates thermal–thermal and condensate–condensate dominance, and constrains the dark-sector parameter space using the requirement that the interaction rate remains below the Hubble rate.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum statistics—especially BEC formation—in bosonic warm dark matter influence microscopic energy transfer and annihilation dynamics in interacting dark-sector models, beyond standard thermal equilibrium assumptions.

Method: The authors decompose the BWDM phase-space distribution into thermal and condensate components, compute thermally averaged annihilation cross sections for all pairwise channels (thermal–thermal, thermal–condensate, condensate–condensate), incorporate long-range scalar-mediated Sommerfeld enhancement, and impose the cosmological constraint that the present-day interaction rate ≤ Hubble rate to bound the parameter space.

Result: A critical condensate fraction r_c is identified, dependent primarily on BWDM mass and scalar mediator mass; above r_c, condensate–condensate annihilation dominates today; the Hubble-rate constraint yields an allowed region in the dark-sector parameter space (e.g., coupling strength vs. masses).

Conclusion: The condensate fraction acts as a key quantum-statistical control parameter for dark-sector energy transfer; neglecting BEC contributions leads to incomplete or inaccurate predictions of present-day interaction rates, necessitating inclusion of quantum degeneracy effects in modeling bosonic dark matter.

Abstract: We investigate the impact of the quantum statistical properties of bosonic warm dark matter (BWDM) on a microscopic interacting dark-sector model mediated by a Yukawa coupling. We consider a BWDM scenario containing a Bose--Einstein condensed (BEC) component. By separating the BWDM phase-space distribution into thermal and condensate components, we derive the thermally averaged annihilation cross sections for the thermal--thermal, thermal--condensate, and condensate--condensate channels. The long-range scalar interaction and its Sommerfeld enhancement are included in the annihilation processes. We find that the condensate fraction provides an additional quantum-statistical degree of freedom controlling the microscopic dark-sector energy transfer. In particular, the transition between the thermal--thermal dominated regime and the condensate--condensate dominated regime is characterized by a critical condensate fraction r_c, which is mainly determined by the BWDM mass and the dark energy scalar field mass. For condensate fractions above this critical value, the condensate--condensate channel dominates the present-day interaction rate. By imposing the condition that the present-day interaction rate does not exceed the Hubble expansion rate, we determine the corresponding region of the dark-sector parameter space satisfying this condition.

</details>


### [48] [Quantum Steering Geometry at High Energy Particle Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30037)
*Juan J. Mejia Alvarez,Andrew J. Wildridge,Angelo Arisi,Juan M. Duarte-Quiros,Santosh Bhandari,Jingyan Li,Giulia Negro,Andreas W. Jung*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces quantum steering ellipsoids (QSEs) as collider observables for spin-1/2 bipartite systems, using top-quark pair production to demonstrate how QSE geometry encodes polarization, spin correlations, entanglement, steerability, and Bell-nonlocality — enabling tomography, quantum diagnostics, and BSM sensitivity across current and future colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop geometric, information-theoretic observables—specifically quantum steering ellipsoids—that directly probe quantum properties (e.g., entanglement, steerability, nonlocality) of fundamental particles in high-energy collisions, bridging quantum information theory and collider physics for precision Standard Model tests and BSM searches.

Method: Formulates collider observables from quantum steering ellipsoids (QSEs) derived from reconstructed two-qubit spin density matrices of spin-1/2 particle pairs; applies them to tt̄ production at hadron colliders; computes QSE geometric features (volume, anisotropy, orientation); evaluates entanglement, steering, and Bell-nonlocality criteria; assesses sensitivity via expected precision metric in boosted central region; maps dimension-six EFT operator effects onto QSE deformations.

Result: QSEs encode polarization, spin correlation anisotropy, accessible-state volume, and dominant correlation axis orientation; they organize the SM spin state in tt̄ events, distinguish entanglement, steerability and Bell-nonlocality regimes, and exhibit operator-specific deformations under dimension-six BSM interactions; expected precision analysis confirms high sensitivity to nonlocal correlations in the boosted central region.

Conclusion: Quantum steering geometry provides a unified, geometric framework for collider quantum state tomography, quantum information diagnostics, and precision BSM searches—applicable across HL-LHC, lepton, muon, flavor, and electron-ion colliders.

Abstract: We formulate collider observables based on quantum steering ellipsoids (QSEs) for reconstructed bipartite systems of spin-$1/2$ particles. A collider spin density matrix defines a two-qubit state, while its QSE gives the geometry of conditional states accessible through local measurements. This makes the ellipsoid a direct probe of the quantum properties of fundamental particles, encoding polarization, spin correlation anisotropy, accessible-state volume, and the orientation of the dominant correlation axes. Using top-quark pair production as a benchmark process, we show how QSE observables organize the Standard Model spin state, probe entanglement, steerability, and Bell-nonlocality criteria, and use an expected precision metric to assess sensitivity to non-local correlations in the boosted central region. We show that different dimension-six operators generate distinctive QSE deformations, and provide a geometric interpretation of quantum information observables in high-energy particle physics. Quantum steering geometry therefore provides a unified framework for particle collider tomography, quantum information diagnostics, and precision searches for physics beyond the Standard Model with applications spanning the HL--LHC and future lepton, muon, flavor, and electron-ion collider programs.

</details>


### [49] [Building a Better Beta: Nucleation and Timescales in Cosmological Phase Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30115)
*William Searle,Csaba Balázs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new timescale, β_ν, defined as the first moment of the bubble lifetime distribution, to more fundamentally characterize first-order phase transitions in the early universe and improve predictions of the associated stochastic gravitational wave background.
