  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches use Taylor expansions of the false vacuum decay rate to characterize transition timescales, which suffer from pathologies and lack a fundamental statistical basis; the authors aim to replace them with a physically grounded, distribution-based timescale.

Method: The authors define β_ν as the first moment of the bubble lifetime distribution, analytically compare it to conventional timescales (e.g., β_H), demonstrate its consistency in limiting regimes and absence of pathologies, and compute its impact on the gravitational wave spectrum using standard envelope approximation and sound-shell models.

Result: β_ν reproduces prior timescale behavior in appropriate limits but avoids their unphysical divergences; incorporating β_ν shifts the peak amplitude of the predicted gravitational wave spectrum by up to one order of magnitude compared to standard definitions.

Conclusion: The bubble lifetime distribution provides a more natural and robust foundation for modeling cosmological first-order phase transitions; adopting β_ν significantly improves the accuracy and reliability of gravitational wave spectrum predictions, critical for interpreting upcoming observations from next-generation detectors.

Abstract: First-order phase transitions in the early universe can generate a stochastic background of gravitational waves, offering a unique probe of high-energy physics. In this work, we investigate aspects of bubble nucleation and transition timescales, which play a central role in shaping the resulting gravitational wave spectrum. Many common approaches characterise the transition rate via a Taylor expansion of the false vacuum decay rate. We argue that a more fundamental description is instead given by the distribution of bubble lifetimes, and define a new timescale, $β_ν$, as the first moment of this distribution. We show that $β_ν$ reproduces the behaviour of previous timescale definitions in the appropriate limits, while avoiding their pathologies, and offers a more natural description of the ensemble of nucleated bubbles. We then quantify the impact of this improved timescale on the predicted gravitational wave spectrum, finding that it shifts the peak amplitude by up to an order of magnitude relative to previous definitions. As next-generation gravitational wave detectors come online, robust theoretical predictions will be essential; we hope this work represents a step in that direction.

</details>


### [50] [Spin-dependent fermion potentials from mixed tensor couplings of massive spin-1 and spin-2 bosons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30240)
*D. Khadka,V. V. Flambaum*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives finite-range spin-dependent potentials mediated by massive spin-1 and spin-2 bosons, extending the standard Dobrescu–Mocioiu 16-potential basis with new mixed and higher-rank couplings (e.g., vector–tensor, axial-tensor–axial-tensor), analyzes parity/time-reversal properties, clarifies contact-term and massless limits, and translates experimental bounds into coupling constraints across mediator mass scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend the standard framework for interpreting spin-dependent force searches—particularly for exotic weak interactions beyond the Standard Model—by deriving complete, finite-range potentials from well-motivated relativistic field-theoretic mediators (spin-1 and spin-2) and mapping them onto the established 16-potential basis for direct comparison with experiments.

Method: Field-theoretic derivation of coordinate-space potentials from tree-level exchange of massive spin-1 (vector, axial-vector, tensor, pseudotensor) and spin-2 (Fierz–Pauli) bosons coupled to Dirac fermions; inclusion of contact terms for atomic-scale relevance; helicity decomposition and symmetry analysis (P, T); mapping onto the rotationally invariant, P/T-classified Dobrescu–Mocioiu V₁₋₁₆ basis; numerical translation of experimental bounds using asymptotic and finite-mass propagator forms.

Result: Complete set of finite-range spin-dependent potentials—including previously omitted mixed (e.g., vector–tensor) and rank-2 (tensor–tensor, pseudotensor–pseudotensor) spin-1 interactions, and novel axial-tensor–axial-tensor spin-2 potentials with longitudinal helicity contributions; proof that minimal spin-2 interactions cannot generate T-odd potentials; explicit mappings to the V₁₋₁₆ basis; updated numerical limits on coupling products across mediator mass regimes (contact, light, heavy, and finite mass).

Conclusion: The spin-1 and spin-2 mediator frameworks significantly enrich the space of testable spin-dependent interactions beyond the conventional subset, enabling more comprehensive reinterpretation of precision experiments (e.g., atomic, molecular, neutron) and revealing gaps in current coverage—especially for T-even, P-odd and higher-rank couplings—thus guiding future searches and effective-field-theory interpretations.

Abstract: Searches for weak spin-dependent forces are commonly interpreted in terms of a basis of sixteen rotationally invariant two-fermion potentials $V_{1-16}$, including potentials which violate parity (P) and time-reversal (T) invariance. We derive finite-range coordinate-space potentials generated by a massive spin-1 boson with vector, axial-vector, tensor, and pseudotensor couplings to Dirac fermions. In addition to the familiar vector--vector, vector--axial-vector, and axial-vector--axial-vector interactions, we obtain the mixed vector--tensor, vector--pseudotensor, axial-vector--tensor, and axial-vector--pseudotensor potentials, together with tensor--tensor, tensor--pseudotensor, and pseudotensor--pseudotensor terms. We retain the contact terms needed in atomic-scale applications and give compact expressions that include both orderings of inequivalent fermion vertices. We then extend the analysis to a massive spin-2 mediator described by a symmetric Fierz--Pauli field. Besides the conventional coupling to the conserved fermion energy--momentum tensor, we consider a symmetric axial-tensor current. Its nonconservation for massive fermions makes the longitudinal spin-2 helicities contribute to axial-tensor--axial-tensor exchange. The leading minimal spin-2 interactions are either $P,T$ even or $P$ odd and $T$ even; in particular, they do not generate a $T$-odd potential. Heavy-mediator contact limits and the distinction between the $M\to0$ massive-spin-2 limit and a genuine massless graviton are also discussed. We map the new interactions onto the sixteen-potential Dobrescu--Mocioiu basis and use this mapping to obtain numerical translations of existing limits for both spin-1 and spin-2 coupling products in the small- and large-mediator-mass regimes, together with representative limits at finite mediator masses.

</details>


### [51] [High energy thermal photons from chirally imbalanced QGP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30264)
*Sourav Duari,Nilanjan Chaudhuri,Pradip Roy,Sourav Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the thermal photon production rate from a chirally asymmetric quark-gluon plasma, combining hard scattering contributions with soft contributions via hard thermal loop (HTL) perturbation theory; it finds cut-off independence and an overall enhancement due to chiral imbalance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how chiral asymmetry affects thermal photon emission in the quark-gluon plasma—a key probe of early-stage heavy-ion collisions and chiral transport phenomena.

Method: Computes hard contribution from chiral quark scattering and combines it with previously obtained soft contribution using Braaten-Pisarski hard thermal loop (HTL) resummation; ensures cut-off independence via consistent matching of hard and soft sectors.

Result: The total thermal photon emission rate is cut-off independent; chiral imbalance enhances the overall emission rate.

Conclusion: Chiral asymmetry significantly modifies electromagnetic radiation from the QGP, and a consistent HTL-based framework successfully yields a physical, scale-invariant photon rate.

Abstract: We compute the thermal photon production rate from a chirally asymmetric quark gluon plasma. We estimate the hard part from scattering of chiral quarks and demonstrate the cut-off independence of the total thermal photon emission rate. This is achieved by combining the hard with the soft contribution evaluated in our previous work using the Braaten-Pisarski method of hard thermal loop perturbation theory. It is observed that the presence of chiral imbalance leads to an overall enhancement in the emission rate of thermal photons.

</details>


### [52] [First-principle predictions of fragmentation functions via quantum computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30375)
*Juan J. Gálvez-Viruet,Felipe J. Llanes-Estrada,Nicolas M. Arenaza,María Gómez-Rocha,T. J. Hobbs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum algorithm to compute QCD fragmentation functions from first principles using light-front quantization, validated via classical simulation (≤30 qubits) for heavy-quark → quarkonium fragmentation and benchmarked against NRQCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable ab initio computation of fragmentation functions—key nonperturbative observables for jet substructure—using quantum computers, circumventing limitations of lattice QCD and phenomenological models.

Method: Constructs a light-front Hamiltonian formulation of QCD in light-front gauge; encodes the Hamiltonian into a qubit representation suitable for quantum simulation; performs classical emulation (exact diagonalization on a memory-limited cluster) of heavy-quark fragmentation into quarkonium.

Result: Successful classical demonstration of the algorithm reproducing quarkonium fragmentation patterns consistent with NRQCD predictions; establishes feasibility and scaling roadmap for future quantum hardware.

Conclusion: Light-front QCD Hamiltonian digitization provides a viable pathway toward quantum-computed fragmentation functions; near-term quantum devices co-located with HL-LHC could enable first-principles extraction of arbitrary parton–hadron fragmentation channels.

Abstract: We report on an algorithm to compute fragmentation functions from the first principles Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) Hamiltonian quantized in Light-Front Gauge, opening a path for digital quantum computers to calculate these longitudinal jet-structure observables. Simulating the behaviour of such computers on a classical cluster (which is memory-limited to about 30 qubits, given the expansive Hilbert spaces of actual quantum computers), we run a demonstration of a heavy-quark leading parton fragmenting into quarkonium, which we benchmark against NRQCD computations. Future quantum computers, perhaps concurrently running with HL-LHC, would have ample opportunity to extract arbitrary parton-hadron combinations.

</details>


### [53] [Two-loop QCD corrections to $CP$-even Higgs boson decays into $Vγ$ in the Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30436)
*Lin-Qiao Qu,Zhong-Yuan Liu,Peng-Fei Duan,Yu Zhang,Wei-Long Duan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NLO QCD corrections to H → Vγ (V = Z, γ) decays of the heavy CP-even Higgs H in the Type-I 2HDM under the alignment limit, finding ~4× larger BR(H→γγ) than BR(H→Zγ), top-loop dominance, strong m_H dependence near tt̄ threshold, monotonic tanβ suppression, and NLO corrections ranging from −20% to +38%, with implications for HL-LHC sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the impact of QCD radiative corrections on loop-induced H → Vγ decays in the Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model, especially given their sensitivity to new physics and current experimental limits from ATLAS.

Method: Fixed-order perturbative QCD calculation at next-to-leading order (NLO) for H → γγ and H → Zγ decay widths within the Type-I THDM, performed under the alignment limit and subject to phenomenological constraints (e.g., electroweak precision, Higgs signal strengths, B-physics); 5000 viable parameter points sampled; dedicated benchmark scans over m_H and tanβ; Coulomb correction analysis near tt̄ threshold using CP selection rules.

Result: H → γγ width is ~4× larger than H → Zγ; top-quark loop dominates both channels; W-loop vanishes and charged-Higgs loop is suppressed in alignment; NLO QCD corrections range from −20% to +20% (alignment), up to +38% for H→γγ at large tanβ (non-alignment); corrections change sign and become positive for H→Zγ off-alignment; decay widths decrease with tanβ and increase with m_H; Coulomb corrections are suppressed near tt̄ threshold due to CP-driven P-wave tt̄ configuration.

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections are significant and parameter-dependent for H → Vγ decays in Type-I THDM; while current ATLAS data do not constrain the model, O(15%) NLO effects will be essential for accurate interpretation of exclusion limits at the HL-LHC.

Abstract: We investigate the QCD corrections to the loop-induced decays of the heavy $CP$-even Higgs boson, $H \to Vγ$ ($V = Z, γ$), within the framework of the Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model (THDM). Owing to the similarity of the underlying loop topologies, the two decay channels exhibit closely correlated behaviour. After imposing relevant phenomenological constraints, we evaluate the decay widths for 5000 viable parameter points at next-to-leading order (NLO) in QCD under the alignment limit. The $H\toγγ$ width exceeds that of $H\to Zγ$ by roughly a factor of four. Both are dominated by the top-quark loop: the $W$ contribution vanishes in the alignment limit, and the charged-Higgs loop is suppressed throughout most of the viable parameter region. The corrections exhibit a pronounced dependence on the heavy Higgs boson mass $m_H$, attaining a maximum in the vicinity of the $t\bar{t}$ production threshold. To elucidate the individual parameter dependences, dedicated benchmark scenarios are analysed, revealing that the decay widths diminish monotonically with increasing $\tanβ$ while growing with $m_H$. The QCD corrections for both decay modes typically span approximately $-20\%$ to $+20\%$, with larger corrections in the non-alignment scenario: in the $H\toγγ$ channel they reach up to $+38\%$ at large $\tanβ$, and in the $H\to Zγ$ channel they cross zero to become positive. The $CP$ selection rule forces the $t\bar{t}$ pair into a $P$-wave configuration at threshold, which strongly suppresses the Coulomb corrections and secures the perturbative stability of the fixed-order prediction. A comparison with ATLAS limits shows that current data do not yet constrain the parameter space, whereas the $\mathcal{O}(15\%)$ NLO corrections may become relevant for interpreting exclusion bounds at the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [54] [Exploring possible $^3_{Λ_c}\text{H}$ bound states through $pΛ_c$ femtoscopic correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30515)
*Ye Yan,Qi Huang,Qian Wu,Hongxia Huang,Jialun Ping*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the pΛc correlation function using femtoscopic techniques in heavy-ion collisions to probe the low-energy NΛc interaction and possible ^3_ΛcH bound states; it employs the quark delocalization color screening model and Koonin–Pratt formalism to show that the correlation function is sensitive to interaction strength, coupled-channel effects, and S-D wave mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the low-energy NΛc interaction and assess the existence of possible charmed hypernuclei (e.g., ^3_ΛcH) via pΛc femtoscopy in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

Method: Constructed three NΛc interaction scenarios with varying strengths using the quark delocalization color screening model; computed spin-averaged pΛc correlation functions within the Koonin–Pratt formalism, including coupled-channel and S-D wave mixing effects.

Result: The pΛc correlation function exhibits clear sensitivity to interaction strength; coupled-channel effects and S-D wave mixing produce measurable enhancements in the correlation signal.

Conclusion: Future pΛc femtoscopic measurements can provide critical constraints on charmed-baryon–nucleon interactions and guide experimental searches for heavy-flavor hypernuclei.

Abstract: The femtoscopic correlation technique in relativistic heavy-ion collisions provides a unique opportunity to investigate hadron-hadron interactions and possible exotic states. In this work, we study the $pΛ_c$ correlation function and its sensitivity to the low-energy $NΛ_c$ interaction related to possible $^3_{Λ_c}\mathrm{H}$ bound states. Based on the quark delocalization color screening model, three interaction scenarios with different strengths are constructed, and the corresponding spin-averaged $pΛ_c$ correlation functions are calculated within the Koonin--Pratt formalism. 
The results demonstrate that the correlation function is sensitive to the $pΛ_c$ interaction strength, with coupled-channel effects and $S$-$D$ wave mixing producing additional enhancements in the correlation signal. These findings suggest that future $pΛ_c$ femtoscopic measurements at relativistic heavy-ion collision experiments can provide valuable constraints on the interaction between charmed baryons and nucleons and offer guidance for exploring possible heavy-flavor hypernuclei.

</details>


### [55] [Charmonium production in p+A collisions at SPS and FAIR energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30560)
*Taesoo Song,Jiaxing Zhao,Joerg Aichelin,Elena Bratkovskaya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the PHSD transport approach with the Remler formalism to dynamically model charmonium formation and dissociation in baryon-rich nuclear matter, validates it against pp and pA data at SPS energies, extracts nuclear absorption cross sections, and predicts charmonium yields and survival probabilities at GSI/FAIR energies for QCD phase structure studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively understand charmonium production and suppression in baryon-rich hot and dense matter—relevant for mapping the QCD phase diagram at moderate temperatures and high baryon densities—as probed in current and upcoming experiments at SPS, FAIR, and NICA.

Method: Employs the Parton-Hadron-String Dynamics (PHSD) transport model coupled with the Remler formalism for dynamical c\bar{c} → charmonium formation; benchmarks against pp and pA collision data at SPS to extract effective nuclear absorption cross sections; applies the extracted cross section to heavy-ion collisions and extrapolates predictions to GSI/FAIR energies.

Result: The Remler+PHSD framework achieves quantitative agreement with charmonium production data in pp and pA collisions at SPS; yields robust predictions for charmonium yields and survival probabilities at FAIR energies, indicating significant medium-induced suppression in baryon-rich environments.

Conclusion: The Remler formalism embedded in PHSD provides a consistent dynamical framework for charmonium formation in baryon-rich matter; the extracted nuclear absorption cross section serves as a key input for interpreting heavy-ion data; results support its use in guiding future experiments targeting the high-baryon-density region of the QCD phase diagram.

Abstract: We employ the Parton-Hadron-String Dynamics (PHSD) transport approach to investigate the influence of baryon-rich matter on charmonium production and dissociation. The Remler formalism is implemented to dynamically model charmonium formation from charm-anticharm pairs. As a validation step, the formalism is first benchmarked against experimental data from elementary pp collisions and then extended to pA systems to extract the effective nuclear absorption cross section of charmonium. This extracted cross section can subsequently be applied in heavy-ion collisions to quantify medium-induced effects. Our results demonstrate that the Remler formalism provides a quantitatively consistent description of charmonium production in pp and pA collisions at SPS energies. The approach is then extrapolated to GSI/FAIR energies, where predictions for charmonium yields and survival probabilities are presented. These findings highlight the relevance of the Remler formalism as a dynamical framework for studying heavy-quark bound-state formation in baryon-rich matter and offer theoretical guidance for future experimental programs at SPS, FAIR and NICA aimed at mapping the QCD phase structure.

</details>


### [56] [Hadron interactions in the U-70 energy range: results and problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30605)
*V. A. Petrov,N. P. Tkachenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the historical significance of the Soviet Serpukhov accelerator (U-70) from 1967–early 1970s as the world's highest-energy proton synchrotron, highlighting key fundamental results in strong interaction physics during that era.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document and contextualize the scientific impact of the Serpukhov accelerator during its period of global leadership in high-energy physics, particularly in advancing understanding of strong interactions.

Method: Historical and conceptual review of experimental results and theoretical paradigms in strong interaction physics obtained using the U-70 accelerator in the late 1960s–early 1970s.

Result: Several fundamental results in strong interaction physics were achieved at U-70, including collaborative work with CERN and French institutions; these contributed to the prevailing theoretical paradigm before QCD emerged.

Conclusion: The U-70 accelerator played a pivotal role in the pre-QCD era of strong interaction physics, serving as a critical experimental platform that shaped theoretical developments in the late 1960s and early 1970s.

Abstract: From late 1967 until the early 1970s, the Soviet proton synchrotron (better known as the Serpukhov accelerator or U-70), with a nominal laboratory energy of up to 76 GeV, was the world leader. During these few years, several results of fundamental significance were obtained (partially in collaboration with physicists from CERN, France, and other countries). Let us briefly outline the main provisions of the paradigm that reigned at that time in the field of strong interaction theory.

</details>


### [57] [Small-x TMD distributions: JIMWLK evolution and the Gaussian truncation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30687)
*Florian Cougoulic,Piotr Korcyl*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the study of small-x TMD distributions by incorporating energy evolution via the JIMWLK equation, analyzing 10 TMDs in quark-gluon and gluon-gluon sectors using MV initial conditions; it assesses the validity of Gaussian truncation of the Balitsky hierarchy across evolution and links discrepancies to fixed-N_c building blocks Ω_{ag}^ω.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how energy evolution affects small-x TMD distributions and to assess the reliability of the Gaussian truncation approximation—previously successful at initial scale—under JIMWLK evolution, especially in relation to large-N_c limits and phenomenological applications.

Method: Numerical solution of the JIMWLK evolution equation for 3 quark-gluon (F_{qg}) and 7 gluon-gluon (F_{gg}) TMD distributions in position space, with initial conditions from the McLerran-Venugopalan model and fixed N_c = 3; comparison against Gaussian-truncated Balitsky hierarchy; analysis using representation-theoretic building blocks Ω_{ag}^ω.

Result: Gaussian truncation remains adequate for some TMDs under evolution but fails quantitatively in most cases; discrepancies are partially explained via the structure of Ω_{ag}^ω, revealing limitations of the truncation beyond leading order in 1/N_c.

Conclusion: The Gaussian truncation is not universally valid under JIMWLK evolution; a more complete treatment—beyond Gaussian approximation and possibly incorporating subleading-N_c effects—is required for accurate small-x TMD phenomenology.

Abstract: We extend the systematic study of small-$x$ Transverse Momentum Dependent (TMD) distributions, initiated in [Cougoulic:2026drr], by including the energy evolution. We use the JIMWLK evolution equation to study the energy dependence of three distributions in the quark-gluon sector, $\mathcal{F}_{qg}$, and seven distributions in the gluon-gluon sector, $\mathcal{F}_{gg}$. The initial conditions are provided by the McLerran-Venugopalan model. We study these TMD distributions in position space and with $N_c = 3$ fixed. We then compare these results to the Gaussian truncation of the Balitsky hierarchy. Although the Gaussian truncation worked very well for the TMD distributions at the initial condition, now we identify some cases where the Gaussian truncation provides a reasonably good description, but in the majority of cases it fails to fully reproduce numerical data. Using the fixed representation building blocks, $Ω_{ag}^ω$, we are able to partially explain the outcomes. This work constitutes the next step with the goal of understanding the interplays between the large-$N_c$ limit and Gaussian truncation as well as the BK/JIMWLK evolution equations for phenomenologically relevant observables.

</details>


### [58] [Exclusive Leptonium Electroproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30850)
*Hao-ye Deng,Qi-Ming Feng,Qi-Wei Hu,Si-Qin Huang,Cong-Feng Qiao,Jia-Xuan Shen,Ting-Ting Wang,Shun-Yan Yu,Hao Zhang,Xuan-Heng Zhang,Yi-Nan Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies exclusive electroproduction of leptonium states (e.g., dimuonium, tauonium) in electron-proton collisions using nonrelativistic QED and collinear GPD factorization; it computes NLO QCD corrections to the dominant Compton form factor ℋ, finds Bethe–Heitler dominates dimuonium production (favoring searches at EIC/JLab), while tauonium remains suppressed and positronium serves as a high-statistics QED probe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable discovery and precision study of undiscovered purely leptonic bound states—particularly dimuonium (μ⁺μ⁻) and tauonium (τ⁺τ⁻)—by predicting their exclusive electroproduction rates and QCD corrections in ep collisions.

Method: Nonrelativistic QED framework with collinear generalized parton distribution (GPD) factorization; includes Bethe–Heitler (BH), double deeply virtual Compton scattering (DDVCS), and their interference; computes NLO QCD corrections to the hard-scattering kernels for the dominant Compton form factor ℋ, distinguishing quark and gluon channels.

Result: NLO QCD correction to DDVCS changes from suppression at low photon virtuality to enhancement at higher virtuality cuts, driven dominantly by the gluon channel; BH process dominates total exclusive dimuonium cross section; positronium production yields large statistics; tauonium remains highly suppressed due to short lifetime and phase-space constraints.

Conclusion: Dimuonium is experimentally accessible via BH-dominated electroproduction at current (JLab) and future (EIC) ep colliders; tauonium discovery remains impractical with present techniques; positronium serves as a benchmark for high-precision QED tests.

Abstract: Purely leptonic bound states provide precision probes of QED. Positronium $(e^+e^-)$ and muonium $(μ^+e^-)$ have long been observed, whereas dimuonium $(μ^+μ^-)$ and tauonium $(τ^+τ^-)$ remain undiscovered. We study exclusive vector-leptonium electroproduction in $ep$ collisions within nonrelativistic QED. We include the Bethe--Heitler and double deeply virtual Compton scattering contributions and their interference, and calculate the NLO QCD hard-scattering kernels entering the dominant Compton form factor $\Hcal$ within collinear GPD factorization. The NLO QCD correction to the DDVCS contribution changes from a strong suppression at low photon virtuality to a sizable enhancement as the lower virtuality cut is raised, with the gluon channel providing the dominant contribution. Bethe--Heitler production dominates the exclusive rate, supporting dedicated dimuonium searches at the EIC and JLab, with larger samples expected at higher-energy electron--proton colliders. The much larger positronium samples provide a high-statistics environment for precision QED studies, whereas tauonium production remains strongly suppressed.

</details>


### [59] [Medium effect on spin alignment of strange and charm vector mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30930)
*Ruixiang Chen,Hiwa A. Ahmed,Yidian Chen,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a four-flavor soft-wall holographic model with anisotropic Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton background at finite T, μ_B, and angular velocity to compute spin alignment (ρ₀₀) of vector mesons (K*, φ, D*, D_s*, J/ψ) via in-medium spectral function splitting; finds flavor-dependent low-p_T behavior (ρ₀₀ > 1/3 for charm, < 1/3 for strange), insensitive to μ_B but weakly sensitive to rotation, establishing an equilibrium baseline for spin alignment studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a nonperturbative, unified theoretical description of vector-meson spin alignment in relativistic heavy-ion collisions—particularly the observed deviations from isotropic spin distribution (ρ₀₀ = 1/3)—by accounting for in-medium spin-dependent modifications across light and heavy flavors.

Method: A bottom-up four-flavor soft-wall AdS/QCD framework with an anisotropic, finite-temperature, finite-baryon-chemical-potential, rotating Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton background; spin alignment ρ₀₀ is extracted from thermal splitting between longitudinal and transverse spin-resolved vector-current spectral functions using instantaneous freeze-out; calculations cover K*, φ, D*, D_s*, and J/ψ across p_T, y, T, μ_B, and angular velocity Ω.

Result: Heavy charm vector mesons (D*, D_s*, J/ψ) show ρ₀₀ > 1/3 at low p_T, while light strange mesons (K*, φ) show ρ₀₀ < 1/3; this dichotomy arises from relative positioning of vacuum mass shell w.r.t. thermally shifted longitudinal/transverse spectral peaks; ρ₀₀ is insensitive to μ_B, weakly dependent on Ω, and qualitatively matches low-to-intermediate p_T data trends.

Conclusion: The holographic equilibrium baseline successfully captures flavor-segregated spin alignment patterns, confirming that in-medium spectral splitting is a key mechanism; deviations from this baseline in experiment would signal contributions from nonequilibrium dynamics, fluctuations, or hard production.

Abstract: Understanding the spin alignment of vector mesons in relativistic heavy-ion collisions requires a nonperturbative description of their spin-dependent in-medium properties. We investigate this problem within a unified four-flavor soft-wall holographic framework that combines an anisotropic Einstein--Maxwell--dilaton background at finite temperature, baryon chemical potential, and angular velocity. Spin alignment is determined from the medium-induced splitting of the spin-resolved vector-current spectral functions through an instantaneous freeze-out prescription. We systematically study the strange and charm vector mesons $K^{*}$, $φ$, $D^{*}$, $D_s^{*}$, and $J/ψ$ and the dependence of their spin alignment on transverse momentum, rapidity, temperature, baryon chemical potential, and angular velocity. We find that the heavy charm vector mesons $D^{*}$, $D_s^{*}$, and $J/ψ$ mesons exhibit $ρ_{00}>1/3$ at low transverse momentum, whereas the light strange vector mesons $K^{*}$ and $φ$ exhibit the opposite low-momentum behavior and angular distributions with $ρ_{00}<1/3$. We trace this flavor-dependent separation to the different locations of the vacuum mass shell relative to the thermally shifted longitudinal and transverse spectral peaks. The results qualitatively reproduce several trends observed at low and intermediate transverse momentum. Spin alignment is insensitive to baryon chemical potential and only weakly affected by angular velocity. These results establish an equilibrium holographic baseline for vector-meson spin alignment across flavor sectors and help delineate the regimes in which additional mechanisms, such as nonequilibrium evolution, fluctuations, and hard production, become important.

</details>


### [60] [Columnar Recasting and High-Luminosity LHC Projections for a Light $L_μ- L_τ$ Gauge Boson in the Four-Muon Channel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31109)
*Cesar Rendon*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper recasts LHC searches for a leptophilic Z' boson in the U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} model, using a full simulation chain (FeynRules → MadGraph → Pythia → Delphes) and modern analysis infrastructure (Coffea/Awkward/pyhf). It validates its fast-simulation sensitivity against CMS Run 2 data (77.3 fb⁻¹), identifies systematic limitations (~2.5× weaker limits), and projects HL-LHC discovery significance of 10–17σ for Z′ masses 5–28 GeV in the 4μ channel.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an independent, reproducible, and physics-transparent recasting of LHC Z′ searches in the theoretically motivated and experimentally constrained U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} model—addressing limitations of prior simplified-model interpretations and enabling robust comparison with future sensitivities.

Method: A FeynRules implementation of a chiral, leptophilic U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} extension; signal generation (MadGraph5_aMC@NLO + Pythia8 + tuned Delphes3) for 4μ final states (5–62 GeV, g = 0.01); analysis via vectorized Coffea/Awkward pipeline with boosted muon recovery; statistical inference using pyhf on digitized CMS Run 2 backgrounds (77.3 fb⁻¹); HL-LHC projections under CERN Scenario II (3000 fb⁻¹).

Result: The recast achieves expected median limits within ~2.5× of published CMS sensitivity—systematically attributed to fast-simulation detector resolution and mass-binned counting vs. experimental unbinned multivariate analysis. HL-LHC projections show 10.22–16.61σ local discovery significance for Z′ masses 5–28 GeV in the 4μ channel.

Conclusion: The analysis establishes a rigorous, conservative, and publicly verifiable sensitivity floor for U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} Z′ searches at current and future LHC runs, confirming the 4μ channel as the dominant discovery probe for light vector portals and providing a scalable framework for BSM reinterpretation.

Abstract: We present an independent recasting of light vector gauge boson ($Z'$) searches at the LHC within the leptophilic $U(1)_{L_μ- L_τ}$ framework. A simplified Standard Model extension is implemented in FeynRules, enforcing symmetric chiral leptonic currents while suppressing hadronic couplings. Signal generation for the four-muon ($4μ$) final state is performed using MadGraph5_aMC@NLO, Pythia8, and a tuned Delphes3 detector emulation, covering the mass range 5-62 GeV under a reference coupling g=0.01.
  The analysis employs a vectorized columnar pipeline in Coffea with Awkward Array masks, enabling parallelized kinematic selections and the recovery of boosted muon topologies. Signal yields are statistically extracted via pyhf against digitized CMS Run 2 backgrounds (77.3 fb$^{-1}$). The resulting expected median limit profile tracks the absolute experimental sensitivity morphology within a factor of $\approx 2.5$, a discrepancy successfully quantified as a systematic consequence of fast-simulation detector limitations and the mass-window binned counting strategy relative to experimental unbinned multivariate profiling, thereby establishing a rigorous and conservative baseline sensitivity floor.
  Furthermore, we project the discovery potential for the future High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC) phase at $\mathcal{L}_{\text{int}} = 3000$ fb$^{-1}$ under the official CERN Scenario II recommendations. We demonstrate quantitatively that the HL-LHC will achieve exceptional expected local statistical discovery significances between 10.22$σ$ and 16.61$σ$ within the 5-28 GeV mass window, comfortably clearing the discovery threshold and establishing the multi-muon channel as a primary probe for lightweight vector portals in upcoming collider phases.

</details>


### [61] [Quantum Simulation of Markovian and Non-Markovian Open Quantum Dynamics in Heavy-Ion Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31173)
*Doojin Kim,Balbeer Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum computing framework to simulate open-quantum-system dynamics—both Markovian and non-Markovian—in the context of heavy-ion collisions, using an auxiliary two-level pseudomode to encode memory effects; tracing it out recovers exact non-Markovian evolution, and the circuit smoothly reduces to the Markovian limit under appropriate time-scale hierarchy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable quantum simulation of open-quantum-system dynamics relevant to heavy-ion collisions—particularly for hard probes (jets, heavy quarks, quarkonia)—where non-Markovian memory effects are physically significant but classically intractable.

Method: Introduces an auxiliary two-level pseudomode coupled to both the subsystem and a residual Markovian bath; derives equivalence between tracing out the pseudomode and exact non-Markovian evolution governed by a prescribed memory kernel; analyzes quantum circuit construction under time-scale hierarchy to ensure convergence to Markovian limit.

Result: Demonstrates exact recovery of non-Markovian dynamics via pseudomode tracing; shows smooth circuit-based transition to Markovian limit; establishes feasibility of quantum simulation for realistic bath memory kernels.

Conclusion: Provides a unified, hardware-aware quantum computing framework for simulating both Markovian and non-Markovian open-system dynamics in high-energy nuclear physics, enabling future quantum studies of in-medium QCD probes.

Abstract: We present a quantum computing framework for simulating open-quantum-system approaches based on Markovian and non-Markovian dynamics, which is relevant to heavy-ion collisions. To simulate the non-Markovian evolution on quantum computers, we introduce an auxiliary two-level pseudomode that carries the memory forward and couples to both the subsystem and the residual Markovian bath. We explicitly show that tracing out the pseudomode reproduces the non-Markovian evolution with the exact memory kernel. Moreover, in the relevant time scale hierarchy, the quantum circuit construction of the pseudomode smoothly converges to the Markovian limit. For a given bath memory kernel, our results demonstrate the feasibility of quantum simulations of both Markovian and non-Markovian dynamics, establishing a framework for future studies of hard probes such as jets, heavy quarks, and quarkonia in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>

### [62] [A Pythia8 Tune for Open Charm and Beauty Production in Fixed-Target Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29076)
*Matei Climescu,Didar Dobur,Kirill Skovpen*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: FTFT is a Pythia8 parameter tune optimized for charm and beauty hadron production in fixed-target collisions at √s ≈ 20–42 GeV, calibrated to D-meson data and inclusive charm cross sections to improve neutrino and hidden-particle flux predictions for beam-dump experiments like SHiP.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate heavy-flavour production modeling in fixed-target collisions at √s ≈ 20–42 GeV is essential for predicting neutrino fluxes and hidden-particle yields in beam-dump experiments (e.g., SHiP), where existing LHC-tuned generators fail to describe data.

Method: Two-stage tuning: (1) fit differential distributions (xF, pT², charge/leading-particle asymmetry) of D mesons to pion- and proton-beam fixed-target data; (2) extract beam-specific K-factors from inclusive charm cross-section measurements to normalize simulated charm production.

Result: FTFT yields improved agreement with fixed-target D-meson data; tuned charm-fragmentation and multiparton-interaction parameters significantly deviate from standard LHC tunes, consistent with prior fixed-target studies.

Conclusion: FTFT provides a validated, experimentally constrained Pythia8 tune for heavy-flavour production in the fixed-target regime, enabling reliable simulation of secondary particle fluxes critical for next-generation intensity-frontier experiments.

Abstract: We present FTFT (Fixed-Target Fragmentation Tune), a set of Pythia8 parameters optimised for the production of charm and beauty hadrons in fixed-target collisions at centre-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s} \simeq 20-42$ GeV. Accurate heavy-flavour production in this regime is needed to predict neutrino fluxes and hidden-particle yields at beam-dump experiments such as SHiP. The tune is derived in two stages. First, differential distributions in Feynman-$x$ ($x_F$), transverse momentum squared ($p_T^2$), and charge or leading-particle production asymmetry of $D$ mesons are fitted to data from pion- and proton-beam fixed-target experiments. Second, $K$-factors for each beam are extracted from inclusive charm cross-section measurements to scale the simulated cross section to the measured data. The fitted charm-fragmentation and multiparton-interaction parameters depart from standard LHC-tuned defaults, in line with earlier fixed-target studies.

</details>


### [63] [Sensitivity of a Missing-Mass Search for a Light Dark Photon with a Positron Beam on a Hydrogen Target](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29159)
*Weizhi Xiong,Ashot Gasparian,Bogdan Wojtsekhowski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a Geant4-based simulation study to probe light dark photons (A') using a positron beam on a liquid-hydrogen target and a forward electromagnetic calorimeter, employing the missing-mass technique in e⁺e⁻ → γA′ annihilation-in-flight; it projects sensitivity to kinetic mixing ε² down to 4×10⁻⁸ for A′ masses 7–19 MeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ongoing R&D toward a low-energy positron facility at Jefferson Lab motivates a dedicated search for light dark photons that avoids dependence on A′ decay modes.

Method: Geant4-based Monte Carlo simulation of positron beam (500 MeV) incident on thin liquid-hydrogen target; missing-mass reconstruction in e⁺e⁻ → γA′ annihilation-in-flight; background estimation from QED annihilation and bremsstrahlung; sensitivity projection for kinetic-mixing parameter ε² vs. A′ mass.

Result: Projected sensitivity reaches ε² = 4×10⁻⁸ at m_{A′} = 19 MeV; experiment covers A′ mass range 7–19 MeV.

Conclusion: The proposed setup provides model-independent sensitivity to light dark photons in the 7–19 MeV mass range, competitive with existing constraints, leveraging high-resolution photon detection and missing-mass kinematics.

Abstract: We present a Geant4-based simulation study of the sensitivity to a light dark photon, $A'$, using a positron beam incident on a thin liquid-hydrogen target and a high-resolution forward electromagnetic calorimeter for final-state photon reconstruction. This study is motivated by ongoing research and development toward a low-energy positron facility at Jefferson Lab. The proposed search employs the missing-mass technique in the annihilation-in-flight process $e^+e^- \to γA'$, providing sensitivity to the $A'$ independently of its decay mode. We evaluate the expected backgrounds from standard QED annihilation and bremsstrahlung processes and present the projected sensitivity to the kinetic-mixing parameter $ε^2$ as a function of the $A'$ mass. The experiment with 500~MeV beam will provide the data for $A'$ mass range 7-19 MeV. The projected sensitivity on $ε^2$ is $4\times10^{-8}$ at $m_{A'}=19$~MeV.

</details>


### [64] [Evidence for the dead-cone effect in bottom quark initiated jets produced in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29412)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS measures bottom quark jet substructure to test the dead-cone effect; data strongly favor parton shower models incorporating quark mass–induced suppression of collinear gluon radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally verify the dead-cone effect—the suppression of collinear gluon emissions from massive quarks—as a key probe of quark mass effects in QCD parton showers.

Method: Differential measurement of emission density in bottom-quark jets (40–200 GeV) from tt̄ events using iterative declustering; analysis of angular separation between subjets in 13 TeV pp collision data (59.8 fb⁻¹) collected by CMS.

Result: The dead-cone–enabled parton shower model agrees with data; the model without dead-cone is excluded at 4.3σ significance.

Conclusion: First direct experimental evidence of the dead-cone effect in hadronic collisions, confirming the role of quark mass in modifying QCD radiation patterns.

Abstract: The suppression of collinear gluon emissions from a massive quark, or the ``dead-cone effect,'' is the primary manifestation of quark mass effects in parton showers. A proton-proton collision data set recorded by the CMS experiment at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV with an integrated luminosity of 59.8 fb$^{-1}$ is analyzed. A differential measurement is presented of the emission density of bottom quark jets, studied as a function of the angular separation between iteratively declustered subjets. The measurement is performed in bottom quark initiated jets with transverse momenta between 40 and 200 GeV originating from top quark pair production. Compared to data, a parton shower Monte Carlo model that includes the dead-cone effect is favored, while a model without the dead-cone effect is disfavored with a significance of 4.3 standard deviations.

</details>

Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: XENONnT achieves the lowest-energy solar neutrino detection to date via elastic neutrino-electron scattering, measuring pp neutrinos at 17–140 keV with 5.0σ significance and flux (10.2 ± 2.0) × 10¹⁰ cm⁻² s⁻¹—consistent with Borexino but higher.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend neutrino detection to the lowest possible energies using existing dark matter infrastructure, leveraging liquid xenon's low-threshold capabilities for solar neutrino spectroscopy and testing solar models.

Method: Analysis of 2.46 t·y exposure from XENONnT’s first two science runs; event selection in electron recoil energy range 1–140 keV; background modeling and statistical inference via profile likelihood to extract pp neutrino signal from elastic ν-e⁻ scattering.

Result: 5.0σ rejection of background-only hypothesis; measured pp neutrino flux = (10.2 ± 2.0) × 10¹⁰ cm⁻² s⁻¹; energy threshold lowered to 17 keV—the lowest ever for neutrino detection.

Conclusion: Demonstrates liquid xenon time projection chambers as versatile keV-scale neutrino detectors; establishes foundation for next-generation multipurpose observatories combining dark matter and neutrino physics.

Abstract: We report on the first measurement of low-energy solar neutrinos through elastic neutrino-electron scattering in a dark matter experiment, establishing the lowest energy threshold for any neutrino detection to date. The measurement utilizes data from the first two science runs of XENONnT, corresponding to an exposure of 2.46 t $\cdot$ y, and covers electron recoil energies between 1 keV and 140 keV, providing sensitivity to solar neutrinos with energies down to 17 keV. We reject the background-only hypothesis with a statistical significance of $5.0σ$ and measure a solar $pp$ neutrino flux of $(10.2 \pm 2.0) \times 10^{10}$ cm$^{-2}$ s$^{-1}$. The result is larger, but statistically consistent with the previous measurement by Borexino at $1.9σ$. Together with recent observations of coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering of $^8$B solar neutrinos in XENONnT and other liquid-xenon time projection chambers, these results demonstrate the growing potential of liquid xenon detectors for neutrino physics down to the keV-scale and represent an important milestone towards a next-generation multipurpose observatory.

</details>


### [66] [From a Long-standing Prediction to a New Family of Hadrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29502)
*Xining Wang,Kai Yi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Review of fully charmed tetraquark discoveries, emphasizing NNU/THU contributions at CMS; covers theoretical origins, experimental breakthroughs (family discovery, resonance interference, quantum number determination), and implications for QCD dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To review the evolution of fully charmed tetraquarks from theoretical prediction to experimental reality, highlighting key milestones and the role of specific university teams in advancing hadron spectroscopy and nonperturbative QCD studies.

Method: Literature review and synthesis of theoretical developments, experimental results (particularly from CMS), and analyses by NNU and THU collaborations—including resonance identification, interference studies, and quantum number determination via angular amplitude analysis.

Result: Discovery of a full family of fully charmed tetraquarks (not just isolated states), experimental confirmation of resonance interference, first unambiguous determination of quantum numbers (e.g., J^PC = 0^++ or 2^++), and establishment of these states as a new class of hadrons.

Conclusion: Fully charmed tetraquarks now constitute a well-established hadronic family; their systematic study provides a unique laboratory for probing strong-interaction dynamics beyond the quark model and testing lattice QCD and effective field theories.

Abstract: Fully charmed tetraquarks have evolved from a long-standing theoretical prediction into one of the most active frontiers in hadron spectroscopy. This article reviews their development from early theoretical studies and experimental searches to the recent breakthroughs at the Large Hadron Collider, with particular emphasis on the contributions of the Nanjing Normal University (NNU) and Tsinghua University (THU) teams at the CMS experiment. We summarize the discovery of the fully charmed tetraquark family, the establishment of interference among resonances, and the first determination of their quantum numbers. These advances have transformed the search for a single exotic resonance into the study of a new family of hadrons and opened a promising avenue for exploring the nonperturbative dynamics of Quantum Chromodynamics.

</details>


### [67] [Low-energy Muon-Nucleon scattering experiment: LUNE (White Paper)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29509)
*Chenlei An,Dong Bai,Ziyu Bai,Kai Chen,Liangwen Chen,Xiang Chen,Jianqiao Deng,Yanxin Dou,Yicheng Feng,Zekai Feng,Lu Gao,Chang Gong,Aiqiang Guo,Liang Han,Qundong Han,Defu Hou,Ruiwen Hou,Huigang Hu,Chen Ji,Xiangdong Ji,Vijay Kumar,Dikai Li,Jiuzhao Li,Liang Li,Qite Li,Xin-Qiang Li,Yuan Li,Qiming Liang,Yutie Liang,Dong Liu,Duanqing Liu,Langtian Liu,Weijie Liu,Zejia Lu,Maowu Nie,Ziwen Pan,Hua Pei,Jinkang Peng,Shusu Shi,Qintao Song,Xiaocheng Song,Xiangming Sun,Yichen Sun,Zhiyu Sun,Enke Wang,Fei Wang,Hulin Wang,Jike Wang,Xiang-Peng Wang,Xiao Wang,Xin-Nian Wang,Yangxi Wang,Yaping Wang,Zeren Simon Wang,Yanbing Wei,Yuehong Xie,Weizhi Xiong,Nu Xu,Yu Xu,Siqi Yang,Yadong Yang,Hang Yin,Xing-Bo Yuan,Dongliang Zhang,Ranyu Zhang,Ruitian Zhang,Xueheng Zhang,Yu Zhang,Yuxiang Zhao,Zihan Zhao,Shuai Zhou,XiaoKang Zhou,Xiaoyu Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The LUNE Collaboration proposes precision muon scattering experiments at the High Intensity heavy-ion Accelerator Facility (HIAF) to address fundamental questions in nuclear and particle physics, including the proton charge radius puzzle, nucleon electromagnetic structure, QED tests via two-photon exchange, and beyond-Standard-Model searches—leveraging high-intensity, broad-momentum (0.5–7.5 GeV/c), charge-symmetric (μ⁺/μ⁻) muon beams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolve the proton charge radius puzzle, precisely map nucleon electromagnetic form factors, test QED (especially two-photon exchange), probe 3D nucleon/nuclear structure (TMDs, gravitational form factors), quantify nuclear medium and Coulomb distortion effects, and search for BSM physics.

Method: Elastic and inelastic muon-proton/nucleus scattering using high-intensity, momentum-tunable (0.5–7.5 GeV/c), charge-symmetric muon beams from HIAF; systematic comparison of μ⁺ and μ⁻ data to isolate two-photon exchange and hadronic effects; dedicated detector system optimized for high-resolution momentum and vertex reconstruction.

Result: Projected ~1.0% precision on proton charge radius via elastic μp scattering; high-precision, kinematically complete measurements of proton electromagnetic form factors (GE, GM) for both charges; first systematic access to TMDs and gravitational form factors with muons; constraints on Coulomb distortions, nuclear medium effects, and BSM couplings.

Conclusion: LUNE establishes muon scattering at HIAF as a unique, complementary probe to electron facilities and future lepton-ion colliders—enabling unprecedented tests of QED, nucleon structure, and fundamental symmetries across multiple physics domains.

Abstract: The HIAF will provide high-intensity, high-quality muon beams with momenta from 0.5 to 7.5 GeV/c. This energy range is uniquely suited for precision muon scattering, bridging the gap between low-energy electron facilities and future high-energy lepton-ion colliders. In particular, HIAF will enable precision measurements with both positive and negative muon beams over a broad kinematic range, complementing existing electron-scattering facilities such as JLab, EicC and EIC.
  Based on HIAF muon source, the LUNE Collaboration has been established to address several fundamental questions in nuclear and particle physics, including the proton charge radius puzzle, nucleon electromagnetic structure, and the dynamics of quantum electrodynamics and hadronic interactions. The program proceeds in two phases, from elastic scattering to nucleon structure and beyond-Standard-Model searches.
  The experiment is expected to determine the proton charge radius with a precision of approximately 1.0\% using elastic muon-proton scattering. It will also perform systematic measurements of the proton electromagnetic form factors with both $μ^+$ and $μ^-$ beams, enabling precise studies of two-photon exchange effects and stringent tests of quantum electrodynamics. Beyond elastic scattering, LUNE will investigate TMD, gravitational form factors, and nuclear charge radii, providing new insights into the 3D structure of nucleons and nuclei. The experiment will further address important topics including Coulomb-distortion corrections, nuclear medium effects, and possible signatures of physics beyond the Standard Model.
  This white paper presents the scientific motivation, detector concept, expected performance, and long-term strategy of LUNE.

</details>

Van Rootselaar,J. van Santen,J. Vara,F. Varsi,M. Velazquez,M. Venugopal,M. Vereecken,S. Vergara Carrasco,S. Verpoest,A. Vijai,J. Villarreal,C. Walck,A. Wang,E. H. S. Warrick,C. Weaver,P. Weigel,A. Weindl,J. Weldert,A. Y. Wen,C. Wendt,J. Werthebach,M. Weyrauch,N. Whitehorn,C. H. Wiebusch,D. R. Williams,L. Witthaus,J. Woodward,G. Wrede,X. W. Xu,J. P. Yanez,Y. Yao,E. Yildizci,S. Yoshida,F. Yu,S. Yu,T. Yuan,S. Yun-C{á}rcamo,A. Zander Jurowitzki,A. Zegarelli,S. Zhang,Z. Zhang,P. Zhelnin,P. Zilberman,C. Zilleruelo Ca{ñ}as*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper uses 10.7 years of IceCube upward-going muon neutrino data (0.5–100 TeV) to constrain low-scale gravity models—specifically large extra dimensions and multiple Standard Model copies—by analyzing Earth-induced spectral distortions in the neutrino flux.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the hierarchy problem via low-scale gravity models that predict observable neutrino spectral distortions due to matter effects during Earth propagation, and to set competitive or novel constraints using high-energy astrophysical neutrinos.

Method: Analysis of IceCube’s 10.7-year upward-going muon neutrino sample (0.5–100 TeV); modeling of neutrino oscillations and matter effects in Earth for low-scale gravity scenarios—including large extra dimensions (LED) and multiple SM copies—followed by likelihood-based statistical inference to derive confidence-level limits.

Result: For large extra dimensions: R ≲ 0.17 μm at 90% CL (both mass orderings); for multiple SM copies: N ≳ O(400), depending on lightest neutrino mass; some limits are strongest to date, others probe unexplored parameter space.

Conclusion: High-energy astrophysical neutrinos from IceCube provide a powerful probe of quantum gravity and beyond-Standard-Model physics; this analysis delivers state-of-the-art or novel constraints on low-scale gravity frameworks tied to the hierarchy problem.

Abstract: The hierarchy problem remains an open question in particle physics. A number of theories that address this problem lower the fundamental scale of gravity, resulting in observable consequences in the neutrino sector. In this work, we place constraints on low-scale gravity scenarios using high-energy neutrinos observed with the IceCube Neutrino Observatory. The analysis is based on 10.7 years of upward-going muon neutrino data in the energy range from 0.5 to 100 TeV. In this energy range, the theories predict characteristic spectral distortions arising from matter effects when neutrinos propagate through Earth. In the context of large extra dimension models, we constrain the compactification radius of the largest extra dimension to $R \lesssim 0.17\,μ\mathrm{m}$ at $90\%$ confidence level for both normal and inverted neutrino mass ordering. For scenarios with multiple Standard Model copies, we obtain lower limits of up to $N \gtrsim \mathcal{O}(400)$, depending on the value of the lightest neutrino mass. In parts of the parameter space, these results constitute the strongest constraints in the literature to our knowledge, while in other regions they probe previously unexplored parameter space.

</details>


### [69] [ConCA: Concentration-Aware Channel Attention for Fine-Grained Visual Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30183)
*Yu-Sheng Liu,Yu-Chen Tung*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: ConCA introduces a concentration-aware channel attention mechanism for fine-grained visual recognition by augmenting global average pooling with a shift-invariant negative-input entropy (NegEnt) to jointly encode activation magnitude and spatial concentration; it uses a lightweight depthwise 1D-convolutional MLP to generate per-channel weights and outperforms existing lightweight attention modules across multiple FGVR benchmarks and backbones.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing lightweight channel attention mechanisms (e.g., SE, ECA) rely solely on global average pooling (GAP), which captures only activation magnitude and fails to distinguish channels with identical means but different spatial concentration — a critical limitation in fine-grained visual recognition where subtle spatial patterns matter.

Method: ConCA constructs a dual-channel descriptor per feature map: (1) global average pooling (magnitude) and (2) shift-invariant negative-input entropy (NegEnt), computed via softmax over negated activations to measure spatial concentration. A depthwise 1-D convolutional MLP—parameter count linear in channel number—maps this pair to per-channel attention weights.

Result: ConCA consistently outperforms attention-free baselines, SE-Net, ECA-Net, and four richer descriptor-based modules on six FGVR benchmarks under a controlled from-scratch training protocol; it also generalizes across eight backbone architectures on iNat2021-mini.

Conclusion: The design of the channel descriptor (beyond just mean pooling) and its mapping to attention weights are pivotal yet underexplored dimensions for lightweight channel attention in FGVR; ConCA demonstrates that explicitly modeling spatial concentration significantly boosts discriminative power.

Abstract: Lightweight channel attention mechanisms are widely used in image classification, yet their effectiveness in fine-grained visual recognition (FGVR) remains limited. Most modules summarize each channel by global average pooling (GAP), which captures activation magnitude but ignores spatial concentration, so channels with different spatial distributions but identical means receive the same descriptor. We propose Concentration-Aware Channel Attention (ConCA), which pairs the mean with a shift-invariant negative-input entropy (NegEnt), computed via a softmax over the negated activations, forming a dual descriptor that jointly encodes magnitude and concentration. A depthwise 1-D convolutional multi-layer perceptron (MLP), whose parameter count is linear in the number of channels, maps the pair to a per-channel weight. On six fine-grained benchmarks, ConCA improves over attention-free, SE-Net, and ECA-Net baselines as well as four richer descriptor-based modules under a controlled from-scratch protocol, and it generalizes across eight backbones on iNat2021-mini. These results indicate that the channel descriptor, together with the per-channel gating that maps it to attention weights, is an important but underexplored aspect of lightweight channel attention in FGVR.

</details>

Girgus,P. Govindaraj,M. Posiadala-Zezula,Y. S. Prabhu,S. B. Boyd,R. Edwards,D. Hadley,M. O'Flaherty,B. Richards,A. Ali,B. Jamieson,C. Bronner,D. Horiguchi,A. Minamino,Y. Sasaki*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a search for proton decay into a positively charged lepton and an invisible neutral particle using Super-Kamiokande's pure water phase data (401 kton·years exposure); no signal was found, leading to improved 90% CL lower limits on partial lifetimes: 1.72×10³³ yr (p→e⁺X) and 0.61×10³³ yr (p→μ⁺X).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test grand unified theories (GUTs) predicting proton decay, specifically the channel p→l⁺X where X is a light or massless neutral particle (e.g., majoron or dark sector mediator), which evades conventional searches relying on visible hadronic final states.

Method: Analysis of fully-contained, single-ring positron-like events in Super-Kamiokande’s pure water phase; background estimation dominated by atmospheric neutrino interactions (especially νₑ and νₘ charged-current quasielastic scattering); event selection optimized for high electron/positron identification efficiency and suppression of muon misidentification and neutron-induced backgrounds; lifetime limits derived via profile likelihood ratio method.

Result: No statistically significant excess over atmospheric neutrino background; 90% confidence level lower limits on partial lifetimes: τ(p→e⁺X) > 1.72×10³³ yr and τ(p→μ⁺X) > 0.61×10³³ yr — representing improvements by factors of ~2 and ~1.5 over prior best limits.

Conclusion: The absence of observed proton decay in these channels constrains models with baryon-number-violating interactions coupled to light neutral scalars or fermions; results place stringent bounds on majoron-emitting or dark-sector-mediated proton decay scenarios, narrowing viable parameter space in beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Abstract: A search for proton decay via $p\rightarrow l^{+}+X$, where $l^{+}$ is a positively charged lepton and $X$ is an invisible, massless, neutral particle, was performed using a 401~kton$\cdot$years exposure representing the entire pure water phase of Super-Kamiokande. No significant indication of a proton decay was observed beyond the expected atmospheric neutrino background. Lower limits on the partial lifetime of the proton were set to at $1.72\times10^{33}$ years for $p\rightarrow e^{+}+X$ and $0.61\times10^{33}$ years for $p\rightarrow μ^{+}+X$ at the $90\%$ confidence level. These results improve on previous limits by factors of 2 and 1.5, respectively.

</details>

Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,T. Zies,D. Zuliani,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper measures the ψ(2S)-to-J/ψ production cross-section ratio in pAr and pp collisions at √s_NN = 113 GeV using LHCb's SMOG2 data, finding a ratio of 0.90 ± 0.04 ± 0.02 in a specific kinematic region — indicating nuclear suppression in pAr — and establishing a baseline for future heavy-ion studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify nuclear effects on charmonium production by comparing ψ(2S)/J/ψ ratios in proton-argon (pAr) versus proton-proton (pp) collisions, serving as a reference for interpreting results in larger nuclear systems.

Method: Fixed-target measurements with LHCb during SMOG2 argon and hydrogen gas injections; differential measurement of the ψ(2S)-to-J/ψ cross-section ratio as a function of transverse momentum (p_T) and centre-of-mass rapidity (y*); statistical and systematic uncertainty evaluation.

Result: The ψ(2S)/J/ψ ratio in pAr relative to pp is 0.90 ± 0.04 (stat) ± 0.02 (syst) for −2.3 < y* < 0.0 and 0 < p_T < 8 GeV/c, showing ~10% suppression consistent with nuclear effects such as initial-state energy loss or final-state dissociation.

Conclusion: Nuclear effects are observed in pAr collisions via suppression of ψ(2S) relative to J/ψ, confirming the sensitivity of this observable to cold nuclear matter; the result provides a crucial baseline for disentangling cold vs. hot nuclear matter effects in future LHCb heavy-ion programs.

Abstract: A measurement of the $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ production cross-section ratio is performed in proton-argon ($p\mathrm{Ar}$) and proton-proton ($pp$) collisions in fixed-target mode at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=113\,\mathrm{GeV}$. Data samples were collected by the LHCb experiment during argon and hydrogen gas injections in the SMOG2 storage cell, resulting in $p\mathrm{Ar}$ and $pp$ collisions, respectively. The $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ production cross-section ratio is measured as a function of the charmonium transverse momentum, $p_{\mathrm{T}}$, and rapidity in the centre-of-mass system, $y^{*}$. The $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ ratio in $p\mathrm{Ar}$ collisions over that in $pp$ collisions is measured to be $0.90 \pm 0.04 \pm 0.02$ for $-2.3<y^{*}<0.0$ and $0<p_{\mathrm{T}}<8\mathrm{GeV}/c$, indicating the emergence of nuclear effects in the $p\mathrm{Ar}$ system. This study acts as a baseline for the interpretation of future measurements with larger systems accessible by the LHCb experiment.

</details>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [72] [Inspiralling Binary merger parameter reconstruction with lunar and satellite Laser Ranging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28753)
*Miguel Vanvlasselaer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates using Earth-Moon and Earth-satellite ranging (LLR and GUEST) to detect gravitational waves from massive black hole binaries, focusing on reconstructing chirp mass (Mc) and luminosity distance (DL). It finds Mc recoverable with single-baseline ranging (e.g., LLR), but DL suffers from sky-angle degeneracy in LLR; only multi-baseline (GUEST) breaks this degeneracy and enables SNR-dependent DL recovery.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore low-frequency gravitational wave detection via precision ranging—complementary to space-based interferometers—and assess feasibility of parameter estimation (especially chirp mass and luminosity distance) from synthetic range data.

Method: Develops a reconstruction framework assuming ideal Keplerian Earth-satellite motion; models perturbations induced by chirping massive black hole binaries on ranging observables; evaluates parameter recovery performance (uncertainties, biases, SNR scaling) for Lunar Laser Ranging (LLR) and two-satellite GUEST configuration.

Result: Chirp mass uncertainty scales as σ_Mc / Mc ∝ SNR^α with α ≈ −1 to −1.8 for both LLR and GUEST; luminosity distance recovery fails in LLR due to severe, SNR-independent degeneracy with sky-orientation angles (fractional uncertainties >100%), while GUEST breaks the degeneracy and restores convergent, SNR-dependent DL recovery comparable to Mc.

Conclusion: Multi-baseline ranging architectures (e.g., GUEST) are essential for extracting luminosity-distance information in ranging-based GW searches; chirp-mass estimation remains robust even with single-baseline systems like Earth–Moon (LLR).

Abstract: Precision tracking of Earth--Moon and Earth--satellite ranges offers a novel, low-frequency avenue for detecting gravitational waves from inspiralling massive black hole binaries, complementary to space-based interferometers. In this paper, we propose a first study of the reconstruction of the binary parameters from a synthetic set of range observations. \textit{Set-up}: Assuming an ideal unperturbed Keplerian motion for the earth-satellite binary, we develop a framework for reconstructing the chirp mass $\mathcal M_c$ and luminosity distance $D_L$ of chirping sources from the perturbations they induce on ranging observables, using both Lunar Laser Ranging (LLR) and a two-satellite ranging configuration (GUEST) as concrete case studies.
  \textit{Result}: We find that chirp-mass recovery tightly converges as a power law in SNR ($σ_{M_c}/M_c \propto \mathrm{SNR}^α$, $α\simeq -1$ to $-1.8$) for both LLR and GUEST. In contrast, the luminosity distance suffers from a severe, SNR-independent degeneracy with the sky-orientation angles in the LLR configuration, yielding fractional biases and uncertainties of tens to over a hundred percent regardless of signal strength. The two-satellite GUEST configuration breaks this degeneracy and restores a convergent, SNR-dependent recovery of $D_L$ comparable in scaling to that of the chirp mass. These results demonstrate that multi-baseline ranging architectures are essential for extracting luminosity-distance information from ranging-based gravitational wave searches, while chirp-mass measurements remain reliably accessible even with a single ranging baseline such as the Earth--Moon system.

</details>


### [73] [A Quasi-local Entropy for Black Hole Space-times](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28755)
*Raymond Isichei*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that the angular components of the Brown-York tensor on a finite-radius boundary around static, spherically symmetric black holes encode a quasi-local entropy, which matches the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy at the horizon and monotonically increases inward—thereby verifying the covariant entropy conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide explicit, quasi-local thermodynamic interpretation of gravitational boundary data (Brown-York tensor) and test the covariant entropy conjecture in a concrete black hole setting.

Method: Analysis of the Brown-York stress-energy tensor on timelike boundaries of static, spherically symmetric black hole spacetimes; identification of angular components with Tolman-Ehrenfest temperature times a derived quasi-local entropy; examination of its radial behavior from infinity to the horizon.

Result: A well-defined, monotonic quasi-local entropy is constructed from the Brown-York tensor’s angular part; it vanishes at spatial infinity, increases inward, and equals the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy exactly at the horizon.

Conclusion: The derived entropy satisfies all key properties required by the covariant entropy conjecture, offering direct geometric and thermodynamic support for its validity in static black hole geometries.

Abstract: We show that for static & spherically symmetric black hole geometries, the angular components of the Brown-York tensor on a finite radius boundary can be interpreted as the product of the Tolman-Ehrenfest temperature and a quasi-local entropy. This entropy vanishes at spatial infinity and monotonically increases as radius decreases, taking its maximum value on the horizon where it is equal to the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy. Thus, the behaviour of this entropy provides explicit verification of the covariant entropy conjecture.

</details>


### [74] [Acceleration and Rotation in Three Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28789)
*Cameron R D Bunney,Ruth Gregory,Robert B Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new family of exact solutions in 3D Einstein gravity describing accelerating, rotating point particles and black holes, analyzes their geometry and AdS₃ embedding, and positions them as tools for studying thermodynamics, asymptotic symmetries, and holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known catalogue of exact solutions in three-dimensional Einstein gravity and provide physically and mathematically tractable models for investigating black hole thermodynamics, asymptotic symmetries, and holographic dualities.

Method: Construction and analysis of exact solutions to the three-dimensional Einstein equations with negative cosmological constant; geometric characterization (e.g., curvature, causal structure, horizons); study of global isometric embedding into three-dimensional anti-de Sitter space (AdS₃).

Result: A novel family of exact solutions representing accelerating and rotating point particles and black holes in 3D Einstein–AdS gravity; full characterization of their geometric structure and explicit embedding into AdS₃.

Conclusion: These solutions enrich the landscape of integrable and controllable models in lower-dimensional gravity, enabling rigorous exploration of quantum gravity phenomena such as entropy, symmetry algebras (e.g., Virasoro), and AdS₃/CFT₂ correspondence.

Abstract: We present a new family of exact solutions representing accelerating, rotating, point particle and black hole solutions in three-dimensional Einstein gravity. We investigate their geometric properties and the global embedding in three dimensional anti-de Sitter space. Our results extend the catalogue of exact solutions in three-dimensional Einstein gravity and provide a useful setting for the study of black hole thermodynamics, asymptotic symmetries, and holography.

</details>


### [75] [Torsion and Nonmetricity Constraints From Neutron Spin Rotation in Polarized Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28832)
*Sepehr Samiei,Ralf Lehnert,W. Michael Snow*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory framework to characterize couplings of electrons and neutrons to spacetime torsion and nonmetricity up to mass dimension six, predicts new neutron-polarization effects in electron backgrounds, and sets first experimental constraints on axially symmetric, direction-dependent in-matter torsion and nonmetricity using neutron spin-rotation data in a polarized-electron ferrimagnet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically probe non-Riemannian geometric degrees of freedom (torsion and nonmetricity) in matter—beyond standard Einstein-Cartan theory—via low-energy precision experiments sensitive to Lorentz- and CPT-violating signatures.

Method: Effective field theory construction of minimal, gauge-invariant, dimension ≤6 operators coupling fermionic (electron, neutron) fields to torsion and nonmetricity; theoretical prediction of neutron spin precession signals induced by electron-background-induced torsion/nonmetricity; reinterpretation of existing neutron spin-rotation measurements in a compensated ferrimagnet with polarized electrons to extract bounds.

Result: First experimental constraints on direction-dependent, axially symmetric combinations of in-matter torsion and nonmetricity; derived quantitative upper limits on corresponding EFT coefficients from spin-rotation null results.

Conclusion: Nonmetricity and torsion in matter generate observable, distinctive spin-coupling signatures; precision neutron experiments in polarized-electron media provide a viable, previously unexploited channel for testing metric-affine gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: Many modified-gravity frameworks employ non-Riemannian connection degrees of freedom, including torsion and nonmetricity. In Einstein-Cartan-type theories, torsion is associated with spin density, while more general metric-affine frameworks can be investigated through phenomenological searches for in-matter torsion and nonmetricity backgrounds. This work applies an effective field theory approach to characterize general couplings of electron and neutron operators to spacetime torsion and nonmetricity up to mass dimension six, predicting novel neutron-polarization signatures in the presence of an electron background. Neutron spin-rotation data from a polarized-electron ensemble inside a compensated ferrimagnet are used to constrain for the first time axially symmetric, direction-dependent in-matter torsion and nonmetricity combinations.

</details>


### [76] [Stationary Rotating Geometries Associated with Nonsingular Pulsating Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28850)
*A. D. Souza,F. A. Brito,J. Spinelly*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a rotating black hole solution by extending a singularity-avoiding static geometry (inspired by LQC/braneworld models) via the Newman–Janis algorithm; it recovers Kerr/Schwarzschild limits as quantum corrections vanish, and performs extended thermodynamic analysis including Davies points and state-space geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model quantum-gravitational or braneworld effects that resolve the central singularity in black holes, while preserving realistic astrophysical features like rotation and thermodynamics.

Method: Starts from a static, singularity-avoiding exterior geometry (Gao–Lu–Shen–Faraoni), couples it to a comoving bouncing interior, then applies the Azreg-Aïnou variant of the Newman–Janis algorithm to generate a stationary, axisymmetric rotating extension; subsequently analyzes horizon structure, surface gravity, Hawking temperature, and extended thermodynamics including Davies singularities and state-space geometry.

Result: A novel rotating black hole metric that is regular at r=0, asymptotically reduces to Kerr for l→0, exhibits well-defined horizon geometry, angular velocity, surface gravity, and Hawking temperature, and supports an extended thermodynamic description with identifiable Davies-type phase-transition singularities.

Conclusion: The constructed solution provides a consistent, rotating, singularity-free black hole model compatible with effective high-density corrections from quantum gravity or extra dimensions; its thermodynamic structure reveals critical behavior analogous to phase transitions, supporting the viability of extended thermodynamic frameworks for such exotic compact objects.

Abstract: Since the first exact solutions of General Relativity (GR) were obtained, it became clear that the theory predicts a new class of compact objects: black holes. The Hawking--Penrose singularity theorems show that, under appropriate assumptions, gravitational collapse in GR leads to singular behavior. Motivated by the expectation that such regimes require physics beyond classical GR, effective approaches inspired by Loop Quantum Cosmology (LQC) and braneworld scenarios introduce high-density corrections. In this work we consider the static exterior geometry introduced by Gao, Lu, Shen, and Faraoni, associated with a comoving interior model in which the singularity is avoided through a sequence of collapse and bounce phases. Using the Newman--Janis algorithm in the Azreg-Aïnou formulation, we construct a stationary rotating exterior extension of this geometry.
