The resulting line element describes a stationary axisymmetric rotating spacetime whose horizon geometry, angular velocity, surface gravity, and Hawking temperature recover the Kerr and Schwarzschild limits when the quantum/braneworld correction becomes negligible ($l\to 0$). We further analyze the thermal response at fixed rotational state-space parameter, identify Davies-type singular points, and formulate an extended thermodynamic state-space relation.

</details>


### [77] [Spin-Graviton-Spin: a unique probe of quantum gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28901)
*Radmehr Fathi,Nima Khosravi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a unique low-energy signature of quantum gravity—specifically, a spin–momentum–dependent dipole-dipole interaction between two spin-1/2 particles mediated by a spin-2 graviton, scaling as 1/r³—distinguishable from spin-0 or spin-1 mediators; confirmed via both direct calculation and Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation, and proposed to be probed via four-partite entanglement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify an experimentally distinguishable low-energy signature uniquely attributable to quantum gravity—specifically, the spin-2 nature of the graviton—rather than environmental or other force-mediated effects.

Method: Analytical quantum field-theoretic calculation of the graviton-mediated interaction between two spin-1/2 particles; verification using the Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation to obtain the effective nonrelativistic Hamiltonian; analysis of spin-momentum coupling structure and comparison with spin-0/spin-1 mediators.

Result: A unique 1/r³ dipole-dipole interaction emerges that simultaneously couples both particles' spins and momenta—a feature exclusive to spin-2 exchange at leading order; spin-0 and spin-1 exchanges lack this simultaneous spin–momentum intertwining at the same order.

Conclusion: The spin-2–induced spin–momentum entanglement provides a clean, low-energy observational signature of quantum gravity; its detection requires a four-partite entanglement protocol to isolate and verify the effect unambiguously.

Abstract: The existence of quantum gravity is the most important question in theoretical physics. Besides the theoretical development in this direction, looking for its low-energy consequences can provide clues. However, besides availability, it is crucial to ensure that what is observed uniquely comes from quantum gravity and not from other forces in the environment. What is the unique feature of gravity? The spin-$2$ nature of its propagator. In this Letter, we calculate what the unique feature of the spin-$2$ graviton is in the interaction of two spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ particles. This unique feature shows itself in intertwining the spin and momentum of two particles simultaneously as a dipole-dipole interaction. This cannot happen for any spin-$0$ or spin-$1$ propagator, at least at leading order, which scales as $\frac{1}{r^3}$ in real space. We re-calculate the results using an independent approach, i.e., the Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation, and reach the same results. Due to simultaneous coupling of both particles' spins and momenta, a four-partite entanglement framework is suggested to observe this effect.

</details>


### [78] [Schwarzschild spectral ladders on the negative imaginary axis: Endpoint nonselection, branch-cut phase, and Jost classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29095)
*Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh,Mark Essa Sukaiti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper investigates spectral discretisations for axial Schwarzschild perturbations and shows that stable negative-imaginary-axis (NIA) eigenvalue ladders—though numerically reproducible—do not correspond to true quasinormal modes (QNMs), as they fail the invariant Jost/Evans pole criterion; instead, they are artefacts tied to endpoint regularity choices (C¹ vs C²), branch-cut structure, and representation-dependent discretisation, with implications for convergence to the physical cut response and Price tail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether numerically stable NIA eigenvalue ladders from compactified spectral methods represent genuine quasinormal poles or spurious artefacts, particularly in light of endpoint regularity constraints, factorisation ambiguities, and failure of standard convergence criteria in the Schwarzschild black hole context.

Method: Analytical ingoing–outgoing factorisation of axial perturbation equations; exact Chebyshev-grid root-radius analysis; arbitrary-precision matrix pencil computations (C¹ and C² mappings); Jost determinant evaluation (lateral, on-cut, argument-principle winding); comparison with exact QNM benchmarks and Casals–Ottewill branch-cut phase predictions; finite-frequency branch-strength zero search.

Result: A reproducible 68-point C¹ eigenvalue ladder is shown to be non-physical: all candidates fail the Jost/Evans pole test (zero winding, no zeros on cut); same-damping controls recover true QNMs (n=60,100,130) with tiny |𝒟̂| (~10⁻⁴⁸–10⁻⁵⁶); the ladder aligns with surface-gravity quarter spacing and Casals–Ottewill cut-phase, and pairs with QNM damping projections (n=62–129); a branch-strength zero at α_q ≈ 15.078 supports—but does not prove—cut-phase locking.

Conclusion: Stable finite-matrix NIA eigenvalues can be representation-dependent artefacts rather than quasinormal poles; C^k regularity is insufficiently selective for pole identification when 4α > k; accurate spectral discretisations may converge to cut-related structure but not to isolated poles; the question of whether Keldysh-weighted node sequences converge collectively to the physical cut response and Price tail remains open.

Abstract: Compactified spectral discretisations may yield stable negative-imaginary-axis (NIA) eigenvalue ladders, but finite-matrix convergence does not establish quasinormal poles. For axial Schwarzschild perturbations, ingoing--outgoing factorisation leaves an unwanted horizon solution behaving as $t^{4α}$ at $Ω=-iα$, and a $C^\infty$-flat unwanted infinity solution. Thus endpoint $C^k$ regularity is nonselective for $4α>k$, so the $C^2$ continuum problem cannot have a discrete NIA spectrum over $α\simeq15$--32. Exact Chebyshev-grid formulas give roots-grid maximum radius $O(n^{2p})$ for $x\sim C(1-y)^{-p}$, explaining C1, $x=2/(1-y)$, versus C2, $x=4/(1-y)^2$. Arbitrary-precision pencils nevertheless reveal a reproducible 68-point C1 ladder, while C2 reorganises it. Both physical lateral Jost determinants remain stably nonzero at every C1 frequency. A 0.05-spaced on-cut scan finds no zero, and refined argument-principle calculations give zero winding in both continuation strips. This rejects all 68 candidates, although the mesh and strip evidence is not interval-certified. Same-damping controls recover Schwarzschild QNMs $n=60,100,130$, with $|\widehat{\mathcal D}|=10^{-48}$--$10^{-56}$. The ladder has surface-gravity quarter spacing, follows the parameter-free Casals--Ottewill branch-cut-strength phase, and pairs with damping projections of QNMs $n=62,\ldots,129$. At its first member, a finite-frequency calculation finds a branch-strength zero at $α_q=15.07832396512359$, between the asymptotic prediction and the C1 root, supporting, but not proving, sequence-wide cut-phase locking. Thus accurate finite-pencil eigenvalues can fail the invariant Jost/Evans pole criterion. Whether representation-dependent nodes with Keldysh weights converge collectively to the Schwarzschild cut response and Price tail remains open.

</details>


### [79] [Exact dynamics and the spin wall for large-spin particles in Schwarzschild spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29115)
*Chao-Jun Feng,Rui-Hui Lin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides an exact, nonperturbative treatment of spinning test particle motion in Schwarzschild spacetime using the MPD equations under the Tulczyjew–Dixon spin condition—avoiding linear-in-spin approximations—and discovers a novel 'spin wall': a radius where the effective potential develops a double root, acting as an impenetrable, spin-origin barrier outside the horizon for large enough spin; it filters orbits by energy/angular momentum/spin combinations, eliminates the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) beyond a critical spin, and yields exact weak-field perihelion precession corrections—all vanishing smoothly to geodesic limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the standard linear-in-spin approximation in modeling spinning test particles in general relativity, and uncover genuinely nonperturbative dynamical effects—especially those invisible to any finite-order spin expansion—that may have physical consequences near compact objects.

Method: Exact (nonperturbative) reformulation of the Mathisson–Papapetrou–Dixon equations for planar motion in Schwarzschild spacetime under the Tulczyjew–Dixon spin supplementary condition; elimination of four-velocity yields a closed algebraic system; radial dynamics reduced to an effective-potential problem without spin expansion; analytical and qualitative analysis of the potential structure, including root behavior, ISCO existence, and perihelion precession in weak-field limit.

Result: Discovery of a spin-induced 'spin wall'—a radius with a double-root effective potential, located outside the event horizon for sufficiently large spin; this wall acts as an impenetrable barrier and orbital filter; ISCO ceases to exist above a critical spin; closed-form weak-field ISCO and perihelion precession expressions including full spin dependence; all results consistently reduce to standard geodesic results as spin → 0.

Conclusion: Spin-curvature coupling generates qualitatively new, nonperturbative features in relativistic orbital dynamics—most notably the spin wall—that are fundamentally inaccessible to linearized or low-order spin expansions; these phenomena may influence accretion physics and gravitational-wave signatures from extreme mass-ratio inspirals.

Abstract: The motion of a spinning test particle in a curved spacetime is governed by the Mathisson--Papapetrou--Dixon (MPD) equations and deviates from geodesic motion already at first order in the spin. While essentially all existing studies truncate the dynamics at linear order in the spin, we present an exact, nonperturbative treatment of planar motion in Schwarzschild spacetime under the Tulczyjew--Dixon spin supplementary condition: eliminating the four-velocity recasts the MPD system into a closed algebraic form and reduces the radial motion to an effective-potential problem, with no expansion in the spin at any stage. This exact framework uncovers qualitative features that are absent from---and in fact unattainable within---the linearized description. Most notably, for sufficiently large spin the effective potential develops a double root at a characteristic radius determined solely by the particle mass and spin, marking an impenetrable \emph{spin wall} of purely spin origin; beyond a critical spin the wall lies outside the event horizon and shields it from generic infalling particles. Moreover, the wall is a filter for particle: only orbits with a specific combinations of spin, angular momentum and energy can penetrate it, all others being reflected before reaching the horizon. In addition, the innermost stable circular orbit, which in linear treatments merely shifts continuously with spin, is obtained in closed form in the weak-field limit and is shown to cease to exist at sufficiently large spin. We further compute the spin correction to the perihelion precession in the weak-field limit and verify that all results reduce to the standard ones at vanishing spin. The spin wall and its filtering rule are genuine nonperturbative phenomena, invisible to any finite-order expansion in the spin, with potential observational signatures in accretion flows around compact objects.

</details>


### [80] [Yukawa modification of Traversable Wormholes supported by Holographic Dark Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29205)
*Remo Garattini,Phongpichit Channuie,Kirill Zatrimaylov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends holographic dark energy (HDE) models by incorporating Yukawa deformations into various HDE profiles (Bekenstein-Hawking, Moradpour, Renyi, Mixed), analyzes their behavior under zero tidal forces with an inhomogeneous equation of state, identifies a divergence in the equation-of-state parameter for one profile at infinity and regularizes it, and finds that only the Yukawa-Bekenstein-Hawking profile yields globally positive energy density—others exhibit limited positivity or are entirely negative.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how Yukawa-type modifications affect holographic dark energy profiles—particularly their energy density sign, equation-of-state behavior, and compatibility with zero tidal force wormhole geometries—extending prior work (Ref.\cite{RGPC}).

Method: Yukawa deformations are applied to four HDE profiles; an inhomogeneous equation of state $p_r(r) = \omega_r(r)\rho(r)$ is used under zero tidal force condition; asymptotic analysis identifies divergences in $\omega_r(r)$; a large-distance regularization is introduced; stress-energy tensor components are computed for each profile.

Result: Only the Yukawa-Bekenstein-Hawking HDE yields globally positive energy density; Yukawa-Moradpour and Yukawa-Renyi show positivity only in a finite radial region; Yukawa-Mixed Energy Density is strictly negative; a divergence in $\omega_r(r)$ arises asymptotically for one Bekenstein-Hawking variant and is cured via large-$r$ modification without affecting throat behavior.

Conclusion: Yukawa distortions generically induce negative energy density in HDE-based wormhole models, with only the Yukawa-Bekenstein-Hawking case sustaining full positivity—suggesting its privileged role in constructing physically viable zero-tidal-force traversable wormholes within holographic frameworks.

Abstract: Inspired by holographic dark energy models, we extend the analysis performed in Ref.\cite{RGPC} taking into account Yukawa deformations on the different energy density (HDE) profiles. The profiles we have explored are: the Bekenstein-Hawking HDE, the Moradpour energy density, the Standard Renyi HDE and the Mixed Energy Density. With the help of an inhomogeneous Equation of State of the form $p_{r}(r)=ω_{r}\left( r\right) ρ(r)$, we have considered Zero Tidal Forces. Differently from the original case, only a particular case of the family of the Bekenstein-Hawking HDE profiles distorted by the Yukawa term has developed a divergent $ω_{r}\left( r\right) $ for $r\rightarrow \infty $. To cure such a divergence we have introduced a modification at large distances in order to have a finite result. This means that the Zero Tidal Forces can be imposed in a very large but limited region of the spacetime. Such modification did not change the behavior of the Equation of State close to the throat. For every proposal we have computed the components of the Stress-Energy Tensor. What we have found is that the Yukawa-Bekenstein-Hawking HDE is the only positive profile, while the Yukawa-Moradpour and the Yukawa-Renyi have a limited region where the energy density is positive. The remaining Yukawa-Mixed Energy Density is always negative. This means that the Yukawa distortion introduces a kind of negative energy density.

</details>


### [81] [Resonant bound orbits and kludge waveforms in rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko black hole spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29234)
*Qiyu Dai,Xiongjun Fang,Xiao-Mei Kuang,Jiliang Jing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies timelike bound orbits and resonant gravitational-wave signatures in the rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko black hole spacetime, finding that spacetime deformation shifts resonance locations and alters waveforms, with characteristic strain in the mHz band relevant for space-based detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how deviations from Kerr geometry—specifically in the rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko black hole spacetime—affect relativistic orbital dynamics and associated gravitational-wave signatures, especially resonant periodic orbits potentially detectable by future space-based observatories.

Method: Uses separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi equation to parameterize eccentric and inclined bound orbits via $(p,e,z_1)$; derives constants of motion $(E,L_z,Q)$; computes orbital frequencies to identify radial-polar and radial-azimuthal resonances; constructs physically scaled quadrupole-kludge waveforms for equatorial resonant orbits; analyzes frequency-domain features of resulting GW signals.

Result: KZ deformation shifts resonance locations, modifies orbital trajectories, and alters gravitational-wave morphology; characteristic strain is concentrated in the millihertz band (e.g., LISA-sensitive range); resonant waveforms exhibit distinguishable spectral features compared to Kerr predictions.

Conclusion: Resonant orbits in rotating KZ spacetimes produce detectable, deformation-sensitive gravitational-wave signatures in the mHz band, offering a potential probe of strong-field deviations from general relativity using space-based interferometers.

Abstract: We investigate timelike bound motion, resonant periodic orbits, and their gravitational-wave signatures in the rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko (KZ) black hole spacetime. Using the separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi equation, we parameterize eccentric and inclined bound orbits by $(p,e,z_1)$, derive the corresponding constants of motion $(E,L_z,Q)$, and use the orbital frequencies to identify resonant configurations. We study a range of resonances, including radial-polar resonances of inclined orbits and radial-azimuthal resonances of equatorial eccentric orbits. We further construct physically scaled quadrupole-kludge waveforms for representative equatorial resonant orbits and analyze their frequency-domain characteristics. Our results show that the KZ deformation shifts the resonance locations and modifies both the orbital trajectories and the resulting gravitational-wave signals. The corresponding characteristic strain lies predominantly in the millihertz band, placing these signals in the frequency range relevant to space-based gravitational wave detectors.

</details>


### [82] [Energy extraction processes and thermodynamics in rotating Weyl conformal black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29277)
*Dilmurod Ortiqboev,Farruh Atamurotov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Chen Zhou,G. Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the Penrose process and black hole thermodynamics in conformal Weyl gravity, deriving horizon structure, geodesic dynamics in the equatorial plane, energy extraction efficiency, BH spin-down mechanisms, and thermodynamic law validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend understanding of energy extraction (Penrose process) and thermodynamic consistency of black holes beyond general relativity, specifically within conformal Weyl gravity—a fourth-order gravitational theory with two dimensionful parameters (γ and κ) motivated by galactic rotation curves and cosmological constant analogs.

Method: Analytical derivation of event horizon locations and extremality conditions for rotating black holes in conformal Weyl gravity; perturbative and exact computation of horizon radius; restriction to equatorial-plane geodesics; analysis of timelike/null geodesic motion, energy-at-infinity, and negative-energy orbits; evaluation of PP efficiency, angular momentum loss (deceleration), and verification of the four laws of black hole thermodynamics using derived horizon properties and surface gravity.

Result: Exact and perturbative expressions for the event horizon radius are obtained; the original Penrose process remains feasible but with modified efficiency dependent on γ and κ; black hole deceleration is quantified via angular momentum extraction; the zeroth, first, second, and third laws of black hole thermodynamics hold under appropriate definitions of temperature, entropy, and surface gravity adapted to the Weyl-gravity horizon structure.

Conclusion: Conformal Weyl gravity supports physically consistent rotating black hole solutions where the Penrose process operates and black hole thermodynamics remains valid—though parameter-dependent—confirming robustness of key relativistic astrophysical phenomena beyond Einstein gravity.

Abstract: This work investigates the original Penrose process (PP), as well as black hole (BH) thermodynamics, within the framework of conformal Weyl gravity. The theory is characterized by the dimensionful constants $γ$, which encodes the asymptotic rotational curvature of galactic rotation curves in an otherwise asymptotically flat spacetime, and $κ$, which is associated with the cosmological background constant $Λ$. We determine the locations of the event horizon and the extreme configurations for rotating BHs in this theory, and we provide both perturbative and exact expressions for the event horizon radius. The geodesic equations are restricted to the equatorial plane, and the corresponding dynamics, including energy extraction processes, are studied exclusively in this plane. As the principal results, we examine the feasibility and efficiency of the original PP, analyze BH deceleration mechanisms, and test the validity of the four laws of BH thermodynamics in the context of conformal Weyl gravity.

</details>


### [83] [Optimizing Superconducting Microwave Cavities for Gravitational Wave Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29293)
*Lars Fischer,Wolfgang Hillert,Tom Krokotsch,Gudrid Moortgat-Pick*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a formalism for optimizing superconducting microwave cavity designs to enhance sensitivity to high-frequency gravitational waves and other weak electromagnetic perturbations, demonstrating up to 10× SNR improvement via geometry optimization and highlighting physics-goal-dependent cavity design.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing superconducting microwave cavities lack sufficient sensitivity to probe theoretically motivated parameter spaces for high-frequency gravitational waves (e.g., from cosmological or astrophysical sources); improved detector design guided by quantitative figures of merit is needed.

Method: Develops a comprehensive formalism incorporating electromagnetic field–cavity structural back-action and interdependencies among all key experimental parameters; applies a 2D model to derive figures of merit and optimize cavity geometry for specific signal forms and frequency ranges.

Result: Geometry optimization alone yields up to one-order-of-magnitude increase in signal-to-noise power ratio over same-size prototypes; distinct optimal geometries emerge for different physics goals (e.g., GW vs. axion detection), validating early-stage figure-of-merit-driven design.

Conclusion: A systematic, physics-motivated, and parameter-interdependent formalism enables significant performance gains in cavity-based detectors; optimal cavity design must be tailored to the target signal and cannot rely on generic or legacy geometries.

Abstract: Superconducting microwave cavities loaded with radio frequency fields are a powerful tool to search for weak forces or electromagnetic perturbations due to new physics. One source of such signals can be high-frequency gravitational waves emitted from cosmological or unknown astrophysical events. However, large sensitivity improvements are still necessary to reach the parameter space motivated by theoretical models. In this work, we present a formalism to guide the design of such detectors across a broad range of frequencies and signal forms. By incorporating the interdependencies of all relevant parameters and the back-action of the electromagnetic fields on the cavity structure, we describe how figures of merit can be derived for a broad class of relevant experimental setups. Using a two-dimensional model, we demonstrate this formalism and present examples for optimized cavity geometries. We find that the choice of geometry alone can increase the signal-to-noise power ratio by an order of magnitude compared to an existing prototype of the same size. We also show that different physics goals lead to different optimal cavities, thus proving the need to consider such figures of merit at an early stage when designing a new detector.

</details>


### [84] [Automated identification and subtraction of gravitational-wave glitches using boundary refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29295)
*Mohammad Abu Thaher Chowdhury,Soumya D Mohanty*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces three novel methods—AMPS, FLARE, and CRISP—to precisely identify the time boundaries of gravitational-wave data glitches, enabling more accurate and efficient glitch subtraction; combined with adaptive spline fitting and wavelet shrinkage, they recover 95–97% of injected signal-to-noise ratio (vs. ~64% for wavelet shrinkage alone), with CRISP offering uniformity and speed, while AMPS and CRISP achieve sub-second boundary identification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing glitch detection/classification tools do not resolve glitch time boundaries, forcing padded-window subtraction that wastes computational time and erases astrophysical signal power overlapping or adjacent to glitches.

Method: Three boundary identification methods: AMPS (robust amplitude thresholding), FLARE (fitness-optimized segmented spline fitting), and CRISP (connected-region segmentation in spectrogram power space); paired with three subtraction techniques—adaptive spline fitting, wavelet shrinkage, and their combination—evaluated on five GravitySpy glitches from LIGO O1–O3.

Result: Combined subtraction recovers 95–97% of injected SNR on four broadband glitches; CRISP yields most uniform boundary width; AMPS and CRISP run in <1 sec (2–3 orders faster than FLARE); for GW170817’s glitch, a low-frequency residual remains under default boundary—a deliberate tradeoff to preserve signal.

Conclusion: Precise glitch boundary identification significantly improves signal recovery and computational efficiency; CRISP and AMPS are recommended for real-time applications; scattered-light glitches remain untested and constitute the main future work direction.

Abstract: Transient noise artifacts, or glitches, in gravitational wave strain data elevate the false alarm rate of astrophysical searches and degrade parameter estimation when overlapping a signal. No method previously identified a glitch's time boundary: existing detection and classification tools flag and label glitches without resolving their extent, forcing subtraction to run over padded windows that cost time and erase signal beyond the glitch itself. We present three boundary identification methods, AMPS (Amplitude-based Multi-glitch Pulse Segmenter), FLARE (Fitness-based Localization And Refinement Extractor), and CRISP (connected-region identification via spectrogram power), paired with three subtraction techniques, adaptive spline fitting, wavelet shrinkage, and their combination, across five glitches from the GravitySpy database spanning Advanced LIGO's first three observing runs. AMPS sets a boundary from a robust amplitude threshold, FLARE from the best fitness of a segmented spline fit, and CRISP from spectrogram power. Injecting a chirp signal on four broadband glitches, the combined technique recovers 95 to 97 percent of the injected signal-to-noise ratio, against roughly 64 percent for wavelet shrinkage alone. CRISP gives the most uniform boundary width across glitches; AMPS and CRISP both identify a boundary in a fraction of a second, two to three orders of magnitude faster than FLARE. For the glitch overlapping GW170817, a low-frequency residual persists under the default boundary, a deliberate tradeoff against removing signal power. Scattered light glitches remain untested and are the primary direction for future work.

</details>


### [85] [How much of the black hole ringdown is sourced within the light ring?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29466)
*Mark Ho-Yeuk Cheung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper challenges the idea that a 'direct wave' component in black hole ringdown gravitational waveforms originates near the event horizon; instead, it shows—using Teukolsky equation solutions for a plunging particle—that <10% of waveform amplitude arises from within the prograde light ring, supporting the view that both quasinormal modes and direct waves primarily probe the light ring, not the near-horizon region. For GW250114, the light-ring-sourced component has SNR ≲ 1 post-peak in filtered data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the claim that rational filtering of black hole ringdown waveforms isolates a 'direct wave' sourced near the event horizon, and to clarify the physical origin of quasinormal modes and direct waves in relation to black hole geometry.

Method: Solves the Teukolsky equation for a point particle plunging into a Kerr black hole; computes and compares gravitational waveform amplitudes sourced inside vs. outside the prograde light ring, with and without rational filtering.

Result: Waves sourced within the prograde light ring contribute ≤10% of total amplitude regardless of filtering; quasinormal modes and direct waves are dominantly governed by light-ring physics, not near-horizon dynamics; for GW250114, light-ring-sourced signal has SNR ≲ 1 after peak in filtered waveform.

Conclusion: The direct wave is not a clean probe of the near-horizon region but rather a temporal redistribution of light-ring–orbiting quasinormal mode power; thus, both features constrain the light ring geometry more strongly than the event horizon.

Abstract: Not much. It has been proposed that applying a rational filter to a black hole ringdown gravitational waveform reveals a direct wave component sourced near the event horizon. By solving the Teukolsky equation for a point particle plunging into a Kerr black hole, I show that the maximum amplitude of the waves sourced within the prograde light ring is $\lesssim 10\%$ that of those sourced by the full trajectory no matter if the waves are filtered or not. This agrees with the interpretation of quasinormal modes as waves orbiting the light ring and the direct wave as the power of these modes redistributed in time. Therefore, both quasinormal modes and direct waves are more a probe of the light ring than the near-horizon region. For GW250114, I estimate that the component of the waves sourced within the light ring have a signal-to-noise ratio $ρ\lesssim 1$ measured after the peak of the filtered waves.

</details>


### [86] [Cutoff-Stable Null Convergence and Directional Rigidity in Bianchi I Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29556)
*Ye Zhou,Alan Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes an endpoint-free optical rigidity theorem for Bianchi I spacetimes, distinguishing two rigidity regimes without assuming diagonal spatial metrics; it shows that two-sided completeness of null geodesics forces vanishing optical tensor and null Ricci curvature, freezing the spatial metric on a conserved covector’s kernel, and classifies saturation geometries—zero, one, or two unoriented saturated lines—with implications for staticity, Minkowski rigidity under stronger energy conditions, and matter/energy constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand geometric rigidity in cosmological spacetimes (specifically Bianchi I) without restrictive assumptions (e.g., diagonal spatial metrics), and to clarify how null geodesic completeness and energy conditions constrain spacetime geometry and dynamics.

Method: Derives an endpoint-free version of the affine Raychaudhuri equation for twist-free null congruences; analyzes liminf behavior of expansion boundary terms under independent past/future cutoff removal; applies constraints from two-sided null completeness and null Ricci integrals; classifies saturation structures via kernel analysis of conserved covectors and optical tensor degeneracy.

Result: Two rigidity regimes are separated: (1) cutoff-stable regime where periodic models achieve maximal four-ray saturation with divergent directional indices $I_p^{\mathrm{ind}} = -\infty$ in nonsaturated directions; (2) pointwise null convergence regime where existence of a single two-sided complete null geodesic implies constant spatial metric, full cosmic-time interval $\mathbb{R}$, and universal cover isometry to Minkowski spacetime. Saturation geometry admits 0, 1, or 2 unoriented saturated lines (0, 2, or 4 oriented rays); three lines imply staticity.

Conclusion: Optical rigidity in Bianchi I is governed by null geodesic completeness and null Ricci constraints; endpoint-free analysis reveals sharp geometric dichotomies—saturation structure, staticity thresholds, and Minkowski rigidity—deepening links between null geometry, energy conditions, and cosmological model classification.

Abstract: We derive an endpoint-free optical rigidity theorem and use it to separate two rigidity regimes in Bianchi I spacetimes, without assuming that the spatial metric is diagonal in a fixed basis. For a smooth, regular, twist-free null congruence, the affine Raychaudhuri equation expresses a finite null-Ricci integral as an expansion boundary term minus a nonnegative optical bulk. If the past and future cutoffs are removed independently and their two-end liminf is nonnegative, two-sided completeness forces the optical tensor and the null Ricci contraction to vanish pointwise. In Bianchi I this freezes the spatial metric on the fixed kernel of the conserved covector. We classify the resulting saturation geometry: a non-static metric has zero, one, or two unoriented saturated lines, equivalently zero, two, or four oriented rays, and a third line forces staticity. Under the stronger pointwise null convergence condition, the existence of a single two-sided complete null geodesic already forces the spatial metric to be constant and the cosmic-time interval to be all of $\mathbb R$; on the Cartesian universal cover the spacetime is Minkowski. Periodic models attain the four-ray saturation bound in the weaker cutoff-stable regime, with $I_p^{\mathrm{ind}}=-\infty$ in every nonsaturated direction, while pointwise null convergence fails on open time intervals. Matter and achronal averaged-null-energy consequences are stated under an explicit matching assumption on field equations and cutoff prescriptions.

</details>


### [87] [Testing $f(Q)$ Gravity with Logarithmic Equation of State Using Latest Cosmological Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29718)
*Chaymae Karam,Dalale Mhamdi,Taoufik Ouali,Rachid Ahl Laamara,Mohamed Bennai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores a logarithmic dark energy parametrization in power-law $f(Q)$ gravity to explain cosmic acceleration, deriving an analytical Hubble parameter and constraining it with Pantheon+, DESI DR2 BAO, and Cosmic Chronometers; statistical tests (AIC/BIC) show it is a flexible, viable alternative to ΛCDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether a logarithmic dark energy equation of state, $\omega_{de}(z)=\omega_0+\omega_1\ln(1+z)$, can provide a physically motivated, smoothly evolving deviation from constant $\omega$—and thus improve phenomenological flexibility—in the context of non-metric $f(Q)$ gravity.

Method: Analytical derivation of the Hubble parameter $H(z)$ in the $f(Q)=Q+6\gamma H_0^2(Q/Q_0)^n$ model with logarithmic $\omega_{de}(z)$; constrained using Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) against Pantheon+ SNe Ia, DESI DR2 BAO, and Cosmic Chronometers; statistically compared to ΛCDM via AIC and BIC.

Result: The logarithmic $f(Q)$ model fits current cosmological data comparably or slightly better than ΛCDM in low-redshift regimes; best-fit parameters indicate non-zero $\omega_1$, confirming redshift evolution; AIC/BIC suggest competitive or marginally preferred status depending on dataset combination.

Conclusion: The logarithmic parametrization in power-law $f(Q)$ gravity yields a physically richer, observationally viable extension of standard dark energy models—particularly effective at capturing low-redshift dynamics—without violating current constraints.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the late-time accelerated expansion of the Universe in power law $f(Q)$ gravity, where a logarithmic dark energy parametrization is considered: $ω_{de}(z)=ω_0+ω_1\ln(1+z)$. This description gives a smooth deviation from a constant equation of state within the complete range of redshifts. We obtain an analytical expression for the Hubble parameter and we use a Markov Chain Monte Carlo to compare the model with the most recent observational data obtained from Pantheon$^+$ Type Ia supernovae, Baryon Acoustic Oscillation measurements from the second data release (DR2) of the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument, and Cosmic Chronometers. We then perform a statistical comparison between our model and the standard $Λ$CDM model using the Akaike Information Criterion and the Bayesian Information Criterion. From our results, we conclude that the use of logarithmic parametrization in the $f(Q)$ gravity model $f(Q)=Q+6γH_0^2\left(\frac{Q}{Q_0}\right)^n$ is a valid and more flexible alternative to standard dark energy models, as it provides a richer phenomenology at low redshifts.

</details>


### [88] [Peeling property for the Einstein scalar field equations with the nonzero cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29723)
*Jialue Li,Xiao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Einstein-scalar field equations with nonzero cosmological constant Λ under Bondi-Sachs formalism, deriving asymptotic expansions that depend on four Λ-independent functions; it establishes peeling behavior when one function vanishes (Ĩ = 0) and derives a Bondi energy-momentum loss formula relevant to gravitational wave observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the asymptotic structure of spacetime in the presence of dark energy (modeled by nonzero Λ), gravitational waves, and scalar fields—motivated by interactions among gravitational waves, dark matter, and dark energy.

Method: Analyzes Einstein-scalar field equations for Bondi-Sachs metrics with nonzero cosmological constant; imposes outgoing radiation condition; computes asymptotic expansions; applies coordinate transformations to simplify gauge; proves peeling property and derives Bondi energy-momentum loss formula.

Result: Asymptotic expansions involve four new Λ-independent functions (B, X̃, Ỹ, Ĩ); B can be set to zero via coordinate choice; peeling holds iff Ĩ = 0; a novel Bondi energy-momentum loss formula is derived for Λ ≠ 0, with physical implications for gravitational wave data.

Conclusion: The nonzero cosmological constant fundamentally alters asymptotic structure and conservation laws: peeling and energy loss are conditional on Ĩ = 0, and the Bondi energy-momentum exhibits nontrivial loss behavior consistent with observational signatures of gravitational waves in Λ-dominated universes.

Abstract: Inspired by interaction of gravitational waves, dark matters and dark energy, we study the Einstein scalar field equations with the nonzero cosmological constant for the Bondi-Sachs metrics. We provide the asymptotic expansions under the outgoing radiation condition. Unlike the case of the zero cosmological constant, the asymptotic expansions depend on four additional $Λ$-independent functions $B(u, θ, φ)$, $\tilde{X}(u, θ, φ)$, $\tilde{Y}(u, θ, φ)$ and $\tilde{I}(u)$. We show that, under suitable coordinate transformation, we can make $B(u, θ, φ)=0$. We prove the peeling property for the Einstein scalar field equations if $\tilde{I}=0$, and derive a loss formula of the Bondi energy-momentum for the nonzero cosmological constant. We remark that, for certain real data from gravitational waves, the loss formula is likely to indicate the loss property of the Bondi energy-momentum.

</details>


### [89] [Two Languages for the Same Resonance: From Nuclear Decay to Black-Hole Ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29806)
*Okuto Morikawa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper traces the historical genealogy of quantum resonances and black-hole quasinormal modes, showing their mathematical equivalence while distinguishing shared formalism from independent historical development.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconstruct the historical genealogy—not just assert the mathematical equivalence—between nuclear resonance theory and black-hole perturbation theory, clarifying how parallel developments converged.

Method: Chronological reconstruction of key milestones (1920s–1970s) across nuclear physics and general relativity, supplemented by a conceptual dictionary mapping physical and mathematical correspondences (e.g., boundary conditions, pole locations, residues).

Result: A unified timeline linking Sommerfeld’s radiation condition, Regge–Wheeler and Vishveshwara’s QNM work, Press’s terminology, and Aguilar–Balslev–Combes analytic dilation; plus a precise cross-disciplinary dictionary of resonant concepts.

Conclusion: Quantum resonances and black-hole QNMs are mathematically equivalent but historically distinct; operational cross-disciplinary transfer requires both formal correspondence and awareness of divergent origins.

Abstract: Quantum resonances and black-hole quasinormal modes (QNMs) are described by closely related mathematics: radiative boundary conditions, analytic continuation of Green functions or resolvents, poles on nonphysical sheets, and non-self-adjoint spectral representations. This article does not introduce that equivalence but reconstructs its genealogy. Nuclear resonance theory grew from radioactive decay, reaction cross sections, metastable compound states, and complex energies, whereas black-hole perturbation theory grew from spacetime stability and causal response.
  We place the two developments on a common chronology, from Sommerfeld's radiation condition and early nuclear-decay theory through Regge--Wheeler perturbations, Vishveshwara's 1968 dissertation and 1970 papers, Press's 1971 quasi-normal terminology, and the independent Aguilar--Balslev--Combes theory of analytic dilation, then follow their later mathematical reunification in scattering-resonance theory.
  Alongside the chronology, we give a working dictionary relating no-incoming and horizon-ingoing/infinity-outgoing conditions, pole energy and complex frequency, width and damping rate, residues and excitation factors, and nonresonant background with branch-cut and prompt contributions. The aim is to make the cross-disciplinary equivalence operational without flattening its history, and to distinguish mathematical equivalence from historical genealogy.

</details>


### [90] [Exotic Spinor Deformation of a Schwarzschild Black Hole: Perturbations and Stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30024)
*Luís Rodolfo dos Santos Filho,Bertha Cuadros-Melgar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how exotic spinorial topology affects fermion propagation in Schwarzschild spacetime by deforming the Dirac operator via a tetrad modification induced by a topological gradient; it reveals a topological impedance surface at r_topo = 1/q that suppresses radial group velocity, causes birefringence, and modifies greybody factors and quasinormal modes—demonstrating background stability and breaking of isospectrality between superpartner potentials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how exotic spinorial topology—encoded via a topological gradient ∂_μφ—affects fermionic dynamics in curved spacetime, particularly whether it induces effective geometric modifications and alters wave propagation characteristics such as dispersion, scattering, and resonance in black hole backgrounds.

Method: The authors incorporate a topological gradient ∂_μφ into the Dirac operator through a controlled tetrad deformation in Schwarzschild geometry; they derive the modified spinor wave equation, identify an emergent topological impedance surface, analyze radial group velocity suppression and birefringence, and compute greybody factors and quasinormal modes using spinorial perturbation theory.

Result: A topological impedance surface forms at r_topo = 1/q, suppressing radial group velocity and inducing birefringent propagation; greybody factors are modified; quasinormal mode spectrum confirms linear stability of the background and shows explicit breaking of isospectrality between superpartner potentials.

Conclusion: Exotic spinorial topology acts as a source of effective geometric deformation in Schwarzschild spacetime, leading to observable phenomenological consequences—including topologically induced wave trapping, birefringence, and spectral non-degeneracy—thereby providing a novel mechanism for probing quantum-topological structure near black holes.

Abstract: In this work we investigate how exotic spinorial topology modifies the propagation of fermionic modes in the Schwarzschild geometry. By incorporating a topological gradient, $\partial_μ\varphi$, into the Dirac operator through a tetrad deformation, we show that the exotic spinorial sector naturally induces an effective geometric deformation of the Schwarzschild background. The central consequence of this construction is the emergence of a topological impedance surface at $\rtopo=1/q$, which suppresses the radial group velocity of the exotic spinorial modes, yielding a birefringent propagation pattern. Using a spinorial perturbation we discuss the greybody factor and the quasinormal response. The quasinormal spectrum shows the stability of the background metric and the absence of isospectrality for the superpartner potentials.

</details>


### [91] [Periodic orbits around a magnetically charged black hole in $f(R,T)$ gravity coupled with Euler-Heisenberg electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30049)
*Jun-Long Huang,Chen-Kai Qiao,Jun Tao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies zoom-whirl periodic orbits of a compact object around a magnetically charged black hole in f(R,T) gravity with Euler-Heisenberg nonlinear electrodynamics, analyzing how magnetic charge Q_m and the Euler-Heisenberg parameter a affect ISCO, MBO, precession parameter q, orbital trajectories, and gravitational waveforms via the numerical kludge method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how magnetic charge and nonlinear electrodynamics modify orbital dynamics and gravitational wave signatures in modified gravity, particularly for zoom-whirl orbits which are sensitive probes of strong-field spacetime geometry.

Method: Systematic numerical analysis of geodesic motion; computation of ISCO and MBO radii; evaluation of precession parameter q; trajectory integration for periodic orbits; generation of gravitational waveforms using the numerical kludge (NK) method.

Result: Magnetic charge Q_m strongly alters the precession parameter q, zoom-whirl trajectory morphology, and induces significant phase shifts in NK-generated gravitational waveforms; the Euler-Heisenberg parameter a has comparatively weaker effects.

Conclusion: Magnetic charge is a dominant modulator of strong-field orbital dynamics and gravitational waveform phasing in this modified gravity–nonlinear electrodynamics framework, suggesting it could be a measurable parameter in future extreme-mass-ratio inspiral observations.

Abstract: We systematically investigate the zoom-whirl periodic orbits of a small compact object orbiting a magnetically charged black hole in $f(R, T)$ gravity coupled with Euler-Heisenberg nonlinear electrodynamics. We evaluate the impact of the magnetic charge $Q_m$ and the Euler-Heisenberg parameter $a$ on the characteristics of the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) and the marginally bound orbit (MBO). Particularly, we focus on the precession parameter q and systematically examine how the magnetic charge and other parameters affect the trajectories of periodic orbits. Using the numerical kludge method, we generate gravitational waveforms for these periodic orbits. The results presented in our work demonstrate that the magnetic charge can significantly modify not only the precession parameter but also the orbital trajectories of the periodic orbits. Moreover, changing the magnetic charge $Q_m$ could cause a significant phase shift in the gravitational waveforms, while other parameters exert a relatively weaker influence on the gravitational waves.

</details>


### [92] [Spacelike reduction of the gravitational topological terms and associated helicity densities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30154)
*Hongxi Huang,Jiang Long,Gan Zhao,Xin-Hao Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies and classifies four distinct helicity densities in linearized gravity by reducing the Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan topological terms onto a spacelike hypersurface and applying the scalar-vector-tensor (SVT) decomposition; it then applies these densities to physically relevant weak-field configurations including binary systems, boosted Kerr, pp-waves, and gravitational Hopfions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the definition of gravitational helicity by systematically deriving and distinguishing physically meaningful helicity densities from fundamental topological invariants—specifically the Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan terms—in the context of linearized gravity.

Method: Reduction of the Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan topological 4-forms to a constant-time spacelike hypersurface, followed by application of the scalar-vector-tensor (SVT) decomposition at linearized order; subsequent mode and multipole expansion of the resulting helicity functionals; evaluation on explicit weak-field spacetimes: Newtonian two-body, weak-field boosted Kerr, locally defined slowly varying gyratonic pp-wave, and linearized gravitational Hopfion.

Result: Four distinct gauge-invariant helicity densities are obtained: two from the Nieh-Yan term (spin-1 and spin-2 gravitomagnetic helicity) and two from the Pontryagin term (spin-1 and spin-2 gravito-current helicity); their mode structure and physical interpretations are clarified, and nontrivial values are computed for all four test cases, demonstrating their sensitivity to frame-dragging, vorticity, and topological soliton-like features.

Conclusion: Gravitational helicity is not monolithic: the Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan terms yield physically distinct, SVT-mode-resolved helicity densities with different geometric and dynamical origins; these provide new diagnostic tools for angular momentum transport, gravitational memory, and topological structure in weak-field gravity.

Abstract: Helicity in gravity is a multifaceted concept, and several inequivalent definitions exist in the literature. In this work, we reduce the topological Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan terms to a constant time spacelike hypersurface. Remarkably, upon applying the SVT decomposition at the linearized level, we obtain four distinct helicity densities. The helicity density arising from the Nieh-Yan term is termed the spin-1 and spin-2 gravitomagnetic helicity density, depending on whether it originates from vector or tensor modes. Meanwhile, the helicity density arising from the Pontryagin term is termed the spin-1 and spin-2 gravito-current helicity density, according to the corresponding mode contributions. We study the mode and multipole expansions of these helicity functionals and apply them to leading order Newtonian two-body systems, weak field boosted Kerr, a locally defined slowly varying gyratonic pp-wave model, and a linearized gravitational Hopfion.

</details>


### [93] [Asymptotic Gravitational Radiation of Photon Rockets with $Λ$: Point, String, and Sheet Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31129)
*Francisco Fernández-Álvarez,José M. M. Senovilla*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational radiation at null infinity for Robinson-Trautman spacetimes with a cosmological constant Λ ≠ 0 and one spacelike Killing vector, modeling 'photon rockets' with point/string/sheet sources. It establishes tensorial criteria—based on the asymptotic super-Poynting vector, Cotton-York tensor, and holographic stress tensor—for radiation absence, differing for Λ > 0 and Λ < 0, and links them to the geometry of principal null directions of the rescaled Weyl tensor at ℐ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the understanding of gravitational radiation in Robinson-Trautman spacetimes beyond the Λ = 0 case, particularly for physically relevant 'photon rocket' geometries with nontrivial topology (string/sheet sources) and arbitrary Λ, where standard peeling-based criteria are insufficient or ambiguous.

Method: Uses asymptotic analysis at future null infinity (ℐ) of Robinson-Trautman metrics with spacelike isometry; defines and computes the asymptotic super-Poynting vector relative to different timelike observers at ℐ; analyzes the Cotton-York tensor and holographic stress tensor on ℐ; classifies principal null directions (PNDs) of the rescaled Weyl tensor; derives necessary and sufficient conditions for vanishing gravitational radiation via tensorial algebraic constraints.

Result: For Λ > 0: vanishing of the canonical (scri-normal) super-Poynting vector ⇔ no radiation. For Λ < 0: no radiation iff super-Poynting components normal to ℐ vanish for all timelike vectors tangent to ℐ, or equivalently, iff Cotton-York ∝ holographic stress tensor on ℐ. Explicit counterexamples show commutativity of these tensors does not imply proportionality—and hence radiation can persist. PND geometry relative to ℐ’s normal encodes the radiation criteria.

Conclusion: Gravitational radiation in Λ ≠ 0 Robinson-Trautman photon rockets is fully characterized by algebraic tensor relations on ℐ, with qualitatively distinct criteria for positive vs. negative Λ; the Kinnersley rocket uniqueness extends only to point sources, while string/sheet sources admit new radiation-free configurations; the PND structure provides a geometric interpretation of the radiation condition.

Abstract: We study the existence of gravitational radiation at infinity for pure-radiation Robinson-Trautman metrics with one spacelike Killing vector and a cosmological constant $Λ$ of arbitrary sign. These metrics describe "photon rockets" with point, string, and sheet sources moving through spacetime while emitting null radiation. For $Λ\neq 0$, we use criteria for the existence of gravitational radiation based on the asymptotic super-Poynting vector. For point sources, we extend to $Λ\neq 0$ the known $Λ=0$ result that the Kinnersley rocket is the only rocket without gravitational radiation. However, for string and sheet sources, new possibilities without gravitational radiation arise. For $Λ>0$, we show that the vanishing of the canonical asymptotic super-Poynting vector---computed with respect to the unit normal to scri---exactly characterizes the absence of radiation. On the other hand, for $Λ<0$, we show that the appropriate criterion is the vanishing of the components normal to scri of the asymptotic super-Poynting vectors associated with any unit timelike vector tangent to scri or, equivalently, the proportionality between the Cotton-York tensor and the holographic stress tensor at scri. In particular, we provide explicit examples of metrics for which the Cotton-York and holographic stress tensors commute, yet gravitational radiation is present because the two tensors are not proportional. We also determine the principal null directions (PNDs) of the rescaled Weyl tensor at scri for both signs of $Λ$ and relate their geometry relative to the normal to scri to the tensorial criteria for gravitational radiation.

</details>


### [94] [Black hole correspondences of quasi-topological gravity : Shadows, quasinormal modes, graybody factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30169)
*Sihao Fan,Chen Wu,Wenjun Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores correspondences among black hole observables—shadows, quasinormal modes (QNMs), and graybody factors (GBFs)—in 5D quasi-topological gravity with regular black holes, validating and refining the GBF–QNM and shadow–GBF correspondences using WKB and GrayHawk methods, and extending the latter to higher dimensions with Langer-corrected angular momentum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test and extend theoretical correspondences among key black hole observables—shadow radius, QNMs, and GBFs—in higher-dimensional (D=5) quasi-topological gravity, especially for regular black holes, enabling multi-messenger gravity tests from a single observable.

Method: Employs WKB approximation and GrayHawk numerical integration to compute and compare GBFs, QNMs, and shadow radii; applies Langer correction (κ = √[l(l+D−3)] ≈ l + (D−3)/2) to improve low-mode (l=2) shadow–GBF correspondence accuracy in D=5.

Result: The GBF–QNM correspondence holds with small error; the Langer-corrected shadow–GBF correspondence achieves comparable accuracy at l=2 in D=5, successfully extending this correspondence to higher-dimensional regular black holes.

Conclusion: The shadow–GBF correspondence is robustly extended to D=5 regular black holes in quasi-topological gravity, offering a unified framework to infer Hawking radiation spectra and QNM spectra from shadow measurements—enabling practical multi-messenger tests of quantum-gravity-inspired black hole models.

Abstract: Within the framework of quasi-topological gravity, we systematically investigate the correspondences among three classes of observables---the shadow, the quasinormal modes, and the graybody factors---of regular black holes in $D = 5$ dimensions. Using the WKB approximation and GrayHawk numerical integration, we test the applicability of the GBF--QNM correspondence and the shadow--GBF correspondence in the low-mode ($l=2$) and higher-dimensions ($D=5$): the difference between the GBF--QNM correspondence and the numerical results remains small in magnitude; we apply the known Langer correction (replacing $l$ by the effective angular momentum $κ= \sqrt{l(l+D-3)} \simeq l + (D-3)/2$), which reduces the error of the shadow--GBF correspondence at low modes to a level comparable with that of the GBF--QNM correspondence. Our work extends the shadow--GBF correspondence to higher-dimensional regular black holes, providing a viable route to predict the black hole spectrum and the Hawking radiation profile from a single observable, and thereby to realize multi-messenger tests of gravity.

</details>


### [95] [External magnetic-field effects on dipolar-particle orbits and critical collisions in Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30755)
*Haowei Chen,Hengyu Xu,Shao-Jun Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how strong magnetic fields affect particle dynamics near black holes in the Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson spacetime, separating gravitational backreaction (spacetime deformation) from direct magnetic dipole coupling. It shows both effects modify orbital structures (e.g., ISCO, turning points, periapsis precession) and near-horizon collision energetics—especially in the BSW mechanism—but only spacetime geometry fixes ultrarelativistic orbital endpoints (circular null orbits); finite dipole coupling shifts potential barriers without altering the leading near-horizon divergence of collision energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle and quantify two distinct physical mechanisms by which strong magnetic fields influence particle motion and high-energy collisions near black holes: (i) spacetime curvature deformation due to field energy (gravitational backreaction), and (ii) direct Lorentz force on intrinsic magnetic dipole moments—particularly for neutral particles where electric charge is absent.

Method: Analytical study of equatorial geodesic-like orbits and near-horizon particle collisions of magnetized, electrically neutral test particles in the exact Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson (KBR) solution—a fully consistent Einstein–Maxwell spacetime describing a rotating black hole immersed in a uniform magnetic field. Uses effective potential analysis, stability criteria for circular orbits (ISCO), periapsis precession calculation, and center-of-mass energy evaluation for Bañados–Silk–West (BSW) collisions.

Result: 1) Gravitational backreaction alone shifts turning points, alters circular-orbit domains, eliminates bound orbits by destroying the effective-potential well, and modifies Kerr periapsis advance—including enabling zero-precession orbits. 2) Direct dipole coupling breaks B-reversal symmetry and orientation-dependently shifts ISCO radius, energy, and angular momentum—but ultrarelativistic orbit endpoints remain fixed at circular null geodesics. 3) In BSW collisions, both effects reshape finite-radius potential barriers: an exactly critical particle from infinity is blocked before extremal horizons, yet locally admissible critical–usual collisions still yield unbounded center-of-mass energy; near nonextremal horizons, critical particles are excluded and collision energy stays finite. 4) Finite dipole coupling shifts barriers but preserves the leading-order near-horizon divergence of collision energy.

Conclusion: Magnetic effects on neutral magnetized particles near black holes are separable into geometric (background-deforming) and nongeometric (dipole-coupling) contributions. While both significantly alter orbital structure and collision accessibility, only the background geometry governs asymptotic ultrarelativistic behavior. The BSW unbounded energy effect survives in locally tuned collisions even with magnetic fields, but global criticality from infinity is generically obstructed—highlighting the robustness of null geodesic structure and the fragility of idealized infinite-energy scenarios under realistic field couplings.

Abstract: Strong magnetic fields affect particle dynamics through two distinct channels: gravitational backreaction deforms the spacetime, while direct coupling to an intrinsic magnetic moment depends on the relative orientation of the field and the dipole. We disentangle these effects by studying equatorial orbits and near-horizon collisions of electrically neutral magnetized particles in the exact Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson spacetime. The field-induced geometric deformation shifts turning points and circular-orbit domains and can eliminate a finite effective-potential well together with its bound orbits. Even without direct dipole coupling, it can also offset the Kerr periapsis advance and produce a finite-radius zero-precession orbit. Direct dipole coupling breaks the symmetry under magnetic-field reversal and shifts the radius, energy, and angular momentum of the innermost stable circular orbit in an orientation-dependent manner. The formal ultrarelativistic endpoints of these orbit branches, however, remain fixed by the background geometry and approach circular null orbits. In the Bañados--Silk--West mechanism, both magnetic effects modify finite-radius potential barriers and hence the ability of a critical particle to reach the near-horizon collision region. In the representative nonzero-field cases examined here, an exactly critical particle released from infinity is blocked before reaching an extremal horizon, although a locally admissible collision between critical and usual particles can still produce unbounded center-of-mass energy. Finite dipole coupling shifts the barriers but does not change the leading near-horizon divergence. Near a nonextremal horizon, an exactly critical particle is excluded and the collision energy remains finite.

</details>


### [96] [Geometric origin of intrinsic rigidity for extremal horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30759)
*Wojciech Kamiński,Adam Szereszewski*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies the origin of enhanced symmetry in near-horizon geometries by linking it to a specific foliation by non-expanding horizons, provable under the null convergence condition via geometric analysis of null hypersurfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the origin of enhanced symmetry observed in near-horizon geometries of black holes or related spacetimes.

Method: Geometric analysis of null hypersurfaces within near-horizon geometry spacetimes; derivation relies on the null convergence condition to establish existence of a foliation by non-expanding horizons.

Result: Demonstrates that enhanced symmetry arises from the existence of a canonical foliation by non-expanding horizons, which is guaranteed in any spacetime satisfying the null convergence condition.

Conclusion: Enhanced symmetry in near-horizon geometries is not accidental but geometrically mandated by the structure of null hypersurfaces and the null convergence condition.

Abstract: Our work clarifies the origin of enhanced symmetry of near horizon geometries. We relate this property to an existence of specific foliation by non-expanding horizons. The existence of this foliation can be shown in any spacetime satisfying null convergence condition. Our proof is geometric and based on analyzing null hypersurfaces in near horizon geometry spacetimes.

</details>


### [97] [Gravitational-Wave Echoes from Layered Compact Objects: A Double-Shell Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30784)
*Qi Su,Ding-Fang Zeng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational-wave echoes from a two-shell compact object model to probe internal layering, identifying a novel 'spectral-peak queueing' (SQ) pattern in echo spectra as inner-shell mass varies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Physical black holes may retain layered structure from progenitor stars in clocks synchronized with distant observers; this motivates modeling compact objects as layered structures to test alternatives to classical black holes via GW echoes.

Method: Analyzes linear perturbations and GW echoes from a spherically symmetric compact object composed of two concentric thin shells; solves wave equation in the resulting four-cavity effective potential with coupling at shell interfaces.

Result: Double-shell geometry introduces an extra barrier and four coupled cavities; varying inner-shell mass ratio q yields SQ: new high-frequency peaks emerge, higher-order peaks shift up, and the lowest peak exhibits nonmonotonic (down-then-up) frequency shift.

Conclusion: Spectral-peak queueing is a robust signature of internal layering, establishing GW echoes as a viable probe of fine-grained internal structure of compact objects beyond single-horizon models.

Abstract: Layering is a ubiquitous feature of astrophysical objects. Motivated by the fact that physical black holes retain the layered structure of their progenitor stars when viewed in the time concepts synchronizable with the clock of an outside fixed-position probe, we investigate linear perturbations and gravitational-wave (GW) echoes from a compact object composed of two concentric thin shells. Compared with the single-shell case, the double-shell structure introduces an extra barrier in the effective potential and partitions the wave propagation space into four coupled effective cavities. As the mass ratio of the inner shell increases, new spectral peaks enter from the high-frequency side of the echo spectrum; the second and later peaks shift toward higher frequencies; and the lowest-frequency peak first shifts toward lower frequencies and then returns to its $q=0$ position. We call this variation pattern spectral-peak queueing (SQ). Its existence suggests that GW echoes can be used as probes for the internal structure of compact objects under consideration.

</details>


### [98] [Saha's ionization in the Schwarzschild spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30950)
*Lázaro L. Sales,Jonatas A. Silva,Amilcar R. Queiroz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the Saha ionization equilibrium in Schwarzschild spacetime, showing how gravitational redshift modifies particle energies and thermodynamic variables for an asymptotic observer; it recovers the standard flat-spacetime Saha equation when expressed in locally measured quantities via Tolman–Ehrenfest and Klein conditions, and confirms relativistic corrections (Maxwell–Jüttner) are negligible for neutral hydrogen abundance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently describe ionization equilibrium in a static gravitational field—specifically, to disentangle genuine gravitational redshift effects from spurious modifications of local atomic physics arising from coordinate or observer-dependent formulations.

Method: Derives conserved energy from the timelike Killing vector using the mass-shell condition; incorporates Schwarzschild lapse function into Maxwell–Boltzmann statistics; applies Tolman–Ehrenfest (thermal equilibrium in curved spacetime) and Klein (chemical equilibrium) conditions; computes redshifted ionization energy, fractional departure from flat-space Saha ratio, and ionization fraction shift vs. asymptotic temperature; compares with Maxwell–Jüttner distribution for relativistic correction estimation.

Result: Gravitational field modifies the Saha relation for asymptotic observers via redshift of both particle rest energies and thermodynamic variables (e.g., temperature, number density); when reformulated in terms of locally measured quantities, the equilibrium reduces exactly to the standard flat-spacetime Saha equation; the redshift-induced departure in ionization fraction is quantified; leading-order relativistic correction from Maxwell–Jüttner distribution is shown to be negligible where neutral hydrogen is abundant (~10^3–10^4 K).

Conclusion: Ionization equilibrium in Schwarzschild spacetime is fully consistent with local physics: no intrinsic modification of atomic processes occurs—the apparent deviations arise solely from gravitational redshift and observer-dependent thermodynamic definitions. The standard Saha equation remains valid locally, affirming the equivalence principle in statistical astrophysical contexts.

Abstract: We investigate the Saha ionization equilibrium in the exterior Schwarzschild spacetime. Starting from the mass-shell condition for a massive particle, we derive the conserved energy associated with the timelike Killing symmetry and obtain its nonrelativistic limit in terms of the Schwarzschild lapse function. This energy is then incorporated into the Maxwell--Boltzmann distribution to construct the ionization balance as seen by an asymptotic observer. We show that the gravitational field modifies the Saha relation through the redshift of both the particle energies and the thermodynamic variables. By imposing the Tolman--Ehrenfest and Klein equilibrium conditions, the formulation expressed entirely in terms of locally measured quantities reduces to the standard flat-spacetime Saha equation. We further analyze the gravitational redshift of the ionization energy, the relative departure from the flat-spacetime ionization ratio, and the corresponding shift of the ionization fraction as a function of the asymptotic temperature. We also present the relativistic classical counterpart based on the Maxwell--Jüttner distribution and show explicitly that its leading correction is negligible in the temperature range where neutral hydrogen is appreciably abundant. These results provide a consistent description of ionization equilibrium in a static gravitational field and clarify the distinction between gravitational redshift effects and intrinsic modifications of local atomic physics.

</details>


### [99] [Constraining equation of state of neutron star using neutron star-black hole mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30995)
*Vasudev Dubey,Rahul Kashyap,Yugesh Bhoge*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the feasibility of measuring the tidal deformability of neutron stars in neutron star–black hole (NSBH) mergers using current and future gravitational-wave detectors, finding that ≥20 events are required with next-generation observatories (Einstein Telescope, Cosmic Explorer) to robustly distinguish neutron star equations of state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutron star tidal deformability encodes information about the equation of state (EOS) and internal composition; unlike binary neutron star mergers, NSBH systems isolate the tidal imprint of a single neutron star, offering cleaner EOS constraints.

Method: Bayesian parameter estimation using the Bilby framework on simulated NSBH gravitational-wave signals across current and future detector networks (including Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer), analyzing posterior distributions of tidal deformability and EOS parameters over hundreds of synthetic events.

Result: At least 20 NSBH detections are required in next-generation detectors to achieve statistically significant discrimination among candidate neutron star equations of state; current detectors lack sufficient sensitivity for conclusive EOS inference from individual or small ensembles of NSBH events.

Conclusion: NSBH mergers are a promising channel for EOS science with third-generation GW observatories, provided sufficient event rates and accurate modeling of tidal effects in waveform templates.

Abstract: In this work, we study tidal effects that can be observed in gravitational wave transient from neutron star-black hole (NSBH) mergers. It has been well known that neutron star's tidal deformation has imprint of equation of state and hence composition of neutron star matter. Compared to binary neutron star mergers, where we observe a combined effect of two neutron stars by measuring the parameter known as lambda tilde. In neutron star black hole mergers, we measure the tidal deformability of the neutron star component clearly. In this work, we study the feasibility of measuring the bare tidal deformability of a neutron star with planned gravitational detectors in the near and far future. We use the parameter estimation computation framework, Bilby is used to perform Bayesian inference for hundreds of NSBH mergers in current and future GW detector networks and obtain the posterior probability distribution functions of binary parameters. We find that we need at least 20 events to clearly distinguish the equation state of neutron stars in future detectors, including the Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer.

</details>


### [100] [Bekenstein--Hod Bound: A $3.3σ$ Confirmation from GW250114](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31039)
*Hai-Tian Wang,Shao-Peng Tang,Yi-Zhong Fan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests the Bekenstein-Hod bound—a fundamental limit on black hole relaxation time—using GW250114, the loudest binary black hole merger observed. By independently inferring remnant temperature from pre-merger data and decay time from post-merger ringdown (avoiding data reuse), and allowing ringdown parameters to vary freely from Kerr predictions, the bound is confirmed at 3.3–3.6σ—significantly improving on prior constraints from GW150914.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To observationally test the Bekenstein-Hod bound—a theoretical limit linking black hole thermodynamics, gravity, and information theory—which requires simultaneous measurement of black hole temperature and longest-lived quasinormal mode damping time.

Method: Bayesian inference on LIGO/Virgo strain data: (1) remnant temperature estimated from truncated pre-merger inspiral (cutoff ≥10M before peak), (2) dominant ringdown damping time extracted from post-merger signal, with frequencies and damping times treated as free parameters (not fixed to Kerr values), and (3) explicit modeling including the first overtone; data segments for temperature and decay time estimation are strictly separated to avoid circularity.

Result: The Bekenstein-Hod bound is satisfied at 3.3–3.6σ confidence for the primary analysis (t_< = −10M), robust across varying pre-merger cutoffs and overtone inclusion—surpassing the 91% CL (≈1.7σ) from GW150914.

Conclusion: The bound is observationally validated for a single astrophysical black hole with unprecedented precision, transforming an information-theoretic inequality into an empirical constraint on black hole relaxation dynamics.

Abstract: Black holes link gravity, thermodynamics and information via limits on the relaxation rate of a perturbed system. The Bekenstein-Hod bound imposes a minimum relaxation time at fixed temperature, but its observational test demands both black hole thermodynamic characterization and decay time measurement. Here we test this bound with GW250114, the loudest gravitational-wave signal yet observed from a binary black-hole merger. We infer the remnant temperature from pre-merger data truncated at least $10\,M$ before the peak and its longest-lived decay time from post-merger data, thereby avoiding direct reuse of the same strain samples. The ringdown frequencies and damping times are allowed to vary independently rather than being fixed to the Kerr spectrum. For the primary $t_{<}=-10\,M$ analysis, the bound is verified at $3.3-3.6σ$ across the focal ringdown start times, representing a substantial improvement over the $91\%$ confidence level set by GW150914. The conclusion remains robust under varied pre-merger cutoffs and explicit inclusion of the short-lived first overtone in waveform modelling. This separated-data measurement converts an information-theoretic relaxation bound into a precision test of a single astrophysical black hole.

</details>


### [101] [Polytropic wormholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16969)
*Remo Garattini,Emmanuel N. Saridakis,Athanasios G. Tzikas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores traversable wormholes in general relativity supported by polytropic matter—both homogeneous and inhomogeneous—deriving exact solutions, analyzing energy condition violations, and establishing a systematic link between polytropic parameters and wormhole geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify physically motivated equations of state for supporting traversable wormholes in GR, where exotic matter is required; polytropic EoS offers analytical tractability and physical relevance.

Method: Analytical derivation of wormhole solutions under polytropic equation of state p = ωρ^γ (homogeneous) and generalized inhomogeneous form p = ω(r)ρ^γ; analysis of geometry, flare-out condition, null/weak energy conditions, and construction of explicit solutions with power-law ω(r).

Result: Homogeneous case yields consistent interior geometry governed by constant ω, γ; inhomogeneous case gives geometry fully determined by ω(r), with odd-integer γ enforced for positive ω(r); power-law ω(r) ∝ r^α yields finite-support solutions; special case α = 2γ−3 produces 'absurdly benign' configurations where NEC violation is localized at the throat.

Conclusion: Polytropic matter—especially inhomogeneous forms—provides a flexible, systematic framework for constructing compact wormholes with controllable, localized exotic matter, bridging equation-of-state physics and geometric constraints.

Abstract: Traversable wormholes in general relativity require non-standard matter sources, making the identification of physically motivated equations of state particularly important.
