We investigate wormholes supported by a polytropic equation of state, considering homogeneous and inhomogeneous configurations within a unified framework. We derive the corresponding solutions and analyze the effects of the polytropic parameters on the geometry and energy conditions. In the homogeneous case, the polytropic construction yields a consistent wormhole interior whose geometry and matter content are governed by the constant polytropic parameters. For the inhomogeneous case, we obtain a general analytical expression showing that the geometry is completely determined by the radial polytropic coefficient $ω(r)$. For positive $ω(r)$, the requirement for physically meaningful solutions naturally restricts the polytropic exponent to odd integer values. Using a power-law profile, we construct explicit classes of solutions exhibiting distinct parameter regimes and finite radial support. Interestingly enough, for an exponent $α=2γ-3$, a generalized absurdly benign traversable wormhole-like configuration emerges naturally. Although the flare-out condition implies null-energy-condition violation at the throat, the inhomogeneous framework allows its radial distribution to be controlled. Our results establish a systematic connection between polytropic matter and wormhole geometry, providing a flexible framework for constructing compact wormholes with localized exotic matter.

</details>


### [102] [Constants of motion and fundamental frequencies for elliptic orbits at fourth post-Newtonian order: aligned-spin contributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31072)
*David Trestini,Jan Steinhoff*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes spin-dependent contributions to the gauge-invariant conservative dynamics of eccentric, aligned-spin binary systems at fourth post-Newtonian (4PN) order, extending prior nonspinning results; it provides the full energy–angular momentum–frequency map, redshift/gyroscopic invariants, circular limits, ISCO frequency via Blanchet-Iyer-Favata criterion linked to periastron advance, and completes the 4PN spin-dependent scattering angle for unbound orbits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the gauge-invariant conservative dynamics of compact binaries to include all spin effects—up to quartic order in spin and including spin-deformability—at 4PN accuracy, enabling precise modeling of eccentric, aligned-spin systems for gravitational-wave astrophysics and fundamental tests of gravity.

Method: Analytic post-Newtonian computation using effective one-body and Hamiltonian formalisms; derivation of gauge-invariant relations between constants of motion (E, L_z) and orbital frequencies (Ω_r, Ω_φ); systematic inclusion of linear through quartic spin terms and spin-induced quadrupole (and higher multipole) deformability; application of the Blanchet-Iyer-Favata stability criterion to ISCO; extension to hyperbolic orbits for scattering angle.

Result: Complete 4PN gauge-invariant E–L_z–Ω_r–Ω_φ map for aligned-spin eccentric binaries including all spin multipoles up to quartic order and deformability parameters; derived redshift and gyroscopic invariants; explicit circular-orbit links; identification of a deep connection between the Blanchet-Iyer-Favata ISCO condition and periastron advance; full 4PN spin-dependent scattering angle for unbound orbits.

Conclusion: The 4PN conservative dynamics of aligned-spin binaries is now fully characterized in a gauge-invariant framework, enabling high-precision comparisons with numerical relativity and gravitational-wave data, and revealing novel structural links between orbital stability, periastron precession, and scattering observables.

Abstract: For eccentric systems with aligned spins, we compute all spin contributions to the gauge-invariant conservative map between the constants of motion (energy and angular momentum) and the fundamental (radial and azimuthal) frequencies at the fourth post-Newtonian order. This extends the nonspinning map derived in [arXiv:2511.10735] to include terms that are linear, quadratic, cubic, and quartic in spin, as well as the associated spin-deformability parameters. We also obtain derived quantities such as the redshift and gyroscopic invariants, as well as circular links. We notice that the Blanchet-Iyer-Favata post-Newtonian stability criterion that determines the dimensionless frequency of the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) is in fact intricately related to the periastron advance for circular orbits. Finally, in the case of unbound orbits, we complete the 4PN scattering angle with all spin contributions.

</details>


### [103] [Evolution of Cosmic String Loops under Gravitational Backreaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31163)
*Lasse Gerblich,Richard A. Battye,E. P. S. Shellard*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how gravitational backreaction modifies the gravitational wave emission from cosmic string loops with cusps, finding that while cusps persist locally, their bursts are weakened and acquire a high-frequency cutoff, reducing detectability in current and future GW detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how gravitational backreaction affects cusp-driven gravitational wave bursts from Nambu-Goto cosmic string loops—crucial for accurate interpretation of GW search results.

Method: Reformulates the Nambu-Goto equations in a convenient gauge to self-consistently include the loop’s dynamically sourced gravitational field, enabling the first continuous numerical evolution of loops under full gravitational backreaction.

Result: Cusps survive locally under backreaction, but the emitted gravitational waveform is significantly altered: cusp bursts are weakened and exhibit a high-frequency cutoff at $f_c \propto (G\mu)^{-3/2}$, suppressing signal power above this frequency.

Conclusion: Gravitational backreaction substantially reduces the expected signal-to-noise ratio of cusp bursts in gravitational wave detectors compared to naive (no-backreaction) predictions, necessitating updated waveform models for robust cosmic string searches.

Abstract: Nambu-Goto cosmic string loops generically develop cusps, points where the string momentarily reaches the speed of light. These cusps produce strong gravitational wave bursts with a characteristic strain spectrum $\tilde{h}(ω)\propto (Gμ)\,ω^{-4/3}$, making them prime targets for gravitational wave searches, where $μ$ is the string mass per unit length. However, this picture is modified when one accounts for gravitational backreaction. Using a convenient gauge, we reformulate the Nambu-Goto equations of motion for a loop moving in its own dynamically sourced gravitational field, enabling the first continuous numerical evolution of loops under this backreaction. A key finding is that locally cusps survive backreaction. Nevertheless, the gravitational waveform calculated in this formalism is significantly modified, weakening the cusp burst and introducing a high-frequency cutoff at $f_c\propto(Gμ)^{-3/2}$. This suppression above $f_c$ reduces the expected signal-to-noise ratio in current and near-future detectors relative to unperturbed waveform predictions.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [104] [Reimagining Gravity: Generalized Symmetries, Double Copy, and Spinning Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28736)
*Joon-Hwi Kim*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This dissertation addresses three open problems in gravity—generalized symmetries in dynamical GR as an EFT, field-theoretic origin of the double copy, and equations of motion for spinning black holes in external fields—using three complementary perspectives: Lorentz-gauge one-form symmetry (Part I), diffeomorphism-gauge / color-kinematics duality (Part II), and self-dual/anti-self-dual decomposition (Part III), yielding a new self-dual black hole metric, a refined interpretation of Kerr as a double copy of Taub-NUT, and a chiral Lagrangian framework for spinning black hole amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve three foundational open questions: (1) existence of generalized symmetries in dynamical gravity as a low-energy effective field theory; (2) field-theoretic explanation of the tree-level double copy between GR and Yang-Mills; (3) exact equations of motion and Lagrangians for 4D spinning black holes in external fields within point-particle effective theory.

Method: Three distinct theoretical frameworks are deployed: (I) Gravity as Lorentz-gauge theory to construct a one-form global symmetry; (II) Gravity as diffeomorphism-gauge theory linked to color-kinematics duality and double copy; (III) 4D gravity decomposed into self-dual (SD) and anti-self-dual (ASD) sectors, applied to derive black hole metrics (via classical double copy and reinterpretation of Newman-Janis), construct SD black hole solutions, formulate a dynamical probe version of Newman-Janis, and develop a chiral Lagrangian formalism for Compton amplitudes of spinning black holes.

Result: A Lorentz one-form symmetry is established in dynamical gravity; Kerr is shown to be a precise double copy of SD and ASD Taub-NUT solutions, elevating Newman-Janis to a rigorous derivation; a novel self-dual black hole metric is obtained; a dynamical probe generalization of Newman-Janis is proposed; and a chiral Lagrangian framework yields spinning black hole Compton amplitudes with correct factorization and no spurious poles across all helicities.

Conclusion: The three perspectives collectively demonstrate that structural insights from gauge theory, duality, and chiral geometry are essential to unify conceptual understanding of symmetries, scattering, and dynamics in quantum and classical gravity—particularly for spinning black holes—and provide concrete tools to advance effective theories of compact objects in gravitational-wave physics.

Abstract: General relativity is a century-old subject. Yet modern explorations through generalized symmetries, scattering amplitudes, and effective field theory have raised open problems, motivating a reassessment of conventional views on gravitation, spacetime, and spin. First, do generalized symmetries exist in dynamical gravity as a low-energy EFT? Second, is there a field-theoretic explanation for the tree-level double copy relationship between general relativity and Yang-Mills theory? Third, what are the exact equations of motion or Lagrangians of four-dimensional spinning black holes in external fields, in point-particle effective theory? In this dissertation, three different perspectives on gravity are developed to shed light on each of these puzzles.
  In Part I, we view gravity as a gauge theory of Lorentz group to establish a one-form symmetry of dynamical gravity. In Part II, we investigate how far gravity can be viewed as a gauge theory of diffeomorphisms, in connection with color-kinematics duality and double copy. In Part III, we view four-dimensional gravity as a nonlinear interaction between self-dual and anti-self-dual parts. We apply this view to the derivation of four-dimensional spinning black hole solutions as well as their dynamics in point-particle effective theory. First, we derive a new self-dual black hole metric from classical double copy and show that the Kerr metric represents a pair of self-dual and anti-self-dual Taub-NUT solutions, elevating the Newman-Janis algorithm to a rigorous derivation. Second, we propose a dynamical probe counterpart of Newman-Janis algorithm. We give a Lagrangian derivation of spinning black hole Compton amplitudes that exhibit correct factorizations and are free of spurious poles for all helicity configurations while developing a chiral formalism that manifests and maximally utilizes the simplicity of the self-dual sector.

</details>


### [105] [Carroll-Cotton Tensors and Gravitational Radiation at Null Infinity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28741)
*Adrien Fiorucci,Simon Pekar,P. Marios Petropoulos,Matthieu Vilatte*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces Cotton tensors on conformal Carroll geometries via gauging the conformal Carroll algebra, yielding a conformally covariant measure of curvature deviation; it serves as a geometric descriptor of gravitational radiation at null infinity, superseding the Bondi news by eliminating the need for supplementary Geroch-tensor data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define a geometrically intrinsic, fully covariant quantity characterizing gravitational radiative degrees of freedom at the conformal boundary of asymptotically flat spacetimes—addressing limitations of the Bondi news, which requires non-geometric supplementary data (the Geroch tensor).

Method: Gauging the conformal Carroll algebra to construct first-principles Cotton tensors on conformal Carroll geometries; leveraging representation theory and Cartan geometry to ensure conformal covariance and geometric naturality.

Result: A well-defined, conformally covariant Cotton tensor on conformal Carroll manifolds; shown to encode gravitational wave emission as deviations from stationarity without auxiliary data—unlike Bondi news, which intrinsically depends on the Geroch tensor for full covariance.

Conclusion: Carroll-Cotton tensors are the natural, fully geometric observables for gravitational radiation at null infinity, providing a cleaner, more fundamental characterization than Bondi news and enabling a purely Carrollian description of asymptotic symmetry dynamics and memory effects.

Abstract: We provide a first-principles definition of Cotton tensors on conformal Carroll geometries by gauging the conformal Carroll algebra. On the mathematical side, this analysis yields a conformally covariant object designed to quantify deviations from conformal flatness in Carrollian geometries. On the physical side, it offers a powerful geometric tool to describe gravitational radiative degrees of freedom at the conformal boundary of four-dimensional asymptotically flat spacetimes. Unlike the Bondi news, whose fully-covariant definition requires amendment by supplementary data that we identify with the Geroch tensor, the Carroll-Cotton tensors offer the natural geometric objects to quantify deviations from stationarity due to the emission of gravitational waves.

</details>


### [106] [OPE and correlation functions in a generally covariant form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28743)
*Arpit Das,Anatoly Konechny,Naveen Balaji Umasankar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Euclidean conformal field theories (CFTs) on curved D≥3 dimensional manifolds, extending scalar OPE constructions on conformally flat spaces and refining the short-distance ansatz for three-point functions on non-conformally flat spaces to ensure local covariant OPE existence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand how Euclidean CFTs behave on generic curved backgrounds—especially beyond conformally flat spaces—by ensuring consistency of operator product expansion (OPE) and short-distance limits with local covariance and geometric constraints.

Method: 1) Generalized descendant construction for scalar-channel OPE on conformally flat spaces using representation theory and conformal symmetry; 2) Ambient space formalism applied to non-conformally flat geometries; 3) Systematic analysis of short-distance asymptotics of three-point functions via Parisini–Skenderis–Withers ansatz, identifying missing curvature-dependent corrections required for OPE locality and covariance.

Result: A complete descendant contribution formula for scalar OPE on conformally flat spaces; identification of necessary curvature-coupled correction terms to the PSW ansatz for three-point functions on generic curved backgrounds; demonstration that these corrections are essential for a well-defined, local, and covariant OPE.

Conclusion: The OPE in Euclidean CFTs on curved spaces requires careful incorporation of background curvature effects—even at leading short-distance order—and the ambient space formalism provides a robust framework to derive consistent, covariant OPE structures beyond conformally flat cases.

Abstract: In this paper we consider some general aspects of Euclidean conformal field theories on curved spaces of dimension $D\ge 3$. We first look at the OPE of scalar primary fields on conformally flat spaces. Extending the results of \cite{Konechny:2026bqg}, we give a general construction of the descendants' contributions to the OPE in the scalar channel. We then discuss CFTs on non-conformally flat spaces in the ambient space formalism. We investigate the short-distance behaviour of three-point functions on such spaces using the general ansatz proposed by Parisini, Skenderis, and Withers \cite{Parisini:2022wkb, Parisini:2023nbd}. We find that some additional corrections need to be added to the ansatz to ensure the existence of a local covariant OPE.

</details>


### [107] [Holographic warped CFTs from General Relativity on AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28751)
*Kristiansen Lara,Ricardo Troncoso*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces new boundary conditions for 3D Einstein gravity with negative cosmological constant, yielding a canonical Virasoro ⊕ u(1) Kac-Moody algebra without charge redefinitions; it links warped modular S-transformation to diffeomorphism between Euclidean BTZ and thermal AdS₃, and derives an exact warped Cardy formula matching Bekenstein-Hawking entropy with Euclidean AdS₃ as ground state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically motivated, mathematically consistent boundary conditions for 3D asymptotically AdS gravity that yield a standard Virasoro ⊕ u(1) algebra without artificial redefinitions of charges, and to clarify the microscopic origin of black hole entropy via a warped CFT interpretation.

Method: Hamiltonian analysis of asymptotic symmetries; derivation of canonical boundary charges and their Poisson algebra; imposition of diffeomorphism equivalence between Euclidean BTZ and thermal AdS₃ to fix modular transformation properties of chemical potentials; application of the warped Cardy formula with ground state identified as Euclidean AdS₃.

Result: A new set of boundary conditions yielding the semidirect sum Virasoro ⊕ u(1) algebra in standard form; warped modular S-transformation emerges naturally from requiring Euclidean BTZ and thermal AdS₃ to be diffeomorphic; the warped Cardy formula exactly reproduces the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy; explicit comparison confirms distinctness from Brown-Henneaux and Compere-Song-Strominger boundary conditions.

Conclusion: The proposed boundary conditions provide a self-consistent holographic setup for warped CFT duals in 3D gravity, where entropy counting is precise and geometrically grounded—establishing Euclidean AdS₃ as the natural vacuum and validating the warped Cardy formula as a first-principles entropy derivation.

Abstract: We introduce a new set of boundary conditions for three-dimensional Einstein gravity with negative cosmological constant. The canonical generators plainly realize the semidirect sum of Virasoro and $u(1)$ Kac-Moody algebras in its standard form, without the need of additional redefinitions of the global charges. We show that requiring Euclidean BTZ and thermal AdS$_3$ to be diffeomorphic precisely induces the warped modular $S$-transformation of the chemical potentials. The corresponding warped Cardy formula then exactly reproduces the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, where the ground state is identified as Euclidean AdS$_3$. An explicit comparison with the boundary conditions of Brown-Henneaux and Compere-Song-Strominger is also addressed.

</details>


### [108] [Action-angle variables and phase space formulation of Hermitian matrix models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28942)
*Arghya Chattopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a phase space description for large-N Hermitian matrix models using orthogonal polynomial recursions, linking semiclassical action-angle variables to eigenvalue-momentum variables; it validates the framework in one-cut (including Gaussian) and symmetric quartic two-cut phases, and sketches its extension to multicut (finite-gap) cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a direct, recursion-based phase space formulation for large-N matrix models—bypassing traditional saddle-point or loop-equation methods—and unify perspectives from orthogonal polynomials, Wigner transforms, and spectral geometry.

Method: Derives semiclassical action-angle variables from the Jacobi (three-term) recursion lattice of orthogonal polynomials; maps them canonically to eigenvalue-momentum phase space; computes momentum profile via zero density of polynomials; cross-validates with Wigner transform of Christoffel-Darboux kernel and planar spectral curve; extends to two-cut case using period-two recursion and Bloch band analysis.

Result: A consistent phase space structure emerges: in the one-cut phase, momentum is determined by zero density and matches Wigner and spectral curve predictions; in the symmetric quartic two-cut phase, two disjoint phase space components arise, each with action equal to a partial 't Hooft coupling; finite-gap generalization is outlined for multicuts.

Conclusion: Orthogonal polynomial recursions provide a fundamental, unifying foundation for phase space quantization in matrix models; the resulting semiclassical geometry encodes both eigenvalue statistics and topological data (e.g., 't Hooft couplings), suggesting deep links between integrability, random matrices, and quantum spectral curves.

Abstract: We develop a phase space description of large $N$ Hermitian one matrix models directly from the recursions satisfied by the orthogonal polynomials. In the one-cut phase, the recursion lattice naturally gives rise to a semiclassical action-angle pair, which can be mapped canonically to eigenvalue and momentum variables. We show that the resulting momentum profile is determined by the density of zeros of the orthogonal polynomials, and that the same phase space structure is reproduced from the Wigner transform of the Christoffel-Darboux projector and from the planar spectral curve. The Gaussian model provides a simple consistency check of the proposal. We then extend the construction to the symmetric quartic two-cut phase, where a period-two Jacobi recursion produces two Bloch bands and two disconnected phase space components with actions given by the partial 't Hooft couplings. Finally, we outline the finite-gap generalization appropriate to multicut phases.

</details>


### [109] [Flat connections on moduli spaces I: Local (1,0)-extension of the DHS connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29169)
*Eric D'Hoker,Benjamin Enriquez,Oliver Schlotterer,Federico Zerbini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper initiates a program to extend the smooth, modular-invariant flat DHS connection $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ — defined on the configuration space of $n$ points on a genus-$h$ Riemann surface $\Sigma$ and valued in $\hat{\mathfrak t}_{h,n}$ — to a global flat connection on the full Teichmüller space $\mathcal T_{h,n}$, valued in $\mathrm{Der}(\hat{\mathfrak t}_{h,n})$. It isolates and solves, locally and explicitly, the system governing the holomorphic component $\mathcal L$ of this extension in adapted coordinates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a global flat connection on the Teichmüller space $\mathcal T_{h,n}$ that extends the known flat DHS connection $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ (which lives only on the fiber over a fixed complex structure) while respecting modularity and encoding variations of complex structure — thereby unifying conformal field theory data across moduli space.

Method: Uses coordinate adaptation to the fibration $\mathcal T_{h,n} \to \mathcal T_h$, decomposes the sought connection into three components ($\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$, holomorphic $\mathcal L$, anti-holomorphic), derives the integrability (flatness) conditions restricting $\mathcal L$, and solves them explicitly in local holomorphic coordinates on $\mathcal T_h$.

Result: The system of differential equations satisfied by the holomorphic piece $\mathcal L$ is fully isolated; its local, explicit solution is obtained. This constitutes the first essential step toward the global flat extension of $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ to $\mathcal T_{h,n}$.

Conclusion: The paper establishes the existence and local explicit form of the holomorphic component $\mathcal L$ required for extending $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ to a flat connection on $\mathcal T_{h,n}$. The global construction and extension of related meromorphic connections are deferred to subsequent papers.

Abstract: The flat DHS connection $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ constructed in arXiv:2602.01461 is smooth on the configuration space of $n$ points on a fixed compact Riemann surface $Σ$ of arbitrary genus $h$, takes values in an infinite-dimensional Lie algebra $\hat{\mathfrak t}_{h,n}$ and is invariant under the modular group $\mathrm{Sp}(2h,\mathbb Z)$. This paper is the first in a series for a program whose goal is to extend the connection $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ to a global flat connection on the Teichmüller space $\mathcal T_{h,n}$ valued in the Lie algebra of derivations of $\hat{\mathfrak t}_{h,n}$. Upon the choice of local coordinates adapted to the map $\mathcal T_{h,n}\to \mathcal T_h$, such a connection splits into three pieces: $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$, a piece $\mathcal L$ corresponding to holomorphic directions of $\mathcal T_h$ and a third piece corresponding to anti-holomorphic directions in $\mathcal T_h$. In this paper, we isolate the system of equations satisfied by $\mathcal L$ and obtain its solution locally and explicitly. The construction of a global extension of $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ to $\mathcal T_{h,n}$ and the extension of the meromorphic connection of arXiv:1112.0864 to $\mathcal T_{h,n}$, are relegated to future publications in this series.

</details>


### [110] [One-loop renormalization group flow of the translation-invariant noncommutative Yukawa theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29213)
*Karim Bouchachia,Smain Kouadik*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the one-loop renormalization group flow of a noncommutative pseudoscalar Yukawa theory on 4D Euclidean Moyal space, showing its one-loop renormalizability and solving the coupled beta-function system analytically via symmetry-adapted variables and an exact invariant; it reveals UV restoration and IR amplification of Moyal ordering symmetry, with dynamical suppression of a non-planar IR singularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish renormalizability and understand the RG dynamics of noncommutative Yukawa theories—specifically how the two inequivalent Moyal-star vertex orderings affect coupling evolution and IR behavior—addressing open questions about symmetry restoration, singularity structure, and comparison to commutative limits.

Method: One-loop perturbative renormalization on 4D Euclidean Moyal space; derivation and analysis of coupled beta functions for two independent Yukawa couplings $g_1$, $g_2$; analytic integration via variable decoupling ($u = g_1^2 + g_2^2$, $v = g_1^2 - g_2^2$), Riccati reduction on symmetric surface, and identification of exact invariant $\mathcal{I} = (g_1 g_2)^3 / (g_1^2 - g_2^2)^4$; closed-form solution of dimensionful parameters ($M^2$, $m$, $a^2$) along symmetric critical trajectory.

Result: The theory is proven one-loop renormalizable; the beta-function system is solved exactly; the ratio $r = v/u$ exhibits UV-attractive symmetric fixed surface ($r=0$) and IR-attractive asymmetric fixed points ($r=\pm1$); the non-planar $1/(\theta^2 p^2)$ coefficient is dynamically suppressed as $p \to 0$, though the singularity persists; dimensionful couplings are solved in closed form on the symmetric trajectory.

Conclusion: The Moyal ordering asymmetry is not fine-tuned but governed by robust RG dynamics: symmetry between orderings is restored at high energies and enhanced at low energies; this leads to infrared suppression of the non-planar divergence's amplitude, suggesting that while the $1/(\theta^2 p^2)$ singularity requires additional IR regularization (e.g., soft breaking or supersymmetry), its physical impact is mitigated by RG flow.

Abstract: We study the one-loop renormalization group flow of the translation-invariant noncommutative pseudoscalar Yukawa theory on four-dimensional Euclidean Moyal space. The two inequivalent orderings of the Moyal-star Yukawa vertex give rise to two independent couplings $g_{1}$ and $g_{2}$, whose beta functions form a coupled nonlinear system; every one-loop divergence is absorbed by a counterterm already present in the action, establishing one-loop renormalizability of the theory. We solve the one-loop system analytically: it decouples in the variables $u=g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}$ and $v=g_{1}^{2}-g_{2}^{2}$, the quartic coupling is obtained by a Riccati reduction on the symmetric surface $g_{1}=g_{2}$, and the generic asymmetric flow is reduced to a single quadrature by the exact invariant $\mathcal{I}=(g_{1}g_{2})^{3}/(g_{1}^{2}-g_{2}^{2})^{4}$. Two dynamical consequences follow. The ratio $r=v/u$ has a UV-attractive symmetric surface $r=0$ and IR-attractive asymmetric directions $r=\pm1$, so that the flow restores the symmetry between the two Moyal orderings towards the ultraviolet and amplifies any initial ordering asymmetry towards the infrared, as a fractional power of the logarithm. In consequence the Yukawa-induced coefficient of the non-planar $1/(θ^{2}p^{2})$ structure is dynamically suppressed towards $p\rightarrow 0$; the singularity itself is not removed, and remains the task of the IR-improving term. We also solve the dimensionful sector ($M^{2}$, $m$, $a^{2}$) in closed form on the symmetric critical trajectory, and compare throughout with the commutative theory.

</details>


### [111] [Inequalities and Positivity in Modular Flowed Entanglement Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29329)
*Liangyu Chen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the modular entanglement shift (MES)—the change in entanglement entropy under modular flow—and proves an exact entropy balance: MES_A + MES_B = mutual information generated. In 2D CFT vacuum with two disjoint intervals, symmetry enforces MES_A = MES_B ≥ 0, shown via conformal transport and Araki relative entropy; the modular-flow orbit is identified with a Connes cocycle orbit, suggesting AdS/CFT applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how entanglement entropy evolves under state-dependent modular flow—a physically distinct alternative to fixed quantum channels—and to explore implications for quantum information, QFT, and holography.

Method: Analytical derivation using modular theory, conformal symmetry (including global conformal involution), Araki relative entropy, and Connes cocycle formalism; applied to tensor-product initial states evolved under their own modular Hamiltonian in 2D CFT vacuum.

Result: 1) Exact entropy balance: MES_A + MES_B = I(A:B) along the orbit ⇒ sum ≥ 0; 2) For two disjoint intervals in 2D CFT vacuum, MES_A = MES_B ≥ 0, proven both via conformally transported regulator and intrinsically via Araki relative entropy; 3) Modular-flow orbit identified as a Connes cocycle orbit.

Conclusion: Modular evolution induces signed but balanced entanglement shifts; symmetry and structure of 2D CFT enforce nonnegativity of individual MESs, revealing deep connections between modular theory, relative entropy, and holography.

Abstract: The modular Hamiltonian plays an important role in quantum information theory, quantum field theory, and the AdS/CFT correspondence. In this paper, we study a specific dynamical setup: starting from the tensor product of the reduced density matrices of a reference state, we evolve this product state under the modular flow generated by the reference state itself. We then investigate the resulting change in the entanglement entropy of a given region, which we call the modular entanglement shift (MES). Unlike evolution under a fixed quantum channel, this modular evolution obeys no general principle requiring the MES to have a definite sign. For a bipartition into subsystems A and B, we establish an exact entropy balance: the sum of the MESs for A and B equals the mutual information generated along the orbit. Consequently, their sum is nonnegative, even though either individual shift may be negative. For two disjoint intervals in the vacuum of a two-dimensional conformal field theory, however, we prove that a global conformal involution exchanging the intervals forces the two MESs to be equal. It follows that each MES is nonnegative along the modular flow. We establish this result both using a conformally transported regulator and intrinsically in terms of Araki relative entropy. Finally, we identify the modular-flow orbit with a Connes cocycle orbit and discuss possible applications to the AdS/CFT correspondence.

</details>


### [112] [Latent Geometry and the Emergence of Lorentzian Time in Matrix Quantum Mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29686)
*Badis Ydri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'latent geometry' in BFSS/BMN matrix models, linking emergent spacetime geometry to a special N=2 sector via Gaussianization and Molien–Weyl projection; at N=2, the same oscillator admits dual AdS₂ and dS₂ decodings, enabling spacetime transmutation and Lorentzian time emergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how classical spacetime geometry emerges from quantum matrix models—specifically, how geometric information persists across phase transitions (e.g., geometric transition) and how Lorentzian signature and time can arise intrinsically in purely Euclidean or algebraic frameworks.

Method: Uses large-d Gaussianization, dimensional reduction, finite-N Gaussianization, and exact Molien–Weyl invariant projection to isolate and encode geometry in the exceptional N=2 sector associated with a baby fuzzy sphere (single spin-1/2 Pauli block); analyzes unitary decodings of the BFSS₂ oscillator for N=2.

Result: Demonstrates that latent geometric data survives evaporation at the geometric transition and is fully encoded in the N=2 sector; shows that BFSS₂ at N=2 supports inequivalent unitary realizations corresponding to hyperbolic (EAdS²_θ) and de Sitter (dS²_θ) geometries—hence realizing spacetime transmutation and intrinsic emergence of Lorentzian time.

Conclusion: Latent geometry is a robust, algebraically encoded feature of BFSS/BMN models, surviving non-geometric phases; its decoding at minimal N=2 reveals that Lorentzian structure—and thus time—is not fundamental but emergent via symmetry-breaking choices in the operator algebra.

Abstract: We introduce \emph{latent geometry} in BFSS/BMN models. Before an emergent geometry evaporates at the geometric transition, large-$d$ Gaussianization encodes its information in the exceptional $N=2$ sector selected by the baby fuzzy sphere, a single active spin-$1/2$ Pauli block. Dimensional reduction, finite-$N$ Gaussianization and exact Molien--Weyl projection transport this encoding to mass-deformed BFSS$_2$. At $N=2$, the same stable BFSS$_2$ oscillator admits inequivalent unitary $\mathbf{H}^2_θ=\mathbf{EAdS}^2_θ$ and $\mathbf{dS}^2_θ$ decodings, realizing spacetime transmutation and the emergence of Lorentzian time.

</details>


### [113] [Bound States in Perturbative Quantum Gravity with Hydrogen-like Degeneracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29402)
*Callum R. T. Jones,Shruti Paranjape,Marcos Skowronek*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates conditions for conservation of a hidden Laplace-Runge-Lenz (LRL) vector in two-body bound states across effective field theories with massless mediators of spins 0–2; finds classical precession absence is generic, but quantum degeneracy preservation requires a precise cancellation between gravitons and six Majorana gravitinos—suggesting special relevance of N=6 supergravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine when relativistic two-body systems with long-range interactions mediated by massless particles (spins 0, 1/2, 1, 3/2, 2) conserve a generalized Laplace-Runge-Lenz vector—both classically (no orbital precession) and quantum mechanically (hydrogen-like spectral degeneracy)—motivated by recent gravitational scattering amplitude results hinting at exact LRL conservation in highly symmetric systems.

Method: Performs a systematic EFT analysis of long-range potentials generated by exchange of massless mediators up to spin-2; extends relativistic post-Minkowskian potential matching to include quantum corrections; incorporates novel long-range forces from pairs of massless fermions; computes classical precession and quantum bound-state spectra (e.g., level degeneracy) for generic couplings and mediator spins.

Result: Classical absence of orbital precession occurs generically for scalar and vector mediators (including Kaluza-Klein theories), but quantum mechanical hydrogen-like degeneracy is fragile—it only survives when contributions from gravitons and exactly six Majorana gravitinos cancel precisely; this points to N=6 supergravity as a distinguished candidate theory.

Conclusion: Exact LRL conservation is not generic quantum mechanically despite being common classically; the required cancellation identifies N=6 supergravity as a unique setting where both classical integrability and quantum degeneracy coexist, suggesting deep connections between hidden symmetries, UV completion, and maximal supersymmetry in gravity.

Abstract: Recent results in the study of gravitational scattering amplitudes indicate that some highly-symmetric relativistic systems may exactly conserve a version of the Laplace-Runge-Lenz (LRL) vector in two-body bound states. We make a systematic study, in the context of a generic EFT of long-range interactions due to the exchange of massless mediator particles of spins 0, $\frac{1}{2}$, 1, $\frac{3}{2}$, 2, of the conditions for the conservation of a hidden LRL vector; both classically (no orbital precession) and quantum mechanically (hydrogen-like degeneracy of bound states). The calculations require several new technical developments including the extension of relativistic post-Minkowskian potential matching to quantum corrections and the incorporation of long-range forces due to the exchange of pairs of massless fermions. We find that while classically the absence of precession is a generic property of a large class of models, including Kaluza-Klein theories, with vector and scalar exchanges, maintaining the degeneracy quantum mechanically requires a surprising cancellation between gravitons and 6 Majorana gravitinos, hinting at a special role for $\mathcal{N}=6$ supergravity.

</details>


### [114] [From Self-Dual to Physical $\mathbb{C}P^{N-1}$: Anomalies, Boundary Stokes Phenomenon, and Global Structure of $θ$-vacua](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30160)
*Yui Hayashi,Mithat Ünsal*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a two-coupling generalization of the CP^{N−1} model to interpolate between self-dual and physical theories, revealing a chiral imbalance interpreted as an imaginary-θ topological deformation; it establishes exact quantum equivalence via a bosonic chiral anomaly–sourced counterterm, solves the deformed theory at large N, determines the nonperturbative self-dual vacuum with field-strength condensate, and resolves a saddle-point paradox via boundary Lefschetz thimbles and a boundary Stokes phenomenon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve a fundamental paradox in the large-N expansion of strongly coupled CP^{N−1}-like theories—where unphysical saddles with θ + 2πn ∼ O(N) erroneously dominate the ground state—and to provide a controlled nonperturbative framework bridging self-dual and physical theories via topological deformation.

Method: Introduces a two-parameter (g, ε) deformation of the CP^{N−1} model; identifies chiral imbalance as imaginary-θ deformation; derives quantum equivalence condition requiring a topological counterterm tied to bosonic chiral anomaly; performs large-N analysis; applies Lefschetz thimble decomposition generalized to include boundary thimbles; analyzes boundary Stokes phenomenon to suppress spurious saddles.

Result: 1. Analytic determination of the self-dual vacuum structure featuring a dynamically generated field-strength condensate; 2. Resolution of the saddle-point paradox: boundary thimbles and boundary Stokes phenomenon render O(N)-shifted θ-saddles topologically inactive, restoring consistency of the large-N expansion.

Conclusion: The two-coupling deformation provides a rigorous nonperturbative tool for CP^{N−1} physics; quantum equivalence mandates a topological counterterm from bosonic chiral anomaly; the large-N solution confirms a self-dual vacuum and validates the expansion by eliminating pathological contributions via generalized thimble analysis.

Abstract: We introduce a two-coupling generalization of $\mathbb{C}P^{N-1}$ model that continuously interpolates between the self-dual ($ε=0$) and the physical ($ε=g$) theories as a useful nonperturbative tool. At $ε\neq g$, this model possesses a chiral imbalance, which may be viewed as a real topological deformation (imaginary-$θ$). We demonstrate that exact quantum equivalence between first- and second-order formulations strictly requires a topological counterterm sourced by a bosonic chiral anomaly. Solving this deformed theory at large $N$ yields two primary results. First, we analytically determine the nonperturbative vacuum structure of the self-dual theory, a self-dual vacuum with a dynamically generated field-strength condensate. Second, we resolve a fundamental paradox where saddles with $(θ+ 2πn) \sim O(N)$ ($n$ is branch number) spuriously yield lower energy densities than the physical ground state. Because the effective action possesses an essential singularity at $F=0$, we show that the Lefschetz thimble analysis must be generalized to include boundary thimbles. A boundary Stokes phenomenon renders the problematic saddles topologically inactive, fully restoring the validity of the large-$N$ expansion for strongly coupled theories.

</details>


### [115] [Teleparallel formulation of $E_{8(8)}$ Exceptional Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29541)
*Davide Rovere*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives the potential for E8(8) × ℝ⁺ exceptional field theory in a teleparallel formulation, using intrinsic torsion as the fundamental geometric object and linking it to the embedding tensor of gauged supergravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically transparent framework for studying 3D maximally supersymmetric compactifications of higher-dimensional supergravities, leveraging the role of intrinsic torsion and duality symmetry.

Method: Constructs the scalar potential from the most general quadratic contractions of intrinsic torsion invariant under generalized diffeomorphisms, then imposes invariance under infinitesimal rotations of the maximal compact subgroup SO(16) of E8(8).

Result: A fully E8(8) × ℝ⁺-covariant potential is obtained in teleparallel exceptional field theory, with intrinsic torsion geometrically identified as the embedding tensor of the resulting 3D gauged supergravity.

Conclusion: The teleparallel formulation elevates intrinsic torsion to a primary geometric variable, unifying the description of fluxes, geometry, and gauging in exceptional field theory and clarifying its connection to lower-dimensional effective theories.

Abstract: The potential of $E_{8(8)} \times \mathbb{R}^+$ exceptional field theory, suited for studying three-dimensional maximally supersymmetric compactifications of higher-dimensional supergravities, is derived in teleparallel formulation. This means that, starting from an ansatz of the most general combination of the quadratic scalar contractions of the intrinsic torsion, which is manifestly invariant under generalised diffeomorphisms, one selects the potential by imposing the invariance under infinitesimal rotation of the maximal compact subgroup of the duality group. The teleparallel formulation makes manifest at geometric level the preeminent role of the intrinsic torsion in studying compactifications of higher-dimensional supergravities, where the intrinsic torsion is identified with the embedding tensor of the resulting gauged supergravity.

</details>


### [116] [Higher-Spin dS/CFT beyond minimal type-A, and the doubled nature of de Sitter space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29594)
*Julian Lang,Yasha Neiman*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores holographic duality between free vector models and higher-spin gravity in 4D de Sitter space, introducing novel reality conditions that extend the duality to include odd spins and spinor (type-B) boundary fields; it proposes two complementary frameworks—antipodal identification 'parallel universes' and twistor-space Legendre duality—to reconcile dS’s two-boundary structure with Euclidean AdS’s one-boundary nature; supersymmetric unification fails under the required reality conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend higher-spin holography beyond type-A (scalar) duality to type-B (spinor) in de Sitter space, incorporate odd-spin bulk fields, and resolve the tension between dS’s two conformal boundaries and Euclidean AdS’s single boundary.

Method: Introduces new reality conditions compatible with higher-spin symmetry and a well-defined single-particle Hilbert-space norm in dS₄; constructs dual descriptions via antipodal identifications (even/odd) and twistor-space Legendre self-duality of the higher-spin partition function; analyzes consistency of supersymmetric extensions under these conditions.

Result: Type-A and type-B dualities are realized as complementary descriptions: either as distinct theories on even vs. odd antipodally identified dS slices, or as Legendre-dual formulations of a unified twistor-space theory in Euclidean AdS; the higher-spin partition function is shown to be self-Legendre-dual; supersymmetric unification with bulk fermions violates the required reality conditions.

Conclusion: Higher-spin holography in de Sitter space admits a consistent extension to type-B via tailored reality conditions and twistorial duality, reconciling geometric boundary counting across spacetimes, but does not admit a supersymmetric completion preserving those conditions.

Abstract: We study the holographic duality between free vector models and Higher-Spin Gravity in 4-dimensional de Sitter space. On both the bulk and boundary sides, we introduce new reality conditions, which allow us to include odd spins in the bulk, and to extend the duality from type-A to type-B (i.e. from boundary scalars to spinors). The new reality conditions are closely related to a higher-spin-symmetric Hilbert-space norm for the single-particle (super)multiplet in de Sitter space. We construct two alternative viewpoints on the type-A and type-B theories. In one view, the two co-exist in "parallel universes", specifically in even vs. odd antipodal identifications of de Sitter space. In the other view, type-A and type-B are Legendre-transformed descriptions of the same twistor-space theory in Euclidean AdS (this involves the observation that the higher-spin partition function is Legendre-dual to itself). In this way, higher-spin holography reconciles the two-boundary nature of de Sitter space with the one-boundary nature of Euclidean AdS. The supersymmetric theory that unifies type-A and type-B with bulk fermions fails to be consistent under the appropriate reality conditions.

</details>


### [117] [Examples of Gribov copies in the presence of planar boundaries, and an emergent boundary gauge invariance in Yang-Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30447)
*David Dudal,Luigi Rosa,Sebbe Stouten*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper constructs zero-modes of the Faddeev-Popov operator for Yang-Mills theory between parallel PEC/PMC plates, proving existence of finite-action Gribov copies and identifying emergent boundary gauge invariance—key for applying the Gribov-Zwanziger formalism to the non-Abelian Casimir effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish the presence of Gribov copies in confined Yang-Mills configurations with physical boundary conditions (PEC/PMC), and to identify structural symmetries (emergent boundary gauge invariance) essential for consistent non-perturbative treatment of the Casimir effect in QCD-like theories.

Method: Explicit construction of zero-modes of the Faddeev-Popov operator using an adapted Henyey ansatz, subject to Dirichlet (PEC) or Neumann (PMC) boundary conditions on parallel plates; analysis of residual gauge symmetries at boundaries.

Result: Existence proof of finite-action, finite-L₂-norm Gribov copies in bounded Yang-Mills theory; identification of an emergent boundary gauge invariance specific to both PEC and PMC configurations.

Conclusion: Gribov ambiguities persist under physically relevant confining boundaries, necessitating Gribov-Zwanziger–type treatments for non-perturbative studies of confinement-driven phenomena like the non-Abelian Casimir effect; the emergent boundary gauge invariance must be preserved in any such quantization scheme.

Abstract: First, we explicitly construct zero-modes of the Yang-Mills Faddeev-Popov operator in the presence of two parallel plates, with either perfect electric (PEC) or perfect magnetic (PMC) boundary conditions imposed. This establishes the existence of Gribov copies with finite action, and even finite L_2-norm, in Yang-Mills theory with non-trivial interfaces. We adapt Henyey's construction, although the boundary configuration imposes extra restrictions on the ansatz for the gauge field. Second, we discuss the phenomenon of an emergent boundary gauge invariance for PEC and PMC plates in Yang-Mills theory. This shall be important when applying the Gribov-Zwanziger procedure to parallel plate setups, of relevance for non-perturbatively studying the non-Abelian Casimir effect in the future.

</details>


### [118] [Genuine Multi-Entropy in Abelian Chern--Simons Theory: Exact Key-Ring Collapse and Its Breakdown for Generic Link States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29627)
*Yen-Cheng Chang,Kaberi Goswami,Norihiro Iizuka,Akihiro Miyata*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes genuine multi-entropy for Abelian Chern-Simons theory on link states, deriving exact Rényi multi-entropies for key-ring links and revealing arithmetic-dependent breakdowns of entanglement collapse in generic links.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure of genuine multipartite entanglement in topological quantum field theories, specifically whether Rényi multi-entropies reduce to lower-order quantities (e.g., tripartite information) universally or only under special topological/arithmetic conditions.

Method: Analytic computation of Rényi multi-entropy for U(1)_k Chern–Simons theory on link states using modular S-matrix and link invariants; exact derivation for key-ring links; numerical scanning over k ∈ [2,24] for generic 4-component links to test collapse at n = 2,3,4,5.

Result: For key-ring links: GM^{(4)}_n = I_{3,n} exactly for all n; GM^{(5)}_n is a linear combination of bipartite and tripartite Rényi entropies. For generic 4-component links: collapse fails generically beyond n=2; n=2 collapse holds for all k ∈ [2,24]; n=3 violations occur iff 3|k, with dependence on the 3-adic valuation of k; n=4,5 violations occur for all k scanned, with k-dependent rates.

Conclusion: The collapse of genuine multi-entropy onto lower-order information measures is not generic—it is topologically constrained (key-ring class) and arithmetically fragile, depending nontrivially on both Rényi index n and the prime-power structure of the Chern–Simons level k.

Abstract: We study genuine multi-entropy in Abelian $U(1)_k$ Chern-Simons theory. For key-ring link states, where only the linking numbers between one distinguished component $K$ and the remaining $\mathtt{q}-1$ components are nonzero, we derive an exact closed-form expression for the $\mathtt{q}$-partite Rényi multi-entropy for general $\mathtt{q}$, level $k$, and Rényi index $n$. For $\mathtt{q}=4$, this shows that the genuine multi-entropy $\mathrm{GM}^{(4)}_n$ collapses exactly onto the tripartite information $I_{3,n}$ for all $n$, while for $\mathtt{q}=5$ it is likewise completely determined, for all $n$, by a linear combination of tripartite and bipartite Rényi multi-entropies. We then go beyond the key-ring class and study general four-component link states with arbitrary pairwise linking numbers. A numerical scan over Chern--Simons levels $2\leq k\leq24$ shows that the all-$n$ collapse found analytically for key-ring states does not survive for generic link states. Remarkably, the collapse remains exact at $n=2$ for every level examined. At $n=3$, violations occur, within the scanned range, only when $3\mid k$, with a further dependence on the $3$-adic valuation of $k$. At $n=4$ and $n=5$, violations occur for every level examined, with rates that vary strongly with $k$. These results show that the breakdown is not controlled simply by the zero-divisor structure of composite $\mathbb Z_k$, but instead exhibits a nontrivial joint dependence on the Rényi index and the arithmetic structure of the Chern--Simons level.

</details>


### [119] [One-Loop Fluctuation Response Along a Constrained Noncommutative Modulus in the Lorentzian IIB Matrix Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29688)
*Tetsuyuki Muramatsu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically studies a one-dimensional modulus in the Lorentzian IIB matrix model at N=5, analyzing constrained classical solutions, one-loop quantum fluctuations (via Pfaffian of fermionic matrix), and higher-L SO(5) spectral probes; it finds that quantum fluctuation data and noncommutative invariants distinguish otherwise spectrally degenerate backgrounds, suggesting their relevance to dimensional dynamics—but without proving dimensional selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe whether microscopic noncommutative structure and quantum fluctuations in the Lorentzian IKKT matrix model encode information about emergent spacetime dimensionality—beyond what is visible in coarse quadratic spectral data like Lorentzian extent.

Method: Numerical continuation of a physical modulus within a fixed-charge (Q_ρ) constrained family of N=5 Lorentzian IIB matrix configurations; exact evaluation of the fermionic Pfaffian Pf M_F for one-loop fluctuations; construction of a Lorentz-representative-independent density one-form from boson–ghost–fermion measure; application of framed SO(5)-covariant higher-L spectral probes (L=1,2,3) to detect structural separation in 3+6 decomposition.

Result: 1) Classical action remains constant along the modulus due to stationary identity; 23 solutions satisfy this to high precision. 2) Lorentzian extent spectrum is numerically invariant, but noncommutative invariants and Pf M_F magnitude vary smoothly along the modulus. 3) The full fluctuation measure forms a density one-form—not a scalar—ensuring Lorentz independence. 4) Higher-L SO(5) probes show increasing 3+6 spectral separation with L, correlating strongly with Pf M_F variation—though this is frame-dependent and not an intrinsic observable.

Conclusion: Quantum fluctuations (encoded in Pf M_F) and higher-L structural probes provide fine-grained discrimination between classically indistinguishable backgrounds, indicating that noncommutative geometry and loop effects may carry dimensional-dynamical information—yet no mechanism for dynamical dimensional selection is established.

Abstract: We study a local constrained problem at matrix size $N=5$ in the Lorentzian IIB (IKKT) matrix model and numerically continue a one-dimensional physical modulus. For an exact fixed-$Q_ρ$ constrained solution family, the stationary identity implies constant classical action; the 23 accepted backgrounds realize this relation to the quoted precision. Across the sampled branch, the spectrum of the Lorentzian extent operator remains common to numerical precision while microscopic noncommutative invariants vary.
  We evaluate finite-dimensional one-loop fluctuations along this branch. At fixed bosonic background, the fermionic Grassmann integral is exact and gives $\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M_F$; its magnitude is invariant under color-gauge and connected proper-Lorentz transformations and varies along the modulus. The complete boson--ghost--fermion quotient measure defines a Lorentz-representative-independent density one-form rather than a scalar potential. Thus quantum fluctuation data distinguish backgrounds unresolved by the coarse quadratic spectrum.
  We also apply a framed higher-$L$ SO(5) structural probe. At the reference background, the ordered $3+6$ spectral separation is larger at $L=2,3$ than at $L=1$, and larger still in the canonical large-$L$ limit. This is a frame-defined diagnostic, not an intrinsic observable or a higher-$N$ solution. The Pfaffian and higher-$L$ responses are closely correlated along the sampled branch, which we treat only as descriptive. These results suggest that microscopic noncommutative structure and quantum fluctuations may contain information relevant to dimensional dynamics, without establishing dimensional selection.

</details>


### [120] [BMN Spread Complexity Across Phase Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29816)
*Dibakar Roychowdhury*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum state dynamics and spread complexity in the mass-deformed BMN matrix model with a U(1) charge at finite temperature and chemical potential, identifying three thermodynamic regimes—gapped (non-chaotic oscillatory), continuum (maximally chaotic with λ_K = π/β), and intermediate (sub-exponential crossover)—via cTFD modeling and Krylov complexity analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how emergent symmetries, mass gaps, and thermal effects jointly govern quantum chaos and complexity growth in gauge/gravity dual systems, particularly beyond standard TFD frameworks by incorporating chemical potential and global charge.

Method: Analyzes spread complexity using the charged Thermofield Double (cTFD) state in the strong-coupling regime; employs Krylov basis construction, Lanczos coefficient asymptotics, spectral moment analysis under grand canonical ensemble, KMS condition enforcement, and residue-based evaluation of retarded Green’s functions; includes symmetry-breaking analysis in Krylov space.

Result: Identifies three distinct dynamical regimes: (i) low-T gapped phase with oscillatory, non-chaotic complexity growth; (ii) high-T continuum phase with maximal scrambling (λ_K = π/β, saturating the bound); (iii) intermediate regime exhibiting sub-exponential crossover governed by b_n ∼ π/β n + γ√n; provides rigorous derivation of KMS-compatible cTFD structure and time-reversal breaking in Krylov space.

Conclusion: The interplay between mass gap, temperature, and chemical potential fundamentally reshapes quantum complexity evolution—transitioning from integrable-like confinement to universal chaos—and establishes the cTFD as a robust framework for charged, finite-density quantum gravity probes.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical behavior and spread complexity of quantum states within the mass-deformed BMN matrix model equipped with an emergent $U(1)$ global charge at finite temperature $β^{-1}$ and chemical potential $ν$. Focusing on the strong-coupling regime ($μ\gg 1$), we model the dynamics via a charged Thermofield Double (cTFD) state and trace the evolution of spread complexity across three distinct thermodynamic regimes. In the low-temperature gapped phase ($βμ\gg 1$), discrete mass-gap bound states dominate, trapping wavepacket dispersion and producing non-chaotic, oscillatory early-time growth. Conversely, in the high-temperature continuum phase ($βμ\ll 1$), thermal excitations overwhelm the mass gap, driving a transition to a continuous advection field that exhibits maximal chaotic scrambling with a Krylov Lyapunov exponent $λ_K = π/ β$ that saturates the universal bound. In the intermediate temperature regime ($βμ\sim 1, βν\sim 1$), the interplay between mass-gap bound states and the continuous thermal background induces a sub-leading correction to the Lanczos coefficients $b_n \sim \fracπβ n + γ\sqrt{n}$, governing a continuous sub-exponential crossover before full chaotic thermalization. Technical derivations regarding KMS boundary conditions, grand canonical spectral moments, residue analysis, and time-reversal symmetry breaking in Krylov space are detailed in four dedicated appendices.

</details>


### [121] [The warp factor of supersymmetric $D=11$ near-horizon geometries: single-point rigidity, the $Spin(7)$ perfect square, and global constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29879)
*Usman Kayani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a pointwise identity for Killing-spinor bilinears on a compact supersymmetric M-horizon section, yielding three key consequences: (1) single-point rigidity—vanishing warp factor and rotation form at one point implies vanishing flux and Ricci-flatness; (2) algebraic determination of the rotation one-form h via Spin(7) flux decomposition, revealing degenerate positivity of the warp factor Δ; (3) a pointwise 'budget' allocating the supersymmetry constant between non-staticity and Spin(7) flux mismatch, with implications for Komar angular momentum and no-go results for additional symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover structural constraints on supersymmetric horizons without assuming Killing spinor norm constancy or global spinorial conditions, leveraging intrinsic geometry of Killing-spinor bilinears on compact horizon sections.

Method: Analysis of Killing-spinor bilinears on a compact connected section S of a supersymmetric M-horizon; derivation and exploitation of a pointwise algebraic identity linking warp factor Δ, rotation one-form h, and spinor norms; Spin(7) representation-theoretic decomposition of the flux; use of bilinear identities to constrain geometry, flux, and symmetry properties.

Result: Three main results: (i) single-point rigidity theorem replacing global spinorial assumptions; (ii) explicit Spin(7)-based algebraic expression for h and degenerate quadratic form for Δ involving only w₇ and Y₇ components; (iii) pointwise conservation-like relation ('budget') bounding non-staticity and flux mismatch by the supersymmetry constant, plus integral constraints on Δ and Komar angular momentum, and two no-go theorems for hidden symmetries and extra isometries.

Conclusion: The unreduced bilinear identity provides a powerful local–global bridge in supersymmetric horizon geometry: it replaces strong global assumptions with pointwise data, reveals intrinsic Spin(7) flux structure, enforces quantitative trade-offs governed by supersymmetry, and rules out certain symmetry enhancements—thereby sharpening classification and rigidity results for M-theory horizons.

Abstract: On a compact connected section ${\cal S}$ of a supersymmetric $M$-horizon the Killing-spinor bilinears give a pointwise identity relating the warp factor $Δ$, the rotation one-form $h$ and the two spinor norms, with nothing assumed about the norm of the Killing spinor. We derive three consequences of that unreduced identity.
  First, a single-point rigidity theorem: if $Δ$ and $h$ vanish at one point of the section, then the flux vanishes identically, ${\cal S}$ is Ricci-flat and the horizon is $\mathbb{R}^{1,1}\times{\cal S}$. A single point replaces the usual global spinorial hypothesis.
  Second, the ${\rm Spin}(7)$ decomposition of the flux fixes $h$ algebraically and exhibits the invariant flux content of the scalar square $Δ=4Φ^2$ known in an adapted gauge,
  $Δ=\frac{1}{108}\|w_{\bf 7}\pmσ({\cal Y}_{\bf 7})\|^2$.
  Only the two ${\bf 7}$-summands $w_{\bf 7}$, ${\cal Y}_{\bf 7}$ reach the warp factor, and the square is degenerate rather than definite: it vanishes on the linear subspace $w_{\bf 7}=\mpσ({\cal Y}_{\bf 7})$ rather than at the origin, so positivity of $Δ$ yields no case list.
  Third, the two combine into a pointwise budget: the single constant of supersymmetry is shared between the deviation from staticity and the ${\rm Spin}(7)$ mismatch of the flux, each bounded by that constant.
  The same identities characterise the constancy hypothesis, equivalent to $V=-fh$ for the bilinear one-form $V$, which fails on the static branch of known warped $AdS_2$ solutions; they fix a weighted integral of the warp factor; and they reduce the Komar angular momentum of the horizon to a positive bilinear integral. Two no-go statements, for hidden symmetries built from the Killing spinor and for a second isometry from its bilinears, are stated with their hypotheses.

</details>


### [122] [Carrollian Wave Equations for Arbitrary Spin: Anyons and the Exotic Particle on the Noncommutative Plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29931)
*Mauricio Valenzuela*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs the Carrollian limit of the Cortés--Plyushchay wave equations for planar anyons, yielding a first-order linear system for Carrollian anyons—particles with arbitrary real spin—and recovers Carrollian bosons/fermions at (half-)integer spins. It identifies a square-root structure of the Carroll wave equation, reveals flat-energy spectra with ±rest energy branches, and connects Galilean and Carroll limits via a shared similarity transformation, highlighting their distinct treatment of negative-energy modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically derive and characterize the non-relativistic Carrollian limit of relativistic anyon wave equations, extending known Galilean anyon frameworks to the ultra-relativistic (c → 0) regime and uncovering novel algebraic and geometric structures—including exotic Carroll symmetry and noncommutative configuration space.

Method: Performs a rigorous Carrollian contraction of the Cortés--Plyushchay wave equations by grading spin-tower components in powers of c and taking c → 0 at fixed rest energy; analyzes the resulting first-order system, its algebraic symmetries (including the exotic Carroll algebra with two central charges), and compares it with the Galilean limit via a common similarity transformation.

Result: Derives a first-order linear wave system for Carrollian anyons of arbitrary real spin; shows it squares to the standard second-order Carroll wave equation (∂_t² + E²)Φ = 0 with doubly degenerate flat spectrum; obtains the exotic Carroll algebra with noncommuting boosts and noncommutative spatial coordinates when scaling spin with c; demonstrates that Galilean and Carroll limits bifurcate from a shared similarity transformation—Galilean projection removes negative-energy modes, while Carroll retains both relativistic branches.

Conclusion: Carrollian anyons constitute a consistent, rich class of ultra-relativistic quantum particles governed by a first-order square root of the Carroll wave equation; their description necessitates either standard or exotic Carroll symmetry depending on spin scaling, and their emergence reveals a structural duality with Galilean anyons rooted in shared group-theoretic origins but divergent spectral projections.

Abstract: We construct the Carrollian limit of the Cortés--Plyushchay wave equations for planar anyons, grading the spin-tower components by powers of $c$ and taking the limit at fixed rest energy. The result is a linear system describing Carrollian particles of any real spin --- \emph{Carrollian anyons} --- and, at (half-)integer spin, Carrollian bosons, fermions and higher-spin fields.
  The system is a first-order square root of the standard Carroll wave equation $(\partial_t^2+E^2)Φ=0$, whose spectrum consists of two flat branches at plus and minus the rest energy. Scaling the spin along with $c$, in the Carrollian analogue of the Jackiw--Nair limit, yields instead the exotic Carroll algebra, with a second central charge, non-commuting boosts, and observable coordinates spanning a noncommutative plane. We show how the Galilean and Carrollian theories branch from one common similarity transformation: the Galilean limit expels the negative-energy modes, whereas the Carroll limit retains both relativistic branches.

</details>


### [123] [Admissible higher-spin algebras in flat space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30039)
*Dmitry Ponomarev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper classifies admissible higher-spin algebras in 4D Minkowski space that admit massless higher-spin fields as representations, identifying two types—collinear and half-collinear—and explicitly solving the Jacobi identity to obtain two Lie algebra families plus a commutative associative collinear algebra (non-commutative only with added internal degrees of freedom).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify higher-spin algebras compatible with massless higher-spin field representations in four-dimensional Minkowski space, under physically motivated simplifying assumptions.

Method: Imposing admissibility conditions (i.e., existence of massless higher-spin field representation) and structural ansätze (collinear and half-collinear), then solving the Jacobi identity for Lie algebras and analyzing associativity and commutativity properties.

Result: Two classes of admissible higher-spin algebras: collinear (yielding two families of Lie algebras via Jacobi identity) and half-collinear (exemplified by the chiral higher-spin algebra); additionally, an associative, generically commutative collinear algebra is constructed.

Conclusion: Admissible higher-spin algebras in 4D Minkowski space fall into two structural classes; the collinear case admits explicit Lie and associative realizations, while the half-collinear case includes known chiral examples—providing a systematic algebraic framework for higher-spin symmetry in flat space.

Abstract: We study admissible higher-spin algebras in four-dimensional Minkowski space, namely algebras that admit the tower of massless higher-spin fields as a representation. Under several simplifying assumptions, we show that these can be of two types, which we refer to as collinear and half-collinear. The chiral higher-spin algebra provides an example of the latter. For the collinear ansatz considered here, direct solution of the Jacobi identity yields two families of Lie algebras. We also find an associative collinear algebra, which is commutative unless internal degrees of freedom are added.

</details>


### [124] [Generalised Symmetries, Anomalies, and Maximal Branches of 3d Chern-Simons Matter Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30349)
*Fabio Marino,Francesca Pretto,Marcus Sperling*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies generalized symmetries and 't Hooft anomalies in 3D N≥3 Chern-Simons-matter quiver theories from Type IIB branes, determining 1-form and 0-form symmetries, their anomalies, and how 1-form gauging affects Coulomb/Higgs branches via frozen D3-brane sectors and anyon obstruction; it also proposes a magnetic quiver extension with non-simply laced edges to capture symmetry-anomaly data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize generalized global symmetries (0-form and 1-form), their 't Hooft anomalies—including self- and mixed anomalies—and their interplay with maximal moduli space branches in 3D N≥3 linear unitary CS-matter quivers engineered via Type IIB branes.

Method: Combines brane engineering (Type IIB), defect operator analysis (Gukov-Witten defects, fractional/integer monopoles), Wilson-line screening, string lattice techniques, and anomaly matching via symmetry realization on moduli branches; uses magnetic quiver formalism to encode symmetry and anomaly data after 1-form gauging.

Result: 1. Complete classification of 1-form symmetry, its self-anomaly, and maximal anomaly-free subgroup. 2. Faithful 0-form symmetry group and possible 2-group structures determined from integer monopole center charges. 3. Mixed anomalies with non-abelian 0-form factors vanish in linear unitary theories. 4. 1-form gauging obstructs at most two maximal branches due to anyonic line obstruction in resulting CS TQFTs. 5. A non-simply laced magnetic quiver extension reproduces the 1-form symmetry and its mixed anomaly with branch isometry.

Conclusion: Generalized symmetries and anomalies in these 3D SCFTs are fully encoded in brane and defect data; 1-form gauging induces controlled obstructions on moduli branches, and the magnetic quiver can be extended to faithfully capture symmetry-anomaly interplay beyond standard simply-laced constructions.

Abstract: The generalised symmetries, 't Hooft anomalies, and their interplay with maximal branches are analysed for linear unitary three-dimensional $\mathcal{N}\geq 3$ Chern-Simons Matter quiver theories realised in Type IIB brane configurations. The 1-form symmetry, its self-anomaly and its maximal anomaly-free subgroup are determined independently from Wilson-line screening and from the string lattice. Fractional monopole endpoints of the generating Gukov-Witten defect are used to extract the anomaly data, while centre charges of integer monopoles determine the faithful 0-form symmetry group, its connected cover and possible 2-group structures. Mixed anomalies with non-abelian 0-form factors are found to cancel in the linear unitary theories considered. The effect of 1-form gauging on maximal branches is related to frozen D3-brane sectors, which flow to Chern-Simons TQFTs whose anyonic lines obstruct the required defect endpoint. This yields a criterion for affected branches and implies that at most two can be affected simultaneously. In such a case, a magnetic-quiver extension by non-simply laced edges is proposed, wherein the 1-form symmetry and its mixed anomaly with the branch isometry are reproduced.

</details>


### [125] [Brane effective actions and their island rule from $T\overline T$ flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30533)
*Nele Callebaut,Matteo Selle*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a 2d effective action for semi-classical AdS₃ gravity with an end-of-the-world (EOW) brane of arbitrary tension via radial Hamiltonian flow in a derivative expansion; identifies it holographically with a T\bar{T}-like deformed CFT; proposes that Neumann/conformal AdS₃/bCFT duals correspond to freeing such a deformed CFT; verifies the proposal for (A)dS₂-brane saddles; provides three physically meaningful matter–gravity splits—including one coupling a T\bar{T}-deformed CFT to T\bar{T}-like deformed Liouville gravity—and uses the action to compute island-rule entanglement entropy matching Ryu-Takayanagi, thereby extending island-rule validation to non-asymptotic branes and giving Wald entropy meaning to T\bar{T} entanglement; finally connects the effective theory to fluctuating radial cutoff in AdS₃ gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a precise holographic dictionary for semi-classical AdS₃ gravity with dynamical or non-asymptotic end-of-the-world branes—particularly under Neumann or conformal boundary conditions—and to unify the interpretation of T\bar{T} deformation, Liouville gravity, and the island rule within a single effective 2d framework.

Method: Radial Hamiltonian reduction of 3d gravity in AdS₃ with an EOW brane, solved perturbatively in a derivative expansion; interpretation of radial flow as RG flow; identification of induced 2d dynamics with T\bar{T}-like deformations; explicit saddle-point evaluation for (A)dS₂-brane geometries; decomposition of effective action into matter + gravity sectors; island-rule entropy computation using the derived effective action and comparison to Ryu-Takayanagi; on-shell action matching to AdS₃ with fluctuating radial cutoff.

Result: A fully derived 2d effective action for arbitrary-tension EOW branes in AdS₃; holographic identification with a T\bar{T}-like deformed CFT; successful verification for (A)dS₂-brane solutions; three consistent matter–gravity splits—with the T\bar{T}-deformed CFT + T\bar{T}-like Liouville split extending the Weyl anomaly integration into the bulk; exact island-rule entropy matching Ryu-Takayanagi for non-asymptotic branes; Wald entropy interpretation for T\bar{T} entanglement; explicit equivalence between the effective theory (including higher-derivative corrections) and AdS₃ gravity with fluctuating radial cutoff.

Conclusion: The semi-classical bulk dynamics of AdS₃ with an EOW brane is universally encoded in a 2d effective theory that is a T\bar{T}-like deformation of a CFT, providing a unified framework linking boundary condition choices, holographic renormalization, quantum entanglement (via islands), and gravitational effective actions—thereby strengthening the conceptual and computational foundations of AdS₃/bCFT and JT-like models.

Abstract: We derive a $2d$ effective action for semi-classical AdS$_3$ gravity with an EOW brane of arbitrary tension by solving the radial Hamiltonian flow in a derivative expansion. Interpreting the radial flow as an RG flow, the effective theory is identified with a $T\bar T$-like deformed CFT. This leads us to propose that the holographic dual for semi-classical AdS$_3$ gravity with Neumann or conformal boundary conditions is obtained by setting the $T\bar T$-like deformed CFT free. We verify our proposal explicitly for saddles corresponding to $(A)dS_2$ branes in empty AdS$_3$. We discuss three useful ways to decompose the effective brane theory into a matter sector ($T\bar T$-deformed, $T\bar T$-like deformed, and undeformed CFT, respectively) and a corresponding braneworld gravity sector. In particular, the split into $T\bar T$-deformed CFT coupled to $T\bar T$-like deformed Liouville gravity directly extends the Liouville description of the integrated Weyl anomaly into the bulk. The effective action is then used to perform an island-rule calculation for a non-asymptotic brane in an AdS/bCFT set-up, finding exact agreement with the Ryu-Takayanagi result. This extends previous holographic checks on the island rule to non-asymptotic regimes of application, as well as provides a Wald entropy interpretation for $T\bar T$ entanglement. Finally, we relate our effective theory and its higher derivative corrections to AdS$_3$ gravity with a fluctuating radial cutoff by explicit calculation of the on-shell action.

</details>


### [126] [A Truncated Majorana](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30723)
*Ali Vahedi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum field dynamics of truncating a Majorana wave packet using a time-dependent channel shutter, revealing an infrared soft-mode memory (logarithmically divergent) due to fermionic Bogoliubov transformation in the global limit, and exact causal filtering in the number-conserving regime—establishing complementarity between global memory and local causality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
